# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1994

## Introduction

The Vedic revelation affirms that all existence is secure under the protection of the one Absolute Truth. That Supreme is the source of creation and its sustenance. *Yato vā imāni bhūtāni jāyante yena jātāni jīvanti:* ”From it all these created beings are born, and by it their lives go on.” (*Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 3.1)

*nityo nityānāṁ cetanaś cetanānām*  
*eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān*

**“Among the many eternal living beings there is one who provides the needs of all the others.” (*****Kaṭha Upaniṣad*** **5.3)**

In the most confidential Vedic texts like the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* it is further revealed that this universal sustainer is the Personality of Godhead, whom fortunate, intelligent devotees in this age know as Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Viśvambhara, the merciful upholder of creation.

He is not only the initiator of *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* the prime means of satisfying the wants and ambitions of the Lord Himself and of all His dependents; He is also the Supreme Soul watching after every one of His charges from within their hearts and personally supervising the indiscriminate distribution of His compassion. Every conditioned soul has an equal right to receive His mercy, and many great persons who understood this fact have served as *ācāryas* to help in His mission.

Outstanding among these magnanimous servants of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the founder of our International Society for Krishna Consciousness, our beloved *guru* and *parama-guru* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. However it was that we reached this terrible place and time, Śrīla Prabhupāda came here to retrieve us. He searched us out one by one and encouraged us to come back to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He intensely endeavored to communicate the refined, difficult science of Godhead in ways that even the dullest fools can appreciate. He taught us everything, and he never discriminated against anyone. By his personal presence, his instructions and admonitions, he kept his movement and its members alive, and through the programs and disciples he created he continues to do so. We have each had some taste of spiritual life because Śrīla Prabhupāda personally gave it to us. He is the single greatest person of our time, but still he has extended his care infinitely to transform the lives of every one of us individually, and those of anyone else we may bring near his lotus feet.

Once a year, therefore, the devotees of ISKCON come together to celebrate the all-auspicious day of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance in this world. This is a book of offerings for this celebration of Vyāsa-pūjā; by now, something of an old tradition in ISKCON. Confronted with the obligation of submitting offerings, we might feel inadequate to write anything, but it is our duty to do it, to write and read our offerings, worship with *Puṣpāñjali* and *ārati,* and remember what we owe Śrīla Prabhupāda.

With the centennial appearance day of Śrīla Prabhupāda approaching, we should now be especially eager to reunite un der Prabhupāda’s banner of ISKCON. This is a good time to give attention to the theme of universal compassion, the mercy which Lord Caitanya and Śrīla Prabhupāda want to distribute without regard to who deserves it or not. Despite the faults – real and imagined – of its past and current participants, ISKCON is the designated vehicle of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy. It is his body, just as the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust is his heart. “Anyone who is trying to be aloof from this Kṛṣṇa Consciousness Society and yet engage in Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is living in a great hallucination, for this is not possible.” (*Bhāg.* 4.9.11, purport) Yet isn’t it just as much an hallucination to think ISKCON can carry on prosperously while leaving behind the devotees it has sadly failed to encourage – the estranged disciples, the doubting *bhaktas*, the lost generation of *gurukulīs?* It is not an easy job to regain lost trust, but we have to try. Maybe each devotee attending today’s Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations around the world can promise to take some individual initiative in this regard. Even if we can’t help everyone, at least Śrīla Prabhupāda will be very happy with our attempt.

Gopīparāṇadhana dāsa

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa

Superficially, the annual festival known as Vyāsa-pūjā is similar to most other Vaiṣṇava festivals. According to *Pañcarātrika* literature (a branch of Vedic literature, of which Śrīla Vyāsadeva is the recognized compiler) a devotional festival always includes four elements, namely *vrata, pūjā, mahātmya,* and *bhojana.* Devotees observe a vow of austerity, namely fasting for a certain period of time; we offer special formal worship with auspicious paraphernalia to the person being honored on the occasion; we glorify him in appropriate ways by describing his activities and teachings; and we offer him an opulent feast, after which the assembled devotees relish generous quantities of the remnants. More or less all Vaiṣṇava festivals follow this pattern, and the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is no exception.

What is it, then, which makes Vyāsa-pūjā so special – so much so that we even publish a book annually to commemorate the occasion? For whom are all the homages in this collection, and in the Vyāsa-pūjā books of previous and future years?

Literally, Vyāsa-pūjā means the occasion of worshiping Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the compiler of the vast library of profound spiritual wisdom known as Vedic literature. But devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa know this occasion to have a much deeper significance. Indeed, for devotees it is the most important celebration of the year, during which we honor our spiritual master on the day of his appearance in this world as the most proper recipient of our veneration. This is so because he is the one deserving of our deepest gratitude; he is the perfect bearer of the Absolute Truth, the true representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. It is he who, out of truly selfless love, takes the trouble and risk to deliver Vyāsa’s compelling message of surrender and service to the Supreme Lord in a form and language we can understand and imbibe. It is he who acts as the perfect example of how to live by that message.

Even more so for all members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of Śrīla Prabhupāda (fittingly occurring the day after Lord Kṛṣṇa’s appearance day celebration) is of greatest significance. It is he who has so kindly and carefully delivered the message of Vyāsa to all of us intact, marking out clearly the spiritual path of perfection for ours and countless generations of devotees to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in his Vyāsa-pūjā offering to his own spiritual master in 1936 spoke of its internal meaning:

“Herein lies the secret of Śri Vyāsa-pūjā. When we meditate on the transcendental pastimes of the Absolute Godhead, we are proud to feel that we are His eternal servitors, and we become jubilant and dance with joy. All glory to my divine master, for it is he who has out of his unceasing flow of mercy stirred up within us such a movement of eternal existence. Let us bow down at his lotus feet.”

Thus for member and well-wishers of ISKCON the special, or esoteric, meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā is “a celebration of the ‘unceasing flow of mercy’ from our divine master, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Each of the homages recorded in this volume bear witness to the fact that the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda continues to flow freely, vitalizing each of us and, collectively – the whole Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement – and, indeed, the people of the whole world. His unceasing mercy makes us “become jubilant and dance with joy,” a joy of gratitude as well as confidence that his mercy will continue to flow to us and through us, as long as we faithfully follow his exacting and sometimes demanding instructions.

Most types of *pūjā,* or worship, of superior persons or demigods, are inevitably conducted with a material motive, in anticipation of some quick tangible gain. Unlike such worship, Vyāsa-pūjā is conducted without any shortsighted, material motivation. Rather, by humbly and sincerely worshiping Vyāsadeva’s representative in a joyful mood, we reap the benefit of the Lord’s eternal shelter, a treasure incomparable to any worldly compensation. Lord Kṛṣṇa is pleased to see His devotee, the bona fide spiritual master, glorified, and the disciples and followers of the spiritual master get the opportunity to “practice” glorifying Kṛṣṇa by glorifying His pure devotee.

As Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us, we worship him daily in *guru-pūjā* with *ārati,* flowers, obeisances, *kīrtana,* and personal prayers. On the annual occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā our prayers become public, in the form of written of extemporaneously spoken homages. The strong emotions we feel on this occasion bear witness to our conviction that Śrīla Prabhupāda is present, hearing our wholehearted attempts to glorify him. We feel our love for Śrīla Prabhupāda reaching new heights by hearing the thoughtful realizations of so many different devotees. And we feel that Śrīla Prabhupāda is inviting everyone to participate in this happy event, knowing that anyone who hears or reads these homages will be benefited, provided the listener or reader is willing to appreciate the value of honoring a pure devotee of the Lord.

One of the special features of the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is, then, to offer this book to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda. Over the next few days and weeks, as devotees read their offerings, everyone is eager to see the new Vyāsa-pūjā book, with its numerous photographs of Śrīla Prabhupāda and the new, carefully composed words of his disciples and grand disciples. In fact, this book is meant to be a source of inspiration for all devotees throughout the year, and, along with other Vyāsa-pūjā books of previous and future years, a source of inspiration for all our lives.

Although this book is necessarily limited in the number of homages included, anyone can take inspiration from it to write his or her own homage, although it might not be published, and to read it to Śrīla Prabhupāda and share it with other devotees. However limited our realizations might be, if we attempt to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda in some small way, our endeavor will certainly not go in vain, and we will feel that incessant flow of mercy by which we may dance with joy as eternal servitors of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

If you had not appeared before me, I shudder to think  
where in the modes of nature I was destined to sink.

My mind argues, “No, I would have acted piously, I would have enjoyed.”  
But in reality, in the service of lust I’d be employed.

As Lord Rāma allowed monkey soldiers to build His bridge of stone,  
as Lord Śiva engages ghosts and *pretas,* treating them as his own,  
There is no question of my qualification, it is only your great compassion,  
You accepted struggles and austerities in the most merciful Vaiṣṇava fashion.

Some may argue that your style of preaching was appropriate for the “mass,”  
while higher, secret, inner things remained hidden for the “class.”

But for one who has unflinching faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, all the purports of the *Vedas* are revealed.  
Lord Caitanya’s intimate *līlās* are realized on the preaching field.

While offering this humble prayer, may I please seek your blessing;  
my innumerable shortcomings I am herewith confessing.

May dedication to your book distribution become my life and soul;  
to become absorbed in the preaching mission be my only goal.

May I never see your purports as only one of many śāstric choices.  
May I never hear your words as one of many Vedic voices.

May I remain exclusively in your service until I draw my last breath.  
May I finally surrender everything, not waiting until my death.

In the way of elaboration,  
these verses with your pure translation  
glorify you in a far better way  
than anything this conditioned soul could ever say.

“The value of a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord cannot even be compared to the attainment of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which are for those meant for death.” (*Bhāg.* 1.18.13)

“For the conditioned souls, the human body is most difficult to achieve, and it can be lost at any moment. But I think that even those who have achieved human life rarely gain the association of pure devotees, who are dear to the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha.” (*Bhāg.* 11.2.29)

“I think you must be direct associates of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is famous as the enemy of the demon Madhu. Indeed, the pure devotees of Lord Viṣṇu wander throughout the universe not for their personal, selfish interest, but to purify all the conditioned souls.” (*Bhāg.* 11.2.28)

“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord.” (*Bhāg.* 7.5.32)

“My Lord, now I have complete experience concerning the worldly opulence, mystic power, longevity and other material pleasures enjoyed by all living entities, from Lord Brahmā down to the ant. As powerful time, You destroy them all. Therefore because of my experience, I do not wish to possess them. My dear Lord, I request You to place me in touch with Your pure devotees and let me serve him as a sincere servant.” (*Bhāg.* 7.9.24)

*Your insignificant servant*  
Bhadrinārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti Charu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. The more I meditate on the prediction of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu – how Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread all over the world in every town and village – the more I become aware that you are the personality who was specially sent by Him to fulfill that prediction and how it has become a reality through you.

I feel extremely fortunate that somehow or other I have become connected to you. Now that I received this rare fortune, I must take full advantage of it by becoming more and more attached to your lotus feet. Sometimes, some devotees say that you are a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* but I feel that you are even more than that. You are not just an *āveśa* of Kṛṣṇa’s *śakti,* but you are Kṛṣṇa’s *śakti.* You are one of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇi’s very intimate associates.

It does not really matter to me who you are in Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. Because, to me you are the greatest object of my love as my Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I want to remain at your lotus feet eternally. Please bless me that I never leave the shelter of your lotus feet.

Due to Kṛṣṇa’s personal request you came to rescue the fallen souls from this material world and take them back home, back to Godhead. You accomplished that mission perfectly, and before leaving this planet, made a perfect arrangement through your ISKCON to continue that mission.

It is such a difficult task in this age of Kali. This is the age of *adharma*, and Kali is the ruler of this age. With the help of Sin and his army consisting of soldiers like Lust, Greed, and Anger, he has already conquered the entire world. Practically everyone has become his loyal subject. However, you came like a valiant knight and waged a war against him. Alone you stood against the entire world. Who will ever dare to do such a thing? In Dvāpara-yuga Kṛṣṇa did that when He stood up against Kaṁsa, but Balarāma was with Him. You, however, started your war alone, against the formidable army of Kali. You went right into the heart of the enemy camp and challenged your enemy to come out and fight. You are the greatest hero of all time, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Right in front of Kali’s eyes you opened the door of his prison, and not only did you rescue his prisoners but recruited them to form your army to fight against Kali.

When you were here this fight was fun for us. Just as a soldier feels confident under an expert general, or a son when he is holding his father’s hands, we felt absolutely confident. But now that you have entrusted us with the responsibility of leading the battle, although we know that you are there with us and guiding us, because you are invisible to our material eyes, we feel afraid.

In your apparent absence Kali’s onslaughts have become extremely severe. However, so far we have been able to thwart them all, although sometimes there were many casualties. In the beginning we were so unprepared that when attacked we almost lost the battle. However, by your mercy finally we won. When Kali is defeated, he does not give up. Endlessly he keeps launching his attacks and each successive attack becomes stronger than the previous one. But Śrīla Prabhupāda, your army is getting stronger, and your soldiers are becoming more expert.

You gave us the secret of how to become strong. You said, “Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate with each other.” We are realizing how this is actually the means to defeat Kali. You also warned us that no one will be able to destroy this movement from outside, it is only from within that it can be destroyed. Within, means internal conflicts among devotees. That is the most deadly attack of Kali. He tries to cause dissension among devotees. Knowing well that he will not be able to defeat the devotees, he uses the devotees to destroy each other.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, now we are becoming aware that whenever there is a cause of dissension we should immediately see it as an attack of Kali and strengthen our defense by becoming united. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we do not want to underestimate our enemy, however, I want to inform you that so far we have been able to withstand all attacks.

Although each successive attack of the enemy is getting more severe, our unity is consequently increasing, as is our love for you. Therefore, I pray that these attacks become extremely severe so that our love for you can blossom fully through our unity. Then Kali will admit his total defeat and fall down at your lotus feet, just as he did 5,000 years ago in front of Parīkṣit Mahārāja. That will be the best way to glorify you.

*Your humble servant,*  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mukaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam aham vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your pastimes are simply wonderful, simply remarkable and simply inconceivable.

Alone, in an elderly body, wherever you would go You attracted hundreds and thousands of young people. Let us worship, with great love and affection, Your divine lotus feet.

Alone, in a foreign language, far removed from the cultured Sanskrit, you presented Kṛṣṇa consciousness so eloquently and so clearly. Let us glorify, to our hearts full content, Your divine lotus feet.

Alone, in all the foreign lands, where distinctions and divisions abound, You make everyone feel part of the same spiritual family. Let us remember now and forever Your divine lotus feet.

Alone, in an aging body, when one would normally retire and meditate, you traveled fourteen times around the world, preaching the glories of Gaura Hari. Let us bathe again and again, Your divine lotus feet.

Alone, in a *mleccha* country steeped in passion and ignorance, you brought, taught and lived the highest crest-jewel of all cultures. Let us meditate, with awe and great reverence, on your divine lotus feet.

Alone, in a penniless condition, with but 40 rupees to your person, you built up an empire of temples, books and surrendered souls. Let us contemplate, with deep and profound appreciation, Your divine lotus feet.

Without your physical presence, dearly remembered by all of us, still your mission continues to expand as the years slip away. Let us meditate, with love and devotion, on your divine lotus feet.

With simply your instructions, always fresh and to the point, we learn to always be with you, for you and your instructions are one. Let us praise, relentlessly and enthusiastically, your divine lotus feet.

With simply your *bhajanas* and *kīrtanas* – so sweet and so profound – our hearts feel joy without bounds, hearing your transcendental sound. Let us always hold, tightly and securely, your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are always with us in your ISKCON, available to one and all. We simply need to reach out with sincerity and purity of heart. You are always there waiting for us. We need to take but that one step. Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda for having come to save us all.

*Your menial servant,*  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My Dear Respected Vaiṣṇavas,

*Praṇāmas!* 1996 is the year of the 100thappearance of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedānta Svāmī Prabhupāda. It is the unlimited blessing of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa that He has mercifully given us the opportunity to render some insignificant *sevā* in this *utsava.* As a humble attempt to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda, we will stage the *Second World Congress for the Synthesis of Science and Religion*, to be held in Calcutta, January 1996. In 1977, in his last days in Vṛndāvana, Śrīla Prabhupāda left us his divine instructions in regards to the Bhaktivedanta Institute’s preaching work, “Organize conferences in every city of the world. Expose the scientists and scholars of the world to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and Bhāgavata culture. The World will be greatly benefited from such conferences.”

Begging the blessing of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, we wish t o make this the most historic conference of the 20thcentury, both in size and content. The thematic focus of the congress will be that the materialistic paradigm of life and the universe has to be revised and the Bhāgavata paradigm of life and the universe has to be seriously studied. The nature of mind, intelligence, consciousness and life from the perspective of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* will be promulgated among the world’s intellectuals. Once exposed, many sincere scientists and scholars will be interested in studying *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Not less than 5 000 great thinkers, scientists – including more than 20 Nobel Laureates – religious leaders, philosophers and educators will attend the congress. Representatives from each and every country of the world will be invited. Everyone who attends the congress will be able to see who Śrīla Prabhupāda is and what he has give to the world.

Some of the special features of the congress will be:

1\. The doctrine of *Bhāgavata-dharma* or *sanātana-dharma, jīvera svarūpa haya kṛṣṇera nitya dāsa,* will be presented to the elite of the world’s intellectuals. This is the contribution of Bhāgavata culture to the world for peace and cooperation to solve the crises of modern living.

2\. The nature of mind as is explained in the *Bhāgavatam* will be discussed extensively. With this it is hoped that there will be better understanding about the mind-body problem.

3\. The nature of consciousness and *ātma-jñāna* (knowledge of the self) will also be elaborately discussed in the light of the teachings outlined in the *Bhāgavatam.*

4\. The chemical evolution of the origin of life and the universe will be critically examined and the *Bhāgavatam’s* paradigm of life’s origin will be presented.

5\. An extensive book, photo and narrative exhibition of the *saṅkīrtana* pastimes of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda and his scientific and cultural preaching activities will be set up on the conference grounds, thus establishing the fact that Śrīla Prabhupāda is the ambassador of *Bhāgavata* culture for the 20thcentury.

6\. On this most auspicious day of the appearance of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly seek your blessings and support. Your generous support in any capacity will make this vision a reality.

Thank you. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please be so tolerant to accept my not so humble obeisances, but never let me rest until I can offer you myself fully, even though I am valueless and a demanding beggar. There are many like myself who usually think of you as having left this world. We are rather foolish because you are in your books, letters, tapes, *mūrtīs,* and, most importantly, in your devotees. If we abuse, misuse, minimize or deny these, then it is not that you have left, but we, the guilty, have left you.

However, even if we say you have temporarily left – in true love, some temporary absence actually makes the heart grow fonder. In true love, some temporary absence allows one a little time to make better arrangements for the next meeting. Those of us who are reneging on our previous commitments and are not trying to quickly improve our condition for the next encounter or meeting with you, must question our sincerity and love for you as well as our love for the Vaiṣṇava community.

Just a few weeks ago you were so kind to make a formal appearance in your Washington, DC, Capitol Hill Institute preaching center in the form of a new *mūrtī*. You saw that I especially had been so dull; so kindly appeared to lovingly remind us of your daily presence and monitoring nature. Your *mūrtī* came to us from Vṛndāvana, surely this is to literally remind us of your *vrajabasi* nature and our need to develop the pure Vaiṣṇava consciousness. (I am a lowly neophyte, so I beg forgiveness for these next remarks.) As I visit some temples and projects, it seems that your *mūrtī* is crying or being angry. Now I am afraid. Will this new Deity express anger or a crying mood?

My solace is that, nevertheless, the *mūrtī’s* presence is actually a wonderful benediction! In the *mūrtī,* we can experience your looking at us, as your eyes are distinct and piercing. We can chant *japa* with you, bathe you, dress you, garland you, perform *ārati* to you, and offer full obeisance to you. In some *mūrtīs*, you are pointing at us preaching in general or giving heavy chastisement. How wonderful to have these *mūrtīs,* but how unfortunate when they are upset.

I feet that this year it is foolish for me to write things I do not truly mean or understand, as I am an expert actor and procrastinator. After all I have been doing this for several lifetimes. On this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I do not only pray, but also I beg and request you to force me to not think or act as if you have left, nor to treat others in this way. I pray for that day when I can come before your *mūrtī* and I can hear you shout out “YOU ARE A GREAT FOOL; however, my son, I forgive you; but you must never again take shelter of sense gratification – gross or subtle.”

I am convinced that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not just theory, but is the highest reality. I know that you are constantly speaking out to all of your disciples and grand disciples. But those who are dull like me have maintained so much static in the transmission and, therefore, do not hear you. Your love is so unconditional and dynamic, therefore, you never cease to communicate; but still I fail to perceive the clear pure communication. Until I am able to come before your *mūrtī*, hear your order, and never deny your request, I will understand my life to be a failure. How can I call myself a disciple when my *guru* is speaking directly to me and I do not and cannot hear?

My dear eternal father, please forgive me; for I am deaf even though you have supplied me with a hearing aid. You have already given me all that is needed and far more than I deserve. Even if I remain deaf, please do not let me make the *mūrtī* cry.

*Your procrastinating son,*  
Bhakti Tīrtha Swamī Kṛṣṇapāda

## Bhūrijana dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisance. All glories to you. We pray that your powerful preaching potency forever continues.

As I write this, Mother Arcā Vigraha is still alive. By the time this offering is read by the devotees, she will be gone. She now lies bedridden within her Ramaṇ-reti house; her thin, almost white hair is cropped close and with *śikhā.* The May Vṛndāvana heat glares, and her body burns as if filled with poison from one thousand scorpions. Only her left arm near the shoulder is fat, for there the cancer spreads from her lymph gland. The rest of that arm lies thin and useless at her side, like the arm of a doll, but burning with pain.

”Good and bad in the material world is the same thing. Our saying one or the other are our mental creations. All is actually distress, for we must die. Human life is meant for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Why should we lose this chance? A Kṛṣṇa conscious person is not under the laws of material nature.” (All quotes are from a 1969 New Vṛndāvana lecture by Prabhupāda.)

Arcā Vigrahā was a famous South African artist. She took initiation from H.H. Girirāja Svāmī years ago and developed deep faith in you, her Guru Mahārāja, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the holy name, Vṛndāvana Dhāma, and the Vaiṣṇavas. Now, in her final days, her face is gaunt; deep crevices rim her eyes. Yet she is neither miserable nor morose nor fearful. Mother Arcā remains either silently Kṛṣṇa conscious or jolly.

“One who is Kṛṣṇa conscious is never afraid or disturbed in any condition of life. This is the effect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore Nārada is trying to convince Vyāsadeva, ‘You preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Whatever you have so far written is useless. Now you write something that by reading one will become immediately Kṛṣṇa conscious.’ The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is so nice, that by reading, immediately one can capture the Supreme Lord within his heart. Our whole program is – Kṛṣṇa is very kind – always kind upon us.”

She hasn’t eaten any solid food for about seven weeks, and had been subsisting on less than 500 milligrams of juice a day. Her life has been sustained by Kṛṣṇa-*kathā,* which she longs to constantly hear. And Mother Arcā still preaches. Despite her pain, as long as she can speak, she greets all with good spirits, encouragement, and Kṛṣṇa conscious advice. “Most problems don’t seem important from this perspective, do they?’ “The body is useless, isn’t it?” “Jaya Jay Śrī Rādhe-Śyām!” Devotees approach her to offer solace but find shelter in her Kṛṣṇa conscious strength.

”The pure devotees, if they are put into difficulty, they think, ‘this is Kṛṣṇa’s grace. It is Kṛṣṇa’s grace.’ They take it in this way. ‘I was to suffer hundreds of times more due to my bad activities in my past life, but You are only giving me a little of it. So it is Your grace.”

We do not know when Kṛṣṇa will actually take Mother Arcā. She almost left just after Gaura Purṇimā, and we rushed to her bedside at 11 PM to find her gasping and wheezing and unable to breathe. The cancer gradually closes off her windpipe. But Kṛṣṇa wouldn’t take her then. She is now quite ready to go and awaits His call. She chants more or less constantly in her mind.

”Hypnotized. It is so nice, hypnotized! Otherwise, why are these boys working so hard? They can earn money – but they are hypnotized here. Kṛṣṇa is so nice. He is all-attractive. He hypnotizes His devotee. Just like the Pāṇḍavas. They are all Kṛṣṇa’s friends. Nārada met Kṛṣṇa, and since he was a devotee, he could speak to Kṛṣṇa with a little criticism. ‘My dear Kṛṣṇa, by becoming your devotee, Your Pāṇḍavas have become happy? They have lost their kingdom. Their wife has been insulted, and now they are banished for 12 years. Is it a very good thing? But the wonderful thing I see, in spite of all these inconveniences, they have increased their love for You!” So Nārada is asking what kind of hypnotism you do I don’t know. In spite of placed in so many inconveniences, their love for you has increased. That is the position of a *bhakta.*”

Both her breathing and swallowing are now strained and painful. I used to ask her if she had anything remaining to accomplish within the world of matter. Her last anxiety was spiritual, to finish the remaining true-to-size drawings for the 6’ x 3’ panels at the back of your *samādhi mandira*. She wasn’t physically capable, but strained and sat up and directed the remaining work to its end. “See, I told you I’d finish!” No further work remains for Mother Arcā Vigrahā within this world.

”Anyone who has associated with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he cannot forget... He cannot give it up. If one once surrenders sincerely, ‘Kṛṣṇa, I am yours.’ Kṛṣṇa will never leave you. He will protect you.”

Prabhupāda, you have already bestowed your mercy on this fine devotee, Mother Arcā Vigrahā. We, out of affection for her, nevertheless, pray to you for her eternal welfare. We also beg you to continue giving us the association of exalted and exemplary Vaiṣṇavas.

*Praying to serve you eternally*  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All Glories to Your Divine Grace. You once told us that on the absolute platform disappearance and appearance are the same – like sunset and sunrise – both are beautiful. Thus today we share some realizations from your disappearance day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Disappearance Day, Vṛndāvana, 1993, 7.25 PM

Jagadiṣa Mahārāja was singing. *Govindam ādi puruṣam.* I thought: You must be very close to Śrīla Prabhupāda. I want to be with you. Please don’t leave me. Bhurijana Prabhu came and put Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *cādar* on my head. Thought: You helped to introduce me to my spiritual master and brought me closer to him. You taught me what is a real Vaiṣṇava. Wherever you go, I will run after you. Please don’t leave me. The devotees joined in the *kīrtana* to express their love and glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda. I thought: Without you I would have fallen down millions of times. I am completely dependent on you all. Please don’t leave me. I thought of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s letter: “After leaving this material realm, does the devotee remain forever with his spiritual master? The answer is yes. But why do you only want to be in the spiritual world with Siddhasvarūpa? Why not all? Siddhasvarūpa will go, you will go, Śyāmasundara will go, and all others will go. We will have another ISKCON there.” We should all be together in the spiritual world, serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā Śyāmasundara and Their associates.

Then B.B. Govinda Mahārāja told a wonderful story. Akiñcana Kṛṣṇa dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja came to meet Śrīla Prabhupāda in the last days. Govinda Mahārāja was singing soft *kīrtana* while Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami was sitting near Śrīla Prabhupāda on the bed. Bābājī Mahārāja jumped up on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s bed. He said something in Bengali, and Śrīla Prabhupāda laughed in ecstasy. Then Śrīla Prabhupāda stopped. He became very serious. “Hmm. *Tādera caraṇa-sebi-bhakta-sane bās janame janame hoy ei abhilaṣ.*” Then again they began joking and laughing, and Śrīla Prabhupāda again became thoughtful. “Hmm. *Tādera caraṇa-sebi-bhakta-sane bās janame janame hoy ei abhilaṣ.”*

I realized that this was my actual prayer – to serve the lotus feet of the Gosvāmīs and live in the association of devotees. This was my actual desire. And I realized where it came from. It came from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no qualification. My only qualification is that you accepted me. By your mercy, others may accept me too.

*Desiring to be your eternal servants’ servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gour Govinda Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest, revered spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda

Kindly accept my prostrated *daṇḍavat-pranāma* at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

This eternal yet most lowly, unworthy servant of Your great Divine Grace is often reminded of the ever-consolatory, sweet nectarean voice of your lordship that “I shall remain your personal guide.” Yes, O dear Śrīla Gurudeva, you have never left us. You, out of your causeless mercy, eternally remain with us, watch us, correct us, guide us, and lead us on the ever-illuminated path of perfect spiritual realization. And, hence, all we need to do is to just follow the immortal instructions of Your Divine Grace “as it is” with right understanding, without making any personal interpretations of them, so as to receive the powerful unending grace which alone is our real strength on the sharp razor-edged path of pure devotional advancement. Of course, O dear great spiritual master, it is only by the strength of your causeless mercy that somehow we can still hear your ever-pure transcendental voice and transcendental instructions, and see your divine lotus feet wherever and whenever we look, within and without.

Of course, dear Śrīla Gurudeva, as miserably weak as we are, we obviously feel helpless amidst the state of uncertainty, perplexity and anxiety after your apparent physical departure from us, your eternal sheltered servants.

Once again, O dear spiritual master, tremendously weak as I am, I often fall prey to illusion under the influence of *māyā*, as a result of which my feeble mind fails to understand the most sublime message of your Divine Grace. Hence, O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I invoke your grace to enlighten me so that darkness that shrouds my mind can be removed for good. May Your Divine Grace from time to time show me the correct path to be trodden by your beloved servant.

I am now carried away in the turbulent whirlpools of a complex state, full of delusion, anxieties and falsehood. Thus I find no other way to extricate myself from this situation except your kind, causeless mercy, which alone shall help me to realize the Supreme Absolute. That way, wherever I go and whomsoever I meet, I shall more intimately feel your divine presence, see your lotus feet and more deeply hear your ever-nectarean voice in a perfect manner. And, at the end, O my dear eternal spiritual master and savior, please once again shower your powerful, immortal mercy and thus enable this unworthy disciple of yours to join the great voice of the renowned *mahājana* and Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in singing the song of the glory of *gurudeva:*

*Gurudeva:*  
*kṛpa-bindu diyā, koro’ ei dāse*  
*tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna*  
*sakala sahane, bala diyā koro’,*  
*nija māne spṛhā-hīna*

**”Gurudeva, give to this servant one drop of mercy so that I will be lower than a blade of grass and give me all help. Give me strength, so that I will be able t o tolerate everything (that may be very intolerable) and I will not desire any respect from anybody.”**

*jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi,*  
*tomāra karuṇā sāra*  
*karuṇā nā hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā,*  
*prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

**”If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful to me, I can only weep, and I will not be able to maintain my life.”**

*Your eternal yet most lowly unworthy servant*  
Gour Govinda Swami

## Guruprasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet, the abode of pure devotional service. Every year it seems increasingly more difficult to prepare this offering. I wonder if I am becoming more stupid, or if there is some other factor involved. After careful analysis I have surmised that by your mercy, the dense layers of false ego are slowly eroding away, and at least theoretically, I can see my discrepancies. I am even more aware of my lowly nature, although I am unwilling to change it. Every year I become a bit more conscious of your ever-increasing glories, principally by the mercy of your true servants. Thus the gap widens between who I thought I was and my still incomplete but increasing understanding of who you are, the most glorious empowered devotee of Kṛṣṇa. I find that my words and thoughts are incapable of approaching Your Divine Grace.

How can I remedy this situation? How will I be able to take advantage of this wonderful opportunity to glorify your infinite attributes? Only by your causeless mercy, which has always been the active and exclusive principle in my frail attempt to serve Kṛṣṇa. I beg you to enlighten me in spite of my constant reluctance due to false pride. Please don’t kick away this beast that doesn’t even have the loyalty of a dog. I do have faith in your unlimited compassion because it has brought me this far. Your transcendental brilliance is so powerful that it penetrates the encrusted layers of arrogance covering my real self.

I have faith that the overpowering internal potency of Kṛṣṇa, manifested by your supremely magnanimous nature, will continue to drag me out of darkness of self-esteem and conceit. That power of yours to engage such unworthy persons as myself in Kṛṣṇa’s service is proof of your pure devotion; of how you are empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. I pray to Them to give me the strength and intelligence to serve you properly instead of burdening you with my resistance. Until then I have no recourse but to hold on to your lotus feet, even without having the proper qualification.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Guruprasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances eternally thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who have saved me from the ocean of birth and death and who has situated me in eternal service to the Lord. Thank you for everything that you have done for me in the past, are doing for me at present, and will do for me in the future as well. Actually I cannot fully understand the wonderful things that you are doing for me, but I am gradually realizing that everything happening in my life is part of your transcendental arrangement to purify me of my material contaminations and situate me as your eternal servant. Please continue to take care of me in this very nice way, and always depend on me to render some kind of meaningful service to you whenever you want it.

I remember when you had the idea that I should write something about communism and its faults. This was in January of 1976 in Sanand, Gujarat. We were staying in the place of the King of Sanand, and you were using the study of the son of the King as your office during your stay there. One day you had some extra time, so you looked through the library of the Prince and came across a book by Lenin. Later, when it was time for your massage, you asked me to go into your room and find that book, extract the essential points, and then tell them to you so that you could defeat his philosophy which was so adverse to God consciousness.

After reading a few lines of that book, I could realize that it was just intellectual nonsense. But always eager to hear you defeat the fools and rascals, I brought the book to you and started to read some of the points. In a wonderful interlude, you completely tore apart Lenin and his concocted ideas. Then you asked me, in a very subtle way, to preach to the communists through writing and distributing your books there. It was done in such a subtle manner that I didn’t realize at first that you were redirecting my life.

Now, at this time I had never thought of preaching in communist countries, so your statement did not really strike me as something significant. I was your BBT Secretary and cook and there was no thought of leaving your service. But you had a transcendental plan in mind, inspired by Kṛṣṇa Himself, and no one but you knew the depth of your request.

Approximately one moth later, you gave me *sannyāsa* and sent me off to preach in America. On my way to America, I stopped in London for a day. There Haṁsadūta dāsa told me about a preaching opportunity in Budapest, and asked me to go. I immediately agreed and went there and did *kīrtana, prasādam* distribution, and a little undercover preaching. Afterwards I sent you some photos of the program and you wrote back that I should continue to preach in this way and that you were very pleased. I could understand that this was your plan all along and that my real position in ISKCON was preaching in the communist countries. Later, you compared ISKCON to a belt of God consciousness around the world, and said that due to this preaching the belt was now complete. Kṛṣṇa consciousness was spreading throughout the Eastern world across Russia to the Pacific Ocean and you were satisfied.

Whenever I was preaching in the Eastern countries, I always felt very close to you. I felt you near to me at every moment. You wanted this program to go on, and almost without any effort, it started because it was Kṛṣṇa’s desire that the program grow and expand. I can understand that your desire is Kṛṣṇa’s desire. You have no independent desires, for you only want what Kṛṣṇa wants for this world. Therefore you are such a powerful preacher.

I am listening to your lectures more frequently now. I love to hear your voice as it confidently and surely exposes the rascals and fools of this world. The demoniac civilization quivers and shakes before your logic and powerful words. My wish is that the people of this world also get the opportunity to hear your words sometime in their lives. If only they would appreciate the potency of your words, they could transform their lives to those of blissful, spiritual life.

Your books are spoken *kīrtanas,* and your words are echoing throughout all the halls of this world through your books. Everyone knows that the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement sells books. This is their preliminary understanding. Later they will come to know that these books are the most important literatures mankind has ever seen in the recent age. People are lost in bewilderment, but you show them the light through your comments on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā*. Please continue to spread your light throughout the world and throughout the whole universe.

I know that you are now preaching somewhere on some other planet. I very much want to see what you have done there. Someday we might hear of this movement expanding on other planets from the residents themselves. They will describe a powerful preacher, empowered by God, who is turning the demoniac civilization on its head. Then we will know where you are. Some of us will actually go there to assist you in your preaching work after our death. This is the perfection of rebirth.

Please keep me engaged in your eternals service life after life. Never let me forget your lotus feet and let me be connected with you eternally as I wander through this material world due to my misdeeds from innumerable lifetimes past. I want to serve you. I want to work for you. Please keep me busy in your service and bless me to become a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa who can actually accomplish something for your satisfaction.

Thank you.

Please accept me as your eternal servant,  
*Your humble dog,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. This year we approach you with special joy and gratitude in our hearts. You have mercifully blessed us with such nice service in the divine mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The book distribution parties are rolling along, and suddenly an international network of devotee scholars has come forward to join hands in establishing a Bhaktivedanta University, the kind that you wanted. All this is your mercy.

Thank you, Prabhupāda, for making this world tasteless to your servants. Thank you for inspiring and empowering us to chant the Lord’s holy names in ecstasy. Thank you for providing us with all that a living being requires to achieve the highest perfection of life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. May we yet beg you, Prabhupāda, to accelerate the flow of your mercy so that in the very near future we may see your true glories blaze forth in this dark world. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give your mercy now so that your humble servants may fulfill their deepest desire, and see the world worship you as the greatest of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*

*From his hopeful servant,*  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

A thousand obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are living in the hearts of all your devotees, giving them intelligence to work and interact according to your plan. I am no one; a foolish person struggling to do some service for you. Still I want to petition you.

Please give your devotees the courage and intelligence to be straightforward and trusting with themselves and one another. You know very well my thoughts on the matter of the “institution,” and my desires and intentions to see others take up this work so I can be free to work according to my own nature and inspiration. Yet I see that devotees often have a hard time trusting one another and working together. I don’t want to see things complicated because of this inability of the devotees to express themselves honestly and respectfully.

Could it be that within me there is still an inclination to think as controller and therefore it causes me anxiety when things don’t go as I think they should? In that case, if it would be better for me to overcome this inclination, please give me the patience and tolerance to transcend the anxiety caused by the complexities of these interactions.

For the moment I will assume that this is the real point in all of this for me to understand. Therefore, again I ask, please give me the patience and tolerance to transcend the complexities of these interactions. Thank you for your constant affection and help.

*The insignificant servant of your devotees,*  
Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances offered prostrated to your lotus feet. All glories to you on the occasion of your all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

Who can adequately appreciate the depth of Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy in bringing the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu around the world and delivering the conditioned souls who were otherwise hopelessly entangled in the modes of ignorance and passion? Even if we had thousands and millions of tongues, we couldn’t express your greatness and our gratitude sufficiently.

When Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu accepted *sannyāsa* and went to Nīlācala He was concerned about the spiritual welfare of the conditioned souls left behind in Bengal. Already the *brāhmaṇas* and elite of society had gotten the causeless mercy, but Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu was concerned about the others, the less fortunate, more grossly and materially entangled persons. Therefore, Lord Gaurāṅga requested Lord Nityānanda Prabhu to go to Bengal to benedict the remaining souls with love of Kṛṣṇa. Thereafter Lord Nityānanda Prabhu proceeded to flood Bengal with the *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana*!

Similarly, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu desired that all the conditioned souls of the world be delivered. He didn’t personally complete that service, but rather left it for Your Divine Grace to do. Just as Lord Nityānanda left the nectarean association of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to carry out His order, Your Divine Grace physically left Vṛndāvana (while carrying the *dhāma* in your heart) and carried out the instructions of your spiritual master. You were so kind to consider how all the remaining fallen souls in distant places who were deprived of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy would be delivered.

Please have your causeless mercy on us that we may follow in your footsteps and realize the unlimited compassion of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda Prabhu that is coming through Your Divine Grace. The world is burning due to lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness! Only by your causeless mercy can that fire be extinguished.

I am most unworthy to serve Your Divine Grace. Only by your compassion am I able to be engaged in devotional service. Always keep me under the shade of your lotus feet. If it pleases Your Divine Grace, bless us that a suitable working design for the Śrī Māyāpura Candrodaya Mandira may be prepared and realized.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet and to all of your sincere followers.

You wrote perfect Vyāsa-pūjā offerings, which are always published in this book. I feel foolish and helpless to try to write, but I know that you are pleased with our efforts, no matter how clumsy they are. Please help that even if I can never become expert in your service, that I will be able to continue trying to at least become sincere.

As usual, your spiritual potency has manifested itself more than ever in the last year. To avoid making too long of a statement, I will not give detailed reports on the following. It requires many to do that, and they are being published.

1\. All the ISKCON temples which are under construction around the world.

2\. Your books which were published in new languages (Kingdoms conquered).

3\. The “Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlā” books which are being written and published, and all the other efforts which are being made to help us to remember you, and to give the suffering souls of Kali-yuga the chance to know you.

4\. All the preparations being made for celebrating your centennial appearance day.

5\. The millions of your books which were distributed.

6\. ISKCON’s ever-increasing congregation.

7\. The numerous Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā festivals around the world.

8\. Navadvīpa Dhāma Parikramā, done by 1500 ISKCON devotees.

9\. So many people taking to devotional service in ISKCON.

10\. The well received “Śrīla Prabhupāda followers” reunions.

11\. So many other preaching projects.

12\. The books that I have failed to publish and my numerous other shortcomings.

In Singapore, where you were not allowed entry, there is a big electric sign saying GRUNDIG. At least for us, that evokes blissful memories of you. Their advertisement should drag that their product was chosen by His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the Founder-ācārya of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, for the most vital work of translating the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* into English. While we slept in darkness and ignorance, you were absorbed in taking dictation from the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You did this out of your compassion for our suffering. You brought the sunlight into our Kali-yuga lives.

As explained in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.3.43:

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate*  
*dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa*  
*purāṇārko ‘dhunoditaḥ*

**”This** ***Bhāgavata Purāṇa*** **is as brilliant as the sun, and it has arisen just after the department of Lord Kṛṣṇa to His own abode, accompanied by religion, knowledge, etc. Persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the age of Kali shall get light from this** ***Purāṇa.”***

You mercifully gave us your “personal ecstasies,” the Bhaktivedanta purports. I pray that I can get the desire to study your books constantly, and to publish and distribute them. You have the full realization of the urgency of spreading Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, and so many of your followers are carrying on with great enthusiasm. Many have understood your introductory statement to your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* “the spiritual master lives forever, and his follower lives with him.” I pray that by the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas’ association I can also realize this truth and never deviate from devotional service for even a moment. Like all of the Bhaktivedanta purports, this one contains such powerful statements. We know that any intelligent person who reads “even one word” will become enlightened and begin his spiritual life. No one could fail to be moved by statements like these: First I hesitated to include your own writings in a letter to you, but then I remembered how much you enjoyed reading your own books. You said they were “Kṛṣṇa’s books.” I remembered that many evenings you would blissfully sit in your garden in New Dvārakā, reading Kṛṣṇa book with your disciples. Your eyes grew huge and bright when you described how Dvivida Gorila could disturb the “whole ocean,” just like we could stir up the water in your garden’s small pond.

”He descends to reclaim the fallen souls and to reestablish codes of religion which are directly enacted by Him. Except for God, no one can establish the principles of religion. Either He or a suitable person empowered by Him can dictate the codes of religion.” (Who can question that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are such a person?)

”... Real religion means to know God, our relation with Him and our duties in relation with Him and to know ultimately our destination after leaving this material body.” (Your ISKCON gives perfect facility for practical application of these points.) “In the Kali-yuga the population is just a royal edition of the animals...

“And prior to His departure from this material world, He empowered Śrī Vyāsadeva through Nārada to compile the messages of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and thus both the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are like torchbearers for the blind people of this age. In other words, if men in this age of Kali want to see the real light of life, they must take to these two books only, and their aim of life will be fulfilled. *Bhagavad-gṭā* is the preliminary study of *Bhāgavatam*. And *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the *summum bonum* of life, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa personified. We must therefore accept *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* as the direct representation of Lord Kṛṣṇa. One who can see *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* can see also Śrī Kṛṣṇa in person. They are identical.”

This is the basis of your movement. “If you ever get money, PRINT BOOKS.” Your perfectly worshiped the order of your *guru mahārāja.*

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā-deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**”Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23)**

In another place you translated the same verse slightly different, giving us deeper understanding and exhibiting your spontaneity.

”One who has unflinching devotion for the Supreme Lord and is directed by the spiritual master, in whom he has similar unflinching faith, can see the Supreme Personality of Godhead by revelation. One cannot understand Kṛṣṇa by mental speculation. For one who does not take personal training under the guidance of a bona fide spiritual master, it is impossible to even begin to understand Kṛṣṇa.”

Your manifested *līlā* was perfectly exemplary. You were completely spontaneous in your loving devotional service, therefore you could inspire anyone, even the most degraded personalities, to engage in devotional service. You put *bhakti* first. I am a fool, but by some causeless good fortune your sincere disciples have enabled me to contact you. I pray that I will not be an ungrateful *guru-druha* and will be allowed to render some sincere service, however insignificant it may be, within the vast ocean of your International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.

So many of your intimate associates are working hard to write books which describe your personality and activities. We must be especially thankful to them, as hearing about you makes us realize that we are simply neophytes, just struggling with the basics. You are absorbed in the topmost ecstasies of Kṛṣṇa-*premā,* but still you are kind enough to nurse us along.

We find that your causeless mercy of associating so intimately with us gives us so much opportunity to be reminded of you. In Cairo, Egypt I saw one big eucalyptus tree. Somehow or other I was able to remember that you would ask your very dear disciple, Jayānanda Prabhu, to send you eucalyptus sticks for cleaning your teeth. Even just remembering the pure service attitude of Jayānanda Prabhu was a great inspiration. He showed us how to please you. I pray that I can always remember you and your sincere servants, and someday be able to give up the desire for sense gratification and to begin devotional service. When I can really understand you, I will be able to fearlessly preach in Cairo and any place else in the universe. The fact that such a fool as me is still in your movement is evidence of your unlimited and unrestricted mercy. Thank you.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet that evoke sweet memories from the past, a feeling of separation at present and fearlessness for the future.

The more time goes by, the more acute becomes the realization that juvenile passion will not suffice to reach you. A mature state of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is required. Just recently two ladies passed away in the temples here in Italy, they both demonstrated great courage and humility at that crucial time. They were mature devotees, ready to pas the test of death. One in particular, Lālasāmayī devī dāsī, kept an amazingly clear consciousness up to the last moments and was full of gratitude and affection for the assembled devotees, as well as anticipation to reach the goal of life.

I was given a chance to measure the depth of my Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I saw clearly how my being a devotee totally rests on your mercy, how my chance to reach the moment of death with undiverted attention and freedom from the devastating effects of *nāmāparādhas* lies in my following your instructions the same way an obedient animal follows his master’s commands. Real surrender is but a state of utter helplessness and dependence on you.

Unfortunately the formidable enemy known as pride always tries to inflict a treacherous blow to my desire to follow in your footsteps. His cutthroats are profit, adoration and distinction.

How long will I seek vainglory in the name of service to your movement? When will the mind become my real friend and find it impossible to entertain thoughts and aspirations that are not pleasing to you? Will I be able to stop holding back and hand over my destiny to your absolute guidance and sweetest compassion?

I treasure the company of those devotees who have no other aim in life but pleasing you. They are the solution to all problems of life and the sworn enemies of my pride. They are your ISKCON Society, the *Brahma-madhva-gauḍīya* ISKCON *sampradāya.* I love ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the same way I love your lotus feet, and I don’t understand how one could think of living without their shelter even for a moment. As long as there will be cooperation among those who love you, ISKCON will be there.

This lowly servant of your servants comes to you with a straw in his mouth asking: “You are the very self of Lord Nityānanda, please bless me so that I may spend the rest of my life in ISKCON, preach in ISKCON, and leave my body in ISKCON.”

Please similarly bless all those who nourish feelings of affection for you and your servants and who are sacrificing their lives for spreading your movement. If you wish, your blessings can extend up to countless conditioned souls and bring to completion the prophecy of Lord Caitanya and the previous *ācāryas* of a golden age of Kṛṣṇa consciousness within the darkness of Kali-yuga.

Grasping your lotus feet and taking them into my heart, I beg you to accept my most humble obeisances, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your unqualified servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Param-gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite tathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**”Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.38)**

During the time you were present among us, you revealed the potency of the transcendental sound vibration, by which the spirit soul can find his original identity in a loving relationship with the Supreme Lord. You showed us that the true connection with the spiritual master, with previous *ācāryas* and ultimately, with the Supreme Lord, is made by transcendental sound vibration. Take for example the songs sung by you. By hearing them attentively, we can see the spiritual world by your spiritual ecstasies of love of God. They are pure manifestations of the transcendental atmosphere. By your causeless mercy, humanity at large can drink the nectar which flows from your lotus lips when you sing.

In this connection, in one letter which you wrote to Brahmānanda you said: “I never thought about the audience. I was prepared to chant even if there were no men to hear me. The principle of chanting is to glorify the Lord and not to attract a crowd. If Kṛṣṇa hears nicely, He will ask some sincere devotees to gather in such a place.”

Also, you taught the world the perfect attitude in the process of chanting the holy name and how *vāṇī* association is more important than *vapu.* Sometimes we hear some devotees expressing their frustration that they never saw you or served you personally. But you have shown us that true association can be established by sound vibration. We can see, speak and associate constantly with you by this transcendental method. Therefore, saying that you are not present anymore is not at all realistic. One can never minimize the potency and truth of your instructions. Like you always used to stress, the one qualification to realize that you are always present is to hear attentively and submissively.

You gave everything so that people in general could be saved. Leaders throughout the world can learn how to guide people by following the instructions that you have left behind in your books. No aspect of how to develop love of God is missing in your books.

Therefore, I humbly pray at your lotus feet to always remember the mission of distributing your books and to be engaged in following your instructions without deviation and, at the same time, in giving them to suffering human society.

*Your eternal, unqualified disciple,*  
Param-gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

’Twas in Montreal that I first took a look  
Into your *Gītā*, that sacred book,  
And saw the card that caught my mind,  
”Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa – life will be sublime.”

Returning to England, I went to see  
The London temple, and heard you personally  
Lecture about Kṛṣṇa. So I decided to join  
Your ISKCON movement and go on *saṅkīrtana.*

Soon I was sent to Paris in France,  
And went out daily on the streets to chant.  
There I received *hari-nāma* from you,  
As well as the name “dāsa” of “Prabhaviṣṇu”.

Later I joined the first traveling group  
That toured the UK to distribute your books.  
”For your plan,” you wrote, “rely on Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.  
Just keep on moving and spreading His message.”

After a year, on your suggestion,  
We went to Ireland to open a center.  
The crowds were so large when they saw us chant,  
That the streets got blocked and the judge locked us up.

Returning to England, I saw you again.  
To the new purchased Manor by ‘copter you came.  
I recall the first *kīrtana* after which you told,  
”All glories to George Harrison,” and thus blessed his soul.

Once more you came on Ratha-yātrā day,  
Marble Arch to Trafalgar, you danced all the way.  
Amidst the devotees, you shone like the sun,  
As you gazed at Lord Jagannātha in bliss and love.

Soon I was sent to serve at Bury Place,  
A transcendental abode of Your Divine Grace.  
Their beautiful Lordships Rādhā-London-īśvara,  
They say are your favorites, more than any others.

During that summer, the devotees worked hard  
To prepare the Manor temple and grounds.  
And on Janmāṣṭamī day you rewarded them all –  
Śrī Śrī Rādhā Gokulānanda you installed.

In ’75, I was sent to Bombay,  
And you told me at Hare Kṛṣṇa Land to stay.  
”This project’s a headache,” I once heard you say,  
”But we ‘re doing it for Kṛṣṇa, so it’s OK.”

The devotees there had to struggle so long,  
That you gave them special mercy just to keep them strong,  
Through your morning walks, classes and *darśanas* galore,  
They got Rādhā-Rāsavihārī’s blessings for sure.

To travel in India, you gave us the task,  
Full sets of your books in libraries to place.  
You asked us to gather top scholars’ reviews,  
Tell all about Kṛṣṇa, and become *guru.*

Later you sent me to preach in Nepal,  
Where you said, “The Hindu sentiment is strong.”  
”There’s no lack of money in ISKCON,” you said,  
”So I will supply and Kṛṣṇa’s empire you’ll spread.”

The Bangladesh preaching you asked us to start,  
With an Englishman’s courage, a Bengali mother’s heart.  
I have no such qualifications for sure.  
One day by your mercy, I may become pure.

A short while after, Your Divine Grace went away  
To the spiritual world, but still here you stay  
In the form of your books and instructions to all:  
”Stay in ISKCON, and follow, and you’ll never fall.”

And so this Vyāsa-pūjā day,  
To be a servant of your servants, I humbly pray.  
Dear spiritual father, please keep me in place,  
At the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace.

Jay Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet again and again.

During the time of your manifest activities among us, I had the wonderful fortune to be formed into a devotee under your direction, to serve in your mission under your leadership, and now and then to walk with you. For twelve years you served your spiritual master among us in the West, writing your books and forging your instrument, ISKCON. Now sixteen years have gone by in which you have not been manifest to our mortal eye, sixteen years which have belied the adage that time heals all wounds. We remain inconsolable in our loss. We, who loved you then, do not, after a decent period of mourning, move to newer loves and loyalties. Rather our love for you only grows with the passing time.

How have you managed to implant yourself so deeply within our hearts? Even those who have tried hard to remove you in order to return to the life of oblivion find it nearly impossible to do. “Prabhupāda is there to stay,” they report, having brought out their old *japa* beads, still hanging in the back of the closet. (They couldn’t bring themselves to throw them away.)

You have rooted yourself so deeply in our innermost core because you authentically represent God, the indwelling, omniscient well-wishing friend of all. Therefore your abiding in our hearts is natural. We have had fathers, mothers, guides, teachers, and leaders who did not so deeply enter into our psychic constitution because they fell short in their duty to represent God. But you care for us as Kṛṣṇa cares for us. You are His care, personified.

I put those last sentences in the present tense because I can perceive that you are more with us now than you ever were, or rather, I should say that we are more with you. Sixteen years of absence has brought you nearer. We have learned, often through our mistakes, to take shelter of you more and more. As a consequence, your living Bhaktivedanta purports unfold themselves to the eagerly inquiring vision, opening like an eternally blossoming rose. Your disciples, some of whom I knew too well as contentious neophytes (like me), now have become decorated with ornaments of *sādhus,* and through them you still deliver your mercy.

I cannot forget you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have seen to that. Please help me to share your presence with every suffering soul on this globe.

Begging again for a drop of your mercy, the elixir of my life, I am

*Your unworthy servant,*  
*Ravindra Svarūpa dāsa*

## Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

”O lotus-eyed Lord, by concentrating one’s meditation on Your lotus feet, which are the reservoir of all existence, and by accepting those lotus feet as the boat by which to cross t he ocean of nescience, one follows in the footsteps of *mahājanas* (*mahat-kṛtena*). By this simple process, one can cross the ocean of nescience as easily as one steps over the footprint of a calf.”

”O Lord, who resembles the shining sun, You are always ready to fulfill the desire of Your devotee, and therefore You are known as a desire tree (*vāñca-kalpataru*). When *ācāryas* completely take shelter under Your lotus feet in order to cross the fierce ocean of nescience, they leave behind on earth the method by which they cross, and because You are very merciful to Your other devotees, You accept this method to help them.”

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You brought to us the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa and you showed us how to take firm shelter at those lotus feet. By your *saṅkīrtana*, you brought us the perfect process to cross the fearful ocean of Kali. Then you went back to Kṛṣṇa to join His eternal *līlā* again. Fortunately, you left the boat of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet behind so that we and all the future generations of devotees can also take shelter in it and very easily cross the Kali-yuga ocean. You once said that at the end of Kali-yuga none of your disciples and followers will be left because they will all be back home, back to Godhead, where we will hopefully be able to meet you again.

This is my great hope, that I will also be among those fortunate souls who will see you once again, being eligible to be eternally engaged in assisting you in your pure service to your most beloved Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Meanwhile I am trying to follow your instructions, although it is not always easy. But by your mercy, because you are *mahā-patita-pāvana,* the most merciful savior of the fallen souls, it will be possible. Therefore I pray to you: “Please give me more and more strength to follow you strictly and execute your order to preach as you have shown us.”

You are so merciful, you are giving me more and more responsibility in the preaching mission, although I do not deserve it. Therefore I pray to you, O merciful master, to always be able to remember the words emanating from your lotus mouth, and how you gracefully walked amongst us, like a transcendental moon, never touched by the external energy of the Lord. You are always drinking the nectar of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet and distributing it to others. Please let me always taste a drop of this nectar. Then I can go on with your mission forever.

The prayer of Nārada Muni to Lord Kṛṣṇa is very appropriate in this connection:

”Now I have seen Your feet, which grant liberation to Your devotees, which even Lord Brahmā and other great personalities of unfathomable intelligence can only meditate upon with their hearts, and which those who have fallen into the well of material existence resort to for deliverance. Please favor me so that I may constantly think of You as I travel about. Please grant me the power to remember You.” (*Bhāg*. 10.69.18)

Please accept gain and again my *daṇḍavats* to your golden lotus feet and please grant me this boon that they may always shine and spread their cooling rays like the full moon in my and everyone’s heart.

*Your servant,*  
Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

There is an aging man, a very simple and friendly fellow, who has recently been frequenting 26 Second Avenue. He remembers you. He remembers you from many years ago, seeing you in Tompkins Square Park chanting in *kīrtana.* He remembers the small but prominent-sounding drum, keeping the rhythm of *kīrtana,* the *ching-ching-ching* of the *karatalas,* the upraised arms of your dancing disciples experiencing some kind of euphoria that he could not understand. He remembers the deep resonance of your voice as you led *kīrtana* with closed eyes and intense brows bunching towards each other in absorbed concentration. Other details also occupy his memory – the quizzical looks pasted onto the faces of the folded-armed onlookers, standing dubiously at a distance, and so forth.

But most of all he remember you. And that memory is one of the most prominent experiences of his life, even though he is not a practitioner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Remembrance of the pure devotee is as good as remembrance of Kṛṣṇa Himself; and so you have given this man and literally millions like him the opportunity to make his unfortunate life *most* fortunate.

Although it is rare to find pure unalloyed devotion in this world, you uniquely embody *all* the features of pure devotion. When Dhruva Mahārāja was returning to the royal palace after just six-months absence, Queen Suruci showered all affection upon Dhruva – although she had earlier bitterly offended him and, in effect, banished him. Why this change in Suruci? It was only due to Dhruva’s attainment of pure devotion. As Maitreya explained to Vidura, “Unto one who has transcendental qualities due to friendly behavior with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, all living entities offer honor, just as water automatically flows down by nature.” (*Bhāg.* 4.9.47)

By your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the all-auspiciousness of pure devotional service was again and again displayed, even in the darkest of places. You transformed inauspiciousness into auspiciousness, by your pure devotion. Your life was a continuous living example, demonstrating in fact Rūpa Gosvāmī’s teachings of *uttama-bhakti*. Rūpa Gosvāmī instructs us that the performance of *uttama-bhakti* in this world has the features of all-auspiciousness. A pure devotee’s presence is a blessing to the whole world; the whole world becomes happy by the pure devotee’s existence here; the pure devotee possesses all good qualities; and he possesses real happiness. You were all these things, Śrīla Prabhupāda, not only for your disciples but also for the world. Just ask our new friend at 26 Second Avenue! He will testify the same. You wanted to change this forsaken place, a den of barbaric, uncultured rejects from human civilization, into Vaikuṇṭha. To accomplish this, you gave your very self, the best of all gifts.
