Although I am but one tine speck, standing before you as a testimonial of that greatly compassionate gesture, expressing my deepest gratitude for “the *best* of all gifts,” your association and service – and for your fearless “rescue mission,” saving me and others from the cracking and crunching jaws of *māyā’s* devouring visage, how can I properly express my gratitude, my deepening love for you and, my indebtedness for all your transcendental treasures given so liberally – to one so unworthy?

I know the answer. The stock response we have learned to supply when this question is raised is, “The disciple can *never* repay the debt to his spiritual master!”

Then what to *do*? I don’t want to grow old, weary, and die, simply proclaiming your mercy for having made me into Kṛṣṇa’s devotee. You have warned us not to unthinkingly and ungratefully “enjoy” the facilities you have created after your departure.

As you wanted to leave this world like a soldier, fighting with māyā by preaching, like Abhimanyu on the battlefield, fighting even without arms – let me imbibe one drop of that same spirit and pass it on to others. As you moved in this world with swan-like grace amongst crows, let me learn from the best of *paramahaṁsas* how to take on such transcendental character!

O Prabhupāda, you are the best at spreading Lord Caitanya’s mission! As Sanātana Gosvāmī glorified Haridāsa Ṭhākura, I want to honor you:

*avatāra-kārya prabhura – nāma-pracāre*  
*sei nija-kārya prabhu karena tomāra dvāre*

**”The mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, for which He has descended as an incarnation, is to spread the importance of chanting the holy name of the Lord. Now instead of personally doing so, He is spreading it through you.” (*****Cc. Antya*** **4.100)**

As you have causelessly bestowed the best of your blessings by making me your devotee, without my so much as even *asking,* I am now falling at your feet, begging your topmost favor, the gift of pure loving devotion to Kṛṣṇa. Please cleanse me and purify me in all ways, and permit me thereby to qualify as your fit instrument in this greatest of all rescue missions – the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You have taught us the means to attain *prema-bhakti,* and you can bestow if fully, as well. Please shower your special kindness upon this fallen and lowly one, standing before you as your forever-indebted menial servant.

*I remain always at your mercy and in your service,*  
Romapāda Swami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you on the celebration of your appearance day.

While planning a composition for your offering, I was attending to my regular correspondence. One letter stood out among the others. I enclose it here, translated into English, for your pleasure.

Dear Śivarāma Swam,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda. I hope you do not mind my writing to you. I have never written to a spiritual master before. You do not know me. My name is Bhaktin Dora and I live in Pecs (Hungary). I am 14 years old, and I live at home with my mother and older sister.

In 1992 I went to the Hare Kṛṣṇa Festival with a friend. I was not very interested, but I enjoyed the chanting and dancing at the end. After it was over I bought a book, *The Science of Self-Realization.* I do not know why, generally I never read, I think it was because of the chanting.

I took the book home and cannot remember what happened to it. One day my mother found it and was very angry with me. She thought that I was reading this kind of thing. You see our family members are all very strict Catholics. They thought Kṛṣṇa consciousness was some kind of “brainwashing.” Actually I wasn’t reading my book, I had forgotten all about it. Somehow it just “appeared.” Anyway my mother was going to throw it away.

My grandmother who is 68 was in the kitchen at that time. She lives in the apartment upstairs. She came in and took the book. She looked at it and scolded me in a very heavy way. I thought that would be the end of it. I did not mind so much as I was in a lot of *māyā* at the time.

About a week later I overheard a conversation between my mother and grandmother. Granny was saying that this was not some ordinary book. She said that what Prabhupāda was saying is what Jesus Christ said and that Kṛṣṇa is God. I was very surprised. She said that we should listen to what Prabhupāda said and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa because that was the religion for this age. There was a lot talk about how Christianity was no more, and no one was following the Bible, but what Prabhupāda said was pure and perfect.

Things really took a turn from there. One day my grandmother visited the *nāma-haṭṭa* center here and began to chant on beads. She also began to buy Prabhupāda’s books one by one. She was spending all her pension on buying what she called t he “beautiful, holy *Bhāgavatam.”* Sometimes she could only afford to eat potatoes, but she kept buying the books. The devotees even came to her flat and helped her set up an altar. When I went upstairs they had taken all the pictures down, and there were Kṛṣṇa pictures everywhere.

That was really the beginning. One night granny had a dream about Prabhupāda. Something really happened to her then. I don’t know what it was, but she began to get very enthusiastic. Next she began to get the whole family involved. I mean, not just me and my mother and sister, but her two sons, their wives and six children as well as her brothers, sisters and relatives. Before she used to carry a *Bible* with her and quote Jesus Christ. Now she has a *Bhagavad-gītā* and quotes “the good Lord Prabhupāda.” She became a veritable transcendental terror. Everyone in the family has to chant at least one round a day. In addition granny made everyone become a vegetarian including my dog Sikra, and we offer our food to a picture of Prabhupāda and Lord Caitanya.

Now I am also getting out of *māyā* and chanting and reading a little also. Where I go to school my friends inquire about Kṛṣṇa, as they know I am a devotee. The whole family goes to the *nāma-haṭṭa,* all sixteen of us. During the Christmas Marathon we all tried to distribute Prabhupāda’s books. Even granny would take books with her to the market and sell them to the vendors. Everyone is afraid of her because she is fearless. They all think she has gone crazy, but she does not care.

Now she is saving to go to Budapest to see the newly installed Deities. She has heard that Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda “came” to Hungary and are being worshiped there by the devotees. She says she wants to see God just once in this life.

At this year’s Hare Kṛṣṇa festival, you were speaking to the guests after the *kīrtana.* You must remember my grandmother because she came and sat right beside you and asked so many questions. At the end when you stood to leave, she even kissed your hand, remember? I also wanted to ask a question, but I was shy. Could I please ask you now? I hope you do not mind, *mahārāja*.

I want to know what kind of man Śrīla Prabhupāda was. He must be so dear to Kṛṣṇa to have spread this message all over the world. What are these books that changed my family so much? How is it possible that he can speak so powerfully through them? You must feel very fortunate to be his disciple. How great a man he is. Sometimes when my granny chants in front of a picture of Kṛṣṇa she cries. How does Prabhupāda do that? I want to cry like that too. Granny dreams of Prabhupāda, and sometimes she talks to his picture. Although it says on the cover of the book that he passed away, is Prabhupāda really dead, or is he still alive? Do you think I can meet him some day?

I am sorry that I have gone on so. I would like to be a good devotee one day and help you and Prabhupāda spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please could you answer my questions?

Your servant,  
Bhaktin Dora

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what is this brand of mercy that you gave this old lady I just barely met, that you never gave to me? She never met you, never saw devotees, she is not even initiated by you. What is this kindness that you bestow upon her, that you do not give me, “your fortunate disciple?”

What am I referring to? It is just this. After having come in contact with you for just a few months, what inspiration did you give this granny in that dream? What did you move in her heart that made her change her life in its final days? That made her turn against the current of banality and tradition and strike out alone to change her world. No sympathy, no association, no institutional support. Boldness I do not possess, changes I do not have the strength to make.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to know what is that you say to her from your picture when she talks to you? I have so many pictures. You do not speak to me through them.

Although I worship Deities daily, I continue to see Them as made of marble and wood. How is it this old lady has the conviction that God has “come” to her country? Why have you not given such vision to me? Where did she get the conviction that a pilgrimage to the city capital would yield the final goal of her life?

One last thing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how is it that when Dora’s granny chants in front of a picture she cries? How do you do it? I want to cry like that too. When will you give me that mercy?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is one letter, from one girl who came in contact with you. How many millions of such souls are there who have yet to write, who are directly experiencing your mercy daily, who read your books with implicit faith, whom you talk to in dreams and pictures, whose lives you change abruptly and reward with tears when chanting the holy names?

How many people cross the boundaries of rules and regulations by the strong boat of your mercy and practice and taste Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a realm beyond logic and argument? I think these people are meeting you every day. How will I acquire their good fortune? When will you one day bestow some of this special mercy upon me that you give to them?

If I am not to acquire it directly, even after begging for it, then I will serve such souls who have reached your mission. I will offer them *prasādam,* give them your books and show them how to practice. I will chant with them. Thus I can hope to gain a new perspective of your greatness, even though I may never fully understand it.

Your insignificant servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

*yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas*  
*tat tad evataro janaḥ*  
*sa yat pramāṇaṁ kurute*  
*lokas tad anuvartante*

***”Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues.”*** **(*****Bg*****. 3.21)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were the greatest leader of men. Your example inspires all the world. Each incident in your blessed life was special.

You were born in the wealthy Mallik community of Calcutta and your father, Gour Mohan De, was a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. He prepared you to become “a nice devotee of Rādhārāṇī.” No karate lessons or TV for you. It was *mṛdaṅga,* harmonium and your own Ratha-yātrā. I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I may be born in a family of devotees in my next life.

At Scottish Churches College Professor Urguhart tried to debunk the philosophy of *karma* by questioning who was the witness to our past activities. Many Indians were influenced by such materialistic propaganda, but you easily saw through it. I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I may develop even an ounce of your uncompromising faith in the teachings of Vedic *śāstra*.

As a young man, you were inclined to marry again, as was Hindu custom, but out of deep respect for your father’s judgment, you surrendered to his advice:

”My dear boy, I understand that you are trying to get yourself married again, but I would advise you not to do this. It is Kṛṣṇa’s Grace that your present wife is not just according to your liking. This will help you not to become attached with wife and home, and this will help you in the matter of your future advancement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I may faithfully follow my authorities in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, even as a householder businessman, you scrutinizingly studied the *Bhagavad-gītā* and took time out from your work to assist the Gauḍīya Maṭha *sannyāsīs* in their preaching. Yours was a lifetime in preparation for your great mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness worldwide. I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I may develop the proper study habits and become His instrument as a preacher.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, fifty years ago you single-handedly started *Back to Godhead* magazine and struggled to complete each issue. Thirty years ago you struggled to find a sponsor to send you to America. In New York you struggled for the first year to start the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. When a disciple asked you: “What makes you happy?” You replied: “I am happy when I see you struggling.” I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I may see my struggle as a sacrifice to help build the preaching mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always completely organized. You were the best businessman, the best leader and the best time manager. You established a worldwide mission of temples, book publication and distribution, farms, schools and initiated thousands of disciples. You were the best because you desired to be an excellent instrument in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s hands. I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of your appearance that I may be able to perform the most difficult of all austerities: to become disciplined (a disciple) and become well organized and competent to effectively preach your glories all over the world.

Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the shade of your divine lotus feet. My heart is parched and dry, devoid of genuine devotional sentiments. Still I shall try to serve Your Divine Grace on this auspicious day of your appearance by offering this small poem.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Garden of Pure Forms**

Socrates, it is said, thought of the Spiritual Power  
As the source of all forms, even the common garden flower,  
Which in this world is false, but in that world a fact –  
But he didn’t say how we’d get our spiritual forms back.

In the *Prīti-sandarbha*, Gosvāmī Jīva informs,  
Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence is full of unlimited forms,  
Each one very beautiful, the Lord doest bestow  
Upon souls He releases from *māyā’s* false show.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy, so amazing to me,  
Through your pure teachings, these spiritual forms we can see.  
Implanting our souls in your garden of service,  
You cultivate pure forms, which Lord Kṛṣṇa will harvest.

Your garden is ISKCON, and for ten thousand years  
Countless souls will bloom there, free of lust, anger, fear,  
To be offered to Kṛṣṇa, their pure love He’ll enjoy,  
*Mañjarīs* (means “blossoms”) for the Divine Cowherd Boy.

Let’s assist Śrīla Prabhupāda in his garden of *bhakti,*  
There’s so many soul-seeds, and Kali-yuga’s so rocky.  
Help me take out these stones, Prabhu sow these seeds over here.  
Pour the Prabhupāda-*vāṇī* nectar right into their ears.

O Prabhupāda, please help me, I’m feeling so weak.  
This work is so hard, I’m in need of a break!  
”In spiritual things there is no exhaustion,” Prabhupāda said,  
If I think this work’s material, I’ve got rocks in my head.

Yes, while I tend Prabhupāda’s garden, I have to be sure  
That I tend my own heart, lest I again become impure.  
Weeds and rocks all around me, within and without,  
If I stumble, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me with a shout!

While toiling honestly, day in and day out,  
Serving in Prabhupāda’s garden, we must without doubt  
Always chant Hare Kṛṣṇa in great ecstasy.  
We’ll all go back to Godhead, Prabhu, just wait and see!

And there in Goloka, Prabhupāda leading his group,  
Of ISCKON devotees in their original *svarūpa*,  
The Form of all forms will bless all with His view,  
Śyāmasundara Śrī Kṛṣṇa, of most beautiful hue.

Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

**The best of Lord Hari’s Servants**

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave many orders meant for other’s benefit, but one in particular seemed more a personal request. Only a few days before he disappeared, he expressed a strong desire to be taken to Govardhana. His eagerness transcended what appeared to us impossible circumstances. Considering his fragile health, we felt helpless to fulfill what proved to be nearly his last request. Only after his departure, as a type of consolation, did we carry Prabhupāda in his *mūrtī* from around Govardhana. Still, I did not feel we had fully satisfied his desire. As the years passed, whenever I would visit Vraja, I would spend time at Govardhana. By understanding the significance of Govardhana, I began to realize why Śrīla Prabhupāda had at last so fervently wished to be taken there, and I lamented that I was unable to satisfy his last desire.

In 1986 we constructed our Houston, Texas temple with three altars: on the left we established Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, on the central altar, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and on the right – well, we weren’t quite sure which Deity to install. In fact for five years the third altar remained vacant. Then, on a yearly pilgrimage to Govardhana, it dawned on me that we should establish Śrī Girirāja Govardhana Himself as the third presiding Deity. Taking permission from a respected *vrajavāsī,* we carried a very large *śilā* away and brought Him to the far-off land of the cowboys. Now He sits enthroned, smiling munificently. Lord Caitanya and Prabhu Nityānanda embrace Him and bathe Him with Their ecstatic tears. Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa perform Their wonderful dancing on His surface. And Śrīla Prabhupāda, form his *vyāsāsana,* can finally see these pastimes he so much longed to behold.

Yet I still didn’t feel that Prabhupada’s desire was entirely satisfied. Whenever I would visit Vraja and go to Govardhana, I would hear a voice inside saying? “Prabhupāda wants to come here.” His own Guru Mahārāja, as well as Śrīla Gaura Kiśora dāsa Bābājī, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and all the previous *ācāryas* to the time of t he six Gosvāmīs – all had resided at Govardhana. Following the words of the *Bhāgavatam* (10.21.18), *hantayam adrir abala hari-dāsa-varyaḥ,* they glorified Śrī Govardhana as *hari-dāsa-varya*, the best of Lord Hari’s servants. Because the Lord’s mercy follows the mercy of His devotee, the *ācāryas* worship Śrī Govardhana not only as Kṛṣṇa Himself, but also as the best of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. They pray to Him as *hari-dāsa-varya,* begging for His blessing to be able to serve Kṛṣṇa. And Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted all his followers to know the value of Śrī Govardhana’s association by showing how much he himself desired that association.

With this thought in mind, we contemplated getting a place at Govardhana. One location that caught our attention was the King of Chaturpur’s palatial building directly on the *parikramā-marga,* a few minutes from Govardhana town. To make a long story short, after more than three years’ efforts, this wonderful property is now officially ISKCON’s. The building is nearly one hundred years old, but once it is renovated, it will be a fit place for Śrīla Prabhupāda to reside. The former kind was a devotee of Śrī Girirāja. He constructed the building as a perfect square, each side one hundred and eight feet in length. The easily accessible roof verandas provide an unobstructed and unequalled view of Śrī Govardhana. I sat there one day, contemplating as follows.

The SB glorifies three personalities as *hari-dāsa*. One is Śrī Uddhava who is glorified as: *kṛṣṇaṁ saṁsmarayan reme hari-dāso vrajaukasam* (10.47.56). Śrī Uddhava is praised for bringing relief to the inhabitants of Vṛndāvana, \[who were suffering from\] Kṛṣṇa’s separation, by reminding them of their dearmost Kṛṣṇa. Yudhiṣṭhīra Mahārāja, the eldest Pāṇḍava, is also referred to as *hari-dāsa: hari-dāsasya rājarṣe rājasūya-mahodayam* (10.75.27). All the saintly guests at Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhīra’s *rājasūya-yajña* glorified the king’s love for Kṛṣṇa which was so deep that the Lord felt bound to live as a servant, friend and master in the Pāṇḍava’s home. Thus the *Bhāgavatam* distinguishes these two exalted personalities as *hari-dāsa* due to their special relationship with Kṛṣṇa. But just imagine the position of Śrī Govardhana: by describing Him as *hari-dāsa-varya*, the *best* of Hari’s servants, Śrī Govardhana’s position is acknowledged to be even beyond that of Śrī Uddhava and Śrī Yudhiṣṭhīra.

As I sat before Śrī Girirāja, I was surprised at how much his protective, merciful presence reminded me of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s association. Reflecting on Śrī Govardhana’s superlative qualities, each seemed equally applicable to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

To begin with, Lord Kṛṣṇa lifts Govardhana to protect the residents of Vṛndavāna. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda has erected ISKCON to shelter the world’s devotees. When the residents of Vṛndāvana worshiped Śrī Govardhana under Lord Kṛṣṇa’s direction, Kṛṣṇa revealed to them that He and Govardhana are non-different. In much the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda established ISKCON and then declared ISKCON to be his body, thus encouraging his disciples to become ISKCON’s unconditional servants. As Lord Kṛṣṇa wanted everyone to understand that Śrī Govardhana can fulfill all their spiritual desires, Śrīla Prabhupāda has this same conviction with regard to ISKCON.

As Śrī Govardhana tolerated the lightning bolts hurled by Lord Indra, Śrīla Prabhupāda tolerated unlimited attacks by the impersonalists, voidists and atheistic demons. Thrilled by the touch of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s little finger, Śrī Govardhana hardly noticed that bolts of lightning were raining upon Him. Similarly, the constant touch of Lord Gaurāṅga’s message enhanced Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching ecstasy, despite all demoniac attacks.

Śrī Girirāja adjusts Himself to be of service to Kṛṣṇa at all times. In the rainy season He becomes dry, warm in the winter, and cool in the summer. Thus He enables Kṛṣṇa to enjoy His pastimes without cessation. Doesn’t Śrīla Prabhupāda do the same? He expertly adjusts all instructions to make every occasion ideal for serving Kṛṣṇa. In fact, Prabhupāda has fashioned his ISKCON body into a dancing stage for Lord Gaurāṅga to perform His brilliant pastimes. By performing His *saṅkīrtana* pastimes directly on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental body. Lord Caitanya has allowed this dearest of his devotees to observe His confidential moods. He has kept nothing hidden from Śrīla Prabhupāda. How similarly Lord Hari has blessed Śrī Girirāja: from late morning to early evening the Lord has utilized Śrī Govardhana’s body for each and every one of His pastimes. He has used the flat places for sitting, dancing and eating, and the caves for His secret rendezvous. In the same way Prabhupāda’s temples provide great halls for public *kīrtanas*, discourses, and taking *prasādam*, while his personal quarters are accessible especially to those who have given their lives to him.

Mountains are hard by nature. Yet upon hearing the sweet sound of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s flute, Śrī Girirāja softens and His stones begin to melt. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda is impervious in māyā’s presence; but in the association of his devotees, he immediately becomes soft and his heart melts. When he hears the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, tears stream from his eyes like the rivers of Śrī Govardhana which collect in Mānasī Gaṅgā and Kusuma-sarovara as a result of Lord Hari’s touch. Sometimes Prabhupāda’s hairs stand on end, and his body shivers as he is overwhelmed by *saṅkīrtana-rasa*. These same symptoms are observed in Śrī Govardhana: Kṛṣṇa’s touch causes Girirāja’s grasses and trees to stand erect, and the breeze made fragrant by the Lord’s bodily aroma causes the trees and grass to shiver with ecstasy. As Prabhupāda nourishes his followers with the fruits of love of God, Girirāja’s grass nourishes the cows, while the desire trees on His slopes offer their flowers and fruits for the Lord’s satisfaction.

The birds that fly over Śrī Girirāja’s landscape are of many varieties. Some alight on the treetops; and, like majestic sages, stare, unmoving, silently observing Lord Hari’s pastimes. Others, like the cuckoo, sing melodiously for the Lord’s delight while Kṛṣṇa’s favorite, the peacocks, dance madly about to the accompaniment of Hari’s flute. Similarly, Prabhupāda’s preaching produces a variety of effects. Non-devotees become silenced by his invincible arguments, devotees praise him with sweet glorifications; while the *saṅkīrtana* men and women dance madly to the beat of his *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga*.

The *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga* produces Prabhupāda’s books and is the main instrument in the *yuga-dharma* of *saṅkīrtana*. This *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga* is like the *durvā* grass which grows on Śrī Govardhana’s hill and which is used for making religious offerings of *arghya*. Prabhupāda’s books like Śrī Govardhana’s medical herbs are like soothing balm for the afflicted hearts of the conditioned souls. They are a life-giving elixir which slakes the thirst and bathes the mind like Śrī Girirāja’s fragrant, cool waterfalls. These books are the rarest jewels and the most valuable possessions. Like the wealthiest of persons, Śrīla Prabhupāda has distributed them unlimitedly without consideration of the recipients worthiness and has thus made the whole population of the world most fortunate. In the same way, Śrī Girirāja’s jewels and minerals have made the *vrajavāsī’s* opulence unequalled even in Vaikuṇṭha. Śrī Girirāja’s smooth stones and glistening waters are lamps and mirrors reflecting the *vrajavāsīs* beautiful countenances, just as Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are shining lamps and mirrors for their readers to see their true consciousness \[selves\] reflected.

I took a large Govardhana *śilā* to Houston, but I left no gold in exchange. Knowing of the local custom, I wondered how I had gotten away with such grand larceny. But now I can hear Śrī Govardhana laughing: To restore this king’s palace will cost you as much gold as the weight of the *śilā* you stole. And what to speak of what I took, and continue to take from Śrīla Prabhupāda. He too is laughing: You’ll be paying for lifetimes, he laughs, and into eternity. O Girirāja Govardhana! O Śrīla Prabhupāda! O best of Lord Hari’s servants! No one can be more liberal and merciful than you. Those who doubt must never have met you two most munificent personalities. Śrī Girirāja Govardhana *kī jaya!* Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!* Śrīla Prabhupāda Vyāsa-pūjā *kī jaya!*

Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Reflections on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Poem Mārkine Bhāgavat-Dharma**

(Delivered as a lecture in Los Angeles on November 16, 1993, commemorating Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disappearance day)

*(1) boro-kṛpā koile kṛṣṇa adhamer prati*  
*ki lāgiyānile hethā koro ebe gati*

*(2) āche kichu kārja taba ei anumāne*  
*nahe keno āniben ei ugra-sthāne*

**”My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever You like with me.**

**”But I guess You have some business here, otherwise why would You bring me to this terrible place?” (*****Mārkine Bhāgavat-dharma*****)**

This poem by Śrīla Prabhupāda, which we have sung today, very nicely speak of his qualities. On such a day as today when we are honoring his transcendental disappearance, we want to concentrate more on thinking about Śrīla Prabhupāda and his qualities.

Our movement is so centered on Śrīla Prabhupāda that there is not a moment that we do not think of him. Nonetheless, on such occasions as today, and on his Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, when we celebrate his divine appearance, we especially focus on his qualities. This poem speaks very much of his qualities, along with our disqualifications.

*(3) rājas tamo guṇe era sabāi ācchanna*  
*vāsudeb-kathā ruci nahe se prasanna*

**He explains, ”Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it.”**

Those are our disqualifications. It is so nicely expressed by this poem, and it is so true. We are actually under the control of the illusory energy. As Prabhupāda further says here,

*(6) tomāra icchāya saba hoy māyā-baśa*  
*tomāra icchaya nāśa māyār paraśa*

**”All living entities have come under the control of the illusory energy by Your will, and therefore, if You like, by Your will, they can also be released from the clutches of illusion.”**

There is no doubt that this is a description of ourselves, that most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. No matter how much we may think otherwise, this is the real fact in this world, as Prabhupāda would say, “our real position.”Prabhupāda also gives the real solution. The next texts read,

*(4) tabe jadi taba kṛpā hoy ahaituki*  
*sakal-i sambhava hoy tumi se kautukī*

*(5) ki bhāve bujhāle tārā bujhe sei rasa*  
*eta kṛpā koro prabhu kori nija-baśa*

**”But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible because You are the most expert mystic. How will they understand the mellows of devotional service? O Lord, I am simply praying for Your mercy so that I will be able to convince them about Your message.”**

Prabhupāda’s qualities here shine forth. And the force that brought him to us is also herein described. There was something within his mind, something within his heart, that brought him.

We constantly analyze his coming to the West, the different material aspects of it, if there even is such a thing as something material in relationship to Śrīla Prabhupāda. For instance, today we listened to recent interviews with Sumati Morarji and a Dr. Patel. Many people had some intimate contact with Śrīla Prabhupāda under different circumstances and were forever touched by him. It can be seen in their emotions when they now speak of him. Sumati Morarji was especially instrumental in Prabhupāda’s coming to the West. As we listened to the interview, we tried to picture these times so many years ago in which Prabhupāda was in the final moments of his preparations for coming to the West. Of course, we say it was a “lifetime in preparation.” During his whole life he was preparing for this grand and tremendous mission.

Today we heard for the first time that he met Sumati Morarji in such a special way at Kurukṣetra. She mentioned how she would generally not go to Kurukṣetra, but had somehow or other gone there and met Śrīla Prabhupāda. On meeting him she felt an inclination to render some service. She asked Śrīla Prabhupāda if he wanted anything to eat, and offered him some coconut water. She was insisting that Prabhupāda takes something. Seeing him as a *sannyāsī,* she felt this desire to offer him some service. She goes on to describe that later they met in Bombay and it was very nice. They had exchanged cards at the Kurukṣetra meeting and she had told him she would like to help him in some way. Not long after he was to make true of her offer.

Listening to these things we can see that there was always a constant force moving Śrīla Prabhupāda to come to the West. There was “something” within Prabhupāda always pushing him. It is our great fortune that this “something” was there, and in this poem it is described by Prabhupāda himself – which reminds us of another prominent characteristic of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his movement.

Prabhupāda had to describe everything to us. He even had to write his own *praṇāma-mantra.* He had to write his own glorification for us. He had to tell us how to honor him. At the same time, it is such a humble prayer, “Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī...” This is how he wrote about himself. He is not writing, you are so great, you are this, you are that. He is writing a particular *praṇāma-mantra* describing himself as a servant.

*Namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe, nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe.* This is what was written by Śrīla Prabhupāda himself. Just see what it says, “You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.” In the early days it would have been impossible for us to conclude this on our own, what to speak of putting it in Sanskrit!

I remember my first exchange with one of the senior devotees of the movement. I was surprised by the statement he made about his first impressions of being in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. What was noteworthy to him was that not only did the devotees have all the answers, they even told him what the questions were; what one should ask. This was the way of Śrīla Prabhupāda. He not only said what the solutions are, but what the problems are as well. This poem is a choice example. It is also very important to determine what the problem is, what the question is.

Actually there were two poems that were found by the devotees in Prabhupāda’s diary. One is the “Prayer to the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa,” and the other is the one we have sung today, *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma*. They were both written on board the ship Jaladuta, with just a few days difference – one on September 13th, 1965, and the one we sang today on September 18th. These two poems speak very much about Śrīla Prabhupāda’s personality and his qualities.

On this occasion let us pray to receive even a small fraction from Śrīla Prabhupāda of that force he had with him that brought him to us. May we inherit even a very small particle of it, a *kaṇikā*, or a ray. Any kind of reflection of a fraction of a particle of that impetus that brought Śrīla Prabhupāda to us would make us very successful as followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We have a duty towards him. Just as he has always done everything, told us everything – due to our ignorance – giving not only the answers but also the questions, just as he even told us how to honor him, in the same way we are going to require his own strength to be able to satisfy him, to fulfill his mission and our duty to him.

We have a role to play in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission, yet we are just insignificant parts in that mission. We need to receive his special blessing to be able to fulfill our role.

By reading this poem we can get a glimpse of how eager he was to help us. This eagerness is also uncommon. Why? Because generally we are not concerned for others. We are concerned with ourselves. This is the tendency in this world. The most dear thing to think about is yourself, and it is a very unpalatable inconvenience to think of others. It is a botheration, as Prabhupāda would say. We think, “Why should I take on other’s people problems? Enough with my own problems!”

So it requires a very particular mood, something very special within oneself. Yesterday we were speaking about the verse*: na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīṁ kavitāṁ vā jagad-īśa kāmaye; mama janmani janmanīśvare bhavatad bhaktir ahaituki tvayi*. Lord Caitanya was speaking about this renunciation, this rejection of so many attractive things. In particular we were analyzing na *janam*, “I don’t want any followers,” and the aspect of this *janam* is the tendency to feel that one is being cared for. That tendency is the normal thing. We are very eager to be cared for, but we are not so eager to care for others. This eagerness is rare. As rare as the *mahātmā* who exhibits it – *sa mahātmā sudurlabhaḥ.*

The other day we were studying the text *manuṣyānāṁ sahasreṣu* from *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.3), wherein Kṛṣṇa says,

”Out of many thousands among men, one may endeavor for perfection, and of those who have achieved perfection, hardly one knows Me in truth.”

Out of thousands it is difficult to find someone who is interested. Prabhupāda is explaining very nicely in his poem how the population is immersed in the mode of ignorance and passion, and how someone immersed in those modes is not looking for anything transcendental. They are very mundane, very materialistic. But then, even when you find someone who is interested in transcendence, he becomes interested in *mukti,* liberation.

We were commenting on this as we listened to a tape of Śrīla Prabhupāda lecturing on the *Nectar of Devotion*. He spoke on the section of the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* where Rūpa Gosvāmī quotes Lord Kapiladeva telling His mother how His devotees are not even interested in liberation. Just imagine, when you finally find one person who is different from the rest, and who is not so materialistic and wants transcendence, it so happens that he is just interested in impersonal realization. He wants to give up his individuality, lose his personality, and thinks that Kṛṣṇa has no personality of His own, that there is no personal God. It is a type of atheism because they believe in only the energy of God. In the ultimate issue, for them God is a thing – an energy.

That is the meaning of *manuṣyāṇām sahasreṣu,* to find a devotee even among transcendentalists is difficult. You have to get a whole crew of those interested in transcendence. Then you may find one devotee of Kṛṣṇa among the big group.

In the same manner it is very difficult to find people who care for others. And when you finally find someone who cares for others, he doesn’t know what to care for. Then you have to search among the whole group who want to do good to find one who knows what is the real good for others. Śrīla Prabhupāda had to teach us what is the real welfare work. If you actually want to do some good for others, find out what is that good. That has been taught by Śrīla Prabhupāda. As a duty to Śrīla Prabhupāda, because he cared for us, in payment we are obliged to care for others.

On this occasion we pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda that he inspire us, that somehow or other he injects us with some fraction of that force that lead him to save the whole world. At such a ripe age this very strong force was moving him.

We pray to him that, by his blessing and mercy, we may receive even some reflection of a particle of that great force that made him do the work that he has done. He has done such great service for Kṛṣṇa and the previous *ācāryas*. Now he is our previous *ācārya* and we need his own energy to fulfill our miniscule mission within his mission. May this be our prayer on this solemn occasion. Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

*His unworthy servant,*  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**”I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Today, more than ever before, I appreciate your greatness and the unique characteristics that distinguished you as a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa and as the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your devotional qualities are unlimited and incomparable. Because I am very foolish, slow to understand and recognize, it has taken twenty years for me to be able to say the following to you.

I have always perceived your spiritual potency by intuition rather than by experience. Today my satisfaction knows no bounds because at last Kṛṣṇa has given me a glimpse of the extraordinary relationship between the Vedic conclusions and your Bhaktivedanta purports, as well as their relationship with your every activity.

Your every action, word, and gesture is a symbol of spiritual perfection. I cherish and love spiritual perfection, but I always thought it something mystical and remote. To see such perfection embodied in Your Divine Grace produces overwhelming sentiments of genuine happiness in my heart. What a great fortune to have met you! What a great pleasure to listen to your transcendental voice in classes and *bhajanas* again and again! What extraordinary peace and joy I experience while reading your matchless books! What an honor and privilege to be your representative! What immense pride to be your disciple.

During the last twenty years I have felt an asphyxiating weight within myself due to lacking the qualifications to be your disciple and member of your glorious ISKCON movement. But today I began to feel new waves of unknown happiness and a sensation of a very special kind of well-being characteristic of those who are on the lap of a benevolent and wish-fulfilling destiny.

In former times I was tormented by uncertainty, but now I am fully confident that all this is true. Now the time of doubts has elapsed, and I feel at home, near to you, although still a considerable distance from your sublime realizations which will always remain a mystery to me. My happiness is so great that it dispels all my preoccupations and complexes. I feel that my love for you has begun because I have accepted you as my only master, my father, and my friend.

I would like to relieve you of the burden I’ve created for you, but I don’t know how. I cannot stop being your presumptuous and incompetent son, who cannot take advantage of your advice. Perhaps I should have told you all this on my initiation day, but as already stated, I am very slow to perceive the real value of things.

One day, six years after my initiation, I remember becoming mad with happiness while chanting *japa* due to realizing the importance of being your disciple. It seemed as though a door was slowly opening in my heart revealing this well-known fact as if it were something absolutely new.

In ordinary consciousness, things manifest in a different way, they are more like mental experiences, I would say “without any touch of transcendence.” Therefore, when I now see the difference, my soul fills with joy because I am confident that I am beginning to see your unlimited opulence. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for accepting me in your service in spite of my disqualification. Please forgive my offenses.

During my recent visit to Māyāpur, I was able to associate with your disciples and followers who enlightened me by their authentic, genuine Vaiṣṇava qualities. They are made of your mercy and, because of their association, I am more aware of your extraordinary spiritual potency. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because even though I am the last of your sons, you have allowed me to participate in this wonderful *saṅga*. Wonderful was the Gaura-maṇḍala Parikramā, and wonderful the festival of Lord Gaurāṅga. Everything happens by your grace. During the annual report given to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, we could see that major obstacles are vanishing, and that the pyramidal progress of Lord Caitanya’s movement is spreading all over the world. As you used to say: “First they will mock you, then they will harass you, but finally they will glorify you.”

The world needed genuine compassion, and you gave it. The benefit of your books expands unchecked all over the planet like a transcendental epidemic awakening feelings of love of Kṛṣṇa in the hearts of all human beings. To see your predictions fulfilled beyond your own expectations produces a constant, overwhelming, exhilarating joy in my heart which can be translated as deeper and deeper acknowledgement of your work, and as an immense and ever-growing impetus to renewed surrender to your divine lotus feet.

We are becoming more aware of our eternal debt to you, and our spiritual intelligence is becoming awakened as we make arrangements for you centennial celebrations. In this way our service to your lotus feet finds the resources of creativity and dynamism that should always be present. During the campaign, the devotees realize that you are their only savior and that ISKCON is the only alternative, the only hope for providing spiritual guidance for humanity. My own experience is that in Costa Rica and Central America in general, where due to historical and cultural reasons people are somewhat indifferent, there is now significant progress indicating the gradual and irrevocable establishment of Vedic culture. Thousands of people have become vegetarians. They make offerings of *bhoga* and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Book distribution continues to increase on every social level. In some places they use your books as school texts. Regularly people ask us to present our philosophy in high schools, hospitals, cultural centers, radio and TV programs. Especially in Costa Rica where the preaching has been more constant, the movement has such popularity and recognition that even we are surprised. Your compassion is thus manifested, and this progress is the irrefutable proof that our movement will provide spiritual leadership for the whole world in due course of time.

Within Vaiṣṇavism ISKCON has a solid and functional structure which can naturally integrate all Vaiṣṇava sects for the purpose of effectively preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Your society has worldwide organizational abilities and besides receiving recognition as a bonafide spiritual movement, it is inspiring different groups of Vaiṣṇavas to adopt your methods. The influence, language and concepts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness can be appreciated in all cultural and religious sectors on the planet. This is symptomatic that the spirituality the world is seeking is contained in every page of your transcendental books and within ISCKON, as formed by your sincere followers.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! You have taught us that there is no limit to what can be done for Kṛṣṇa. Limitations exist within us according to our desire to please *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. Personally I am so limited and deficient that even now I cannot take advantage of the extraordinary teachings of your exemplary life which is fully dedicated to the service of your Guru Mahārāja and Kṛṣṇa.

Excessive arrogance hinders my surrender to your divine lotus feet. Please give your blessings, even in the form of chastisement, so that I can be rid of the pride that still lingers in my heart. Bless me that I can dedicate myself eternally to your compassionate mission to rescue the conditioned souls who are suffering without any hope in the world of illusion in this dangerous age of Kali.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakti Abhay Caran Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
A thing called pūjā,  
An ancient rite  
Of dhūpa and of dīpa  
For fragrance and light,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
Subjis and savories,  
An entire cuisine,  
The method as well  
For keeping it clean,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
A recipe for the teeth,  
Some oil, some salt,  
Calcium and clover,  
Free from dental fault,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
The image of God  
A wide-eyed Lord,  
A Deity so festive  
With cart and with cord,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
Saffron and śikhā.  
It gives us a mark,  
An unique attire.  
Who’ll not remark?  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
A place for the family  
A husband, a wife  
Children and house  
A lesson of strife,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
The simple way of life,  
The bull and the cow  
And land to be worked  
With the sweat of the brow,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
The Lord and His consort  
In golds and in reds,  
The best bedecked  
From feet to Their heads,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
Management and order  
With palms we pursue,  
Lakṣmī and men  
In file and in queue,  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
International club  
Each brother and sister,  
The principle “respect  
And love for one another,”  
But we are ungrateful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have given us everything,  
Even your very self  
And all that you know  
Nothing is amiss.  
That you did not show,  
But we are ungrateful.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**”I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**”I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-ṣūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**”Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

**Hope**

Observing my own disqualification and the progressive symptoms of violence, disruption and hypocrisy in this world, I realize you are my only hope, O Lord and master. The only hope I have is to develop the strong conviction that I will one day receive your favor by trying my best to follow your instructions. At the same time I feel quite incompetent to do so. This gives me great pain and hopelessness.

Coming across a verse by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, again I received great hope and inspiration. He says, “I have no love for Kṛṣṇa, nor for the causes of developing love of Kṛṣṇa – namely, hearing and chanting. And the process of *bhakti-yoga,* by which one is always thinking of Kṛṣṇa and fixing His lotus feet in the heart, is also lacking in me. As far as philosophical knowledge or pious works are concerned, I don’t see any opportunity for me to execute such activities. But above all, I am not even born of a nice family. Therefore I must simply wish and hope that some way or other I may be able to approach Your lotus feet, and this hope is giving me pain, because I think myself quite incompetent to approach that transcendental goal of life.”

Commenting on this verse, you kindly explain that one should continue to hope against hope that some way or other one will be able to approach the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Similarly, I will continue to hope against hope that one day I will be able to approach your divine lotus feet by following your instructions, because you have taken full shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord. Therefore your instructions are my life and soul. *Guru-mukha padma vākya, citte koriyā aikya,* ”Make the teachings emanating from the lotus mouth of the spiritual master one with your heart, and do not desire anything else.” By earnestly trying to follow your instructions, I always feel your association. You have given me transcendental knowledge, therefore you are my lord birth after birth, *cakhu dān dilo jei, janme janme prabhu sei – dibya jñān hṛde prokāśito*.

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, let the whole world worship Your Divine Grace as the perfect representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s mind is compared to the sky because the sky is the transmitting agent for knowledge by means of sound. All other sages throughout the entire universe, great poets and thinkers, as well as writers, take advantage of the vast knowledge given by Śrīla Vyāsadeva. It is said that the whole world is tasting the remnants of Vyāsa’s knowledge. Whatever ideas people have can be traced back to the writings of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. They have been borrowed consciously of unconsciously from Him. Similarly, we particularly notice that more and more influential and important members of world society are extracting the ideas presented in their writings, movies, songs, public speeches, etc., directly or indirectly from the sky like treasury house of your transcendental knowledge. I am yearning to see that day when human society actually recognizes your true position as the pure representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, empowered to give shelter to the whole world.

I hope to be able to serve you as a humble instrument in spreading your glory all over the world and thus remain chaste to the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace. This hope maintains my life in this hopeless world.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances on his most auspicious occasion of your appearance. At this time I remember my good fortune in meeting you many years ago, and how you showered your boundless compassion upon my unworthy self.

The first time I saw you was a picture in *Life* magazine in 1967, which had an article about all the different “*yogīs*” doing their thing in New York City. As I read the article with interest, I could not help feeling uneasy with the pictures of the bogus yogīs displayed there. They all had beady eyes, long stringy hair and beards. They looked like crooks and pickpockets. Then I saw your picture. I was immediately struck by the immense gravity of your expression, and the atmosphere of peace in which you seemed to be situated. Again and again I came back to your picture. Finally I took a pair of scissors and cut it out from the magazine. I thought for a while what to do with this special prize and then decided to paste it on the door of my locker at high school. While all my classmates had pin-up girls or sports stars on their lockers, I had you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I don’t think here there was a kid in my school who did not come to see your picture at some time or other to ponder your transcendental pose.

I did not know how to pronounce your name and did not even try to remember it, I simply knew that at some time, somehow, I would become your follower and learn how to worship Lord Kṛṣṇa from you. Many moths passed. When spring vacation came in April of 1968, my older brother invited me to go with him to New York City. I unhesitatingly agreed to go in hopes of meeting you. I prayed to God for the good fortune of finding you and your devotees during my visit to New York City.

We took a train from upstate New York. Upon reaching the city, my brother took me to Greenwich Village. From there we wandered around until we got lost. We eventually wound up on the Bowery, where I lost all belief in the greatness of the America way of life. We continued wandering until we reached Second Avenue. As we were turning on First Street to head towards the West Side, something suddenly burst into my awareness through the corner of my eye. It caught my attention like the shining sun popping out from behind a black storm cloud. It was a store sign which proclaimed: “Hare Krishna.” My head suddenly yanked around, followed by my body. Reflexively I darted across the busy street and stepped inside the hollowed space of the 26 Second Avenue. Inside I met a teenage girl devotee named Sudarśana. In the course of our conversation, she informed me that you would be arriving the next day from San Francisco and that I should come to see you then.

You arrived the next morning and that evening I came to New York City to see you. I entered the temple room and sat on a musty Persian rug not far from your āsana. Other devotees were coming in and sitting all around me. Candles flickered and incense filled the air. Then the devotees began to slowly chime the hand cymbals and to thump our rhythms with the clay *mṛdaṅgas* as they sang your praises with the Sanskrit verse: *nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-pādāya bhūtale, śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*. As the volume and tempo of the chanting increased, so did the ecstasy of the devotees. The temple room was crammed full of devotees and guests like myself, who were all eagerly awaiting your arrival. In the back of the temple room, near a side entranceway on the left, stood Brahmānanda and Pradyumna. Near them were *brahmacārīṇīs* – Kāñcambala, Līlāśuka, Indirā and Ekāyanī. They were frantically jumping up and down to the rhythm of the chanting and repeatedly screaming “Swamiji” with their shrill voices. I noticed that several devotees next to the side door had conchshells, but I did not know that these were for blowing.

Suddenly, when the chanting had reached its peak, there was heard the loud blast of conchshells. To me it sounded like sirens, and since everyone immediately threw themselves on the ground, I did likewise, expecting an imminent explosion from a bomb. Instead, however, there was only the gentle murmur of prayers being recited by the bowed devotees. I raised my head up to see what was going on. At that moment the side door opened and from behind it emerged a mass of glowing saffron. As I gazed at you, I realized that although before our eyes, you were not from this world. You were present before us at a point where the spiritual plane met the material plane so that we could have a glimpse of transcendence. With wide blinking eyes, I watched as you made your way across the crowded room. It appeared to me that your feet never touched the ground. You obviously had no need for such trivial things. You sat upon your seat in a dignified manner and acknowledged the presence of everyone with a reassuring glance. As your glance caught my eyes, I understood that you saw my naked soul in my heart, and thus knew everything about me.

You picked up your hand cymbals and began chanting the prayers to the disciplic succession. The mode of the chanting was solemn. As I listened, I felt that I was awash in an ocean of timelessness. By the prayers you vibrated I could feel myself connected with Godhead Himself. Then you began chanting the mahāmantra: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare; Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. There was a stirring among the assembled devotees. They rose from the floor and began dancing. You were deeply absorbed in concentration as you lead the chanting. Soon the devotees were swaying back and forth with the chanting, their arms extended upwards toward the ceiling. I also danced. As the chanting became more intense, the devotees jumped and shouted, with tears streaking down their cheeks. My body was trembling and I was sweating so profusely that my clothes were completely drenched. I had gone from an ocean of timelessness into the depths of an ocean of transcendental bliss from which I never wished to leave.

Finally you brought the chanting to an end and proceeded to give a lecture. I could tell by your expression and the tone of your voice that the subject was very important, but I was unable to understand very much, because of my unfamiliarity with your accent. Even so, I listened everything you said. After finishing the lecture, you retired to your room upstairs. The devotees rolled out a long plastic mat and placed wooden bowls along both sides. Soon they were serving hot banana milk.

After tasting my first *prasāda,* a devotee named Advaita asked me if I wanted to see the altar from up close. I agreed. He took me right up to the altar, which was a wooden framework covered with velvet, and stretched his right arm toward the picture on the altar. As I looked to see which pictures he was pointing at, I saw before me a larger version of the same picture of you which I had pasted on my locker door at high school. I was shocked and realized that Kṛṣṇa had brought me to you and that I had to surrender myself to you as your disciple – I had no other alternative. I knew that it was not by chance that I was standing before your picture at that time. I also understood that my decision to surrender myself to you was the most important undertaking of my entire life. I was scared. I was only eighteen years old, but my life was no longer mine to live. I would have to abandon all my personal plans and become your menial servant. I was incapable of making any other choice. Many years have passed since then, yet my prayer is still the same: Let me always remain engaged in your service in the association of your dear devotees. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my eternal spiritual father. All glories to the wonderful society of devotees – The International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness – established by you for our deliverance.

*Your humble servant,*  
R.K. Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

## Bhaktivikāśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in my last Vyāsa-pūjā offering (which unfortunately was not published in the 1993 Vyasa puja book, for what reason I do not know) I asked you for the benediction to be able to engage in writing as my service. By your grace, the two major obstacles hindering my writing have been removed, at least temporarily. By your grace, I have been able to offer you three new books in the last year, namely *A Message to the Youth of India, Vaṁśi dāsa Bābājī,* and *Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!* (the last is a special offering to Your Divine Grace). I have also been able to bring out some of my books in different languages. By your grace also, my writings have been well received. I feel very happy to be able to contribute in this small way to the furtherance of your mission. I thank you for allowing me to serve in this way, and request your continued blessings to go on with this work.

Here are some observations about your mission that I am submitting to you as my 1994 Vyāsa-pūjā offering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda established Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world and planted the seeds for a great spiritual upsurge. He emphasized the distribution of his books as the best means to bring about a revolution in the misdirected lives of a godless civilization.

Since the departure of Śrīla Prabhupāda in 1977, his movement has been through many ups and downs. However, the book distribution and preaching has always continued. Sadly, in strategically important America, book distribution has greatly declined. However, the many millions of books that have been distributed there in the past are having their effect.

Śrīla Prabhupāda predicted that his books would change the world and perceived their effect in all spheres of life. For instance, when Śrīla Prabhupāda was informed that a major platform of Jimmy Carter’s successful Presidential campaign was his commitment to religion, Prabhupāda ascribed the prominence being given to religion as a result of his preaching.

Today, despite the continued ravages of Kali-yuga, there are similar encouraging signs. For instance, twenty years ago in the West, vegetarianism was looked upon as a crazy fad. Now it is an accepted lifestyle for millions. Similarly, many have foregone alcohol and tobacco. The external cause for these major behavioral changes is health considerations. However, devotees recognize the underlying influence of the mode of goodness brought about by the distribution of Śrīla Prabhupāda books.

Even more exciting, the truths of *karma* and reincarnation are becoming ever more widely accepted in the West. At least there is a widespread awareness of the concept of reincarnation. Twenty years ago there was not.

Another positive development is the “Straight Edge.” Thousands of young Americans now eschew, or aspire to eschew, non-vegetarian food, intoxication, gambling and (amazingly!) illicit sex. Better still, they are spiritual seekers, and are open to Kṛṣṇa. Their numbers may not be significant, but their ideals are.

Perhaps the most important development, however, is in the field of science – that most conservative bastion of sophisticated godlessness. Śrīla Prabhupāda saw materialistic scientists as high priests of a society deliberately turned away from God. Prabhupāda was certain that if the attention of scientists could somehow be focused on *bhagavat-tattva-vijñāna,* they would gradually have to acknowledge its superiority. Convincing scientists is practically our most important preaching, for if they accept Kṛṣṇa consciousness, everyone else will follow. Winning over scientists is not easy, and overnight changes are not to be expected. However, there are very encouraging signs.

Consider the following extract from “Cosmos, Bios, Theos,” a 1992 survey of the views of seventy top scientists:

Stranger than the strangest concepts and theories of science is the appearance of God on the intellectual horizon of late twentieth –century science. Pioneers and giants of modern science like Einstein, Planck, and Heisenberg were equally at home with the “hard facts” of science and with a theological frame of reference. The paradox persists to this very day. Einstein once said “I want to know how God created this world... I want to know His thoughts, the rest are details.” Stephen Hawking, the theoretical physicist who is often described as Einstein’s successor, has declared in his recent bestseller, “A Brief History of Time,” that our goal should be to “know the mind of God.” And astrophysicist Robert Jastrow begins “God and the Astronomers,” his celebrated survey of modern cosmology, with a remarkable observation: “For the scientist who has lived by his faith in the power of reason, the story ends like a bad dream. He has scaled the mountain of ignorance; he is about to conquer the highest peak; as he pulls himself over the final rock, he is greeted by a band of theologians who have been sitting there for centuries.”

Previously, scientists rejected Biblical concepts of creation and cosmology as being grossly inaccurate and superstitious. Today, the scientist, in his quest for ever deeper knowledge of the universe, finds himself back at the door of transcendence. The modern philosopher of science, in his metaphysical wanderings, is not restricted to Christian theology, for present day seekers of truth are free to avail of the many systems of religion and philosophy extant in the world. Recent attempts to wed science with the supramundane mostly led to the “Tao of Physics” type of impersonalistic speculation. It is significant to note from such publications as “Cosmos, Bios, Theos,” that several highly reputed scientists are now gravitating towards the understanding of God as the supreme controller of the laws of nature. In his books, Śrīla Prabhupāda presents knowledge of God and His energies with a clarity and depth not available from any other source. Therefore, it is quite feasible that in the not too distant future, *bhagavat-tattva-vijñāna*, the *Bhāgavata* philosophy in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books will become accepted in scientific circles, first, as a legitimate hypothesis of reality, and eventually as the guiding light for major breakthroughs in scientific understanding.

The astonishing rapid growth of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the ex-communist block continues unabated. These tremendous advances in a large and significant area of the planet promise to be a prominent factor in establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness as a major world religious force.

In the West also, there are good signs. The movement is well established having been around for almost thirty years. Many people like devotees, think of them as good people, and appreciate their dedication, joyful chanting, and *prasād*a distribution. Many young people, especially, think of the Hare Kṛṣṇa as being a genuinely spiritual movement.

In India, ISKCON has become a household word. ISKCON is widely respected for its worldwide propagation of Kṛṣṇa-*bhakti*, its exemplary standard of Deity worship, and for the sincerity, purity and strictness of its members. Whereas, twenty years ago, devotees often met with the response, “I am a Hindu, and I know everything,” it is now not unusual for highly-educated and well-positioned Indians to approach Western devotees and respectfully submit, “We Hindus now have to learn about our own culture and religion from you.”

In India, where impersonalism ruled supreme for so long, the power of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books is beginning to manifest. Many Hindus are now emerging from the confusing of *māyāvāda* to understand *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* – unconditional surrender to Lord Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

In all ways, on all fronts, the preaching field is opening up and getting more and more exciting. This is all the grace of Lord Caitanya, as manifest through the activities of His most dear devotee, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. There is tremendous scope for our movement to surge forward and to truly amazing things.

Perhaps our best offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda for his Centennial Celebration would be to offer ourselves, the members of his movement, as a truly united family, serving together with full trust and cooperation, combinedly working towards the goal that Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted most of all – a new world order under the flag of Kṛṣṇa.

All glories to your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, people often ask me if I am your disciple, and I proudly proclaim: “Yes, I am a disciple of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.” Of course, it is good that I am proud of you, my eternal father. But, actually being your disciple is such a elevated position, that I have no right to consider myself worthy of the designation: “Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciple.”

In a letter written to one of your disciples who referred to himself as a devotee in a poem he wrote for your pleasure, you stated: “The poem is very nice, however one should not think himself a devotee... one cannot call himself a devotee, but servant he can call himself always... Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu presented Himself as*: patitaṁ kiṅkaram māṁ viṣame bhavāmbudhau.’ Kiṅkaram* means servant.” (letter from Śrīla Prabhupāda)

To be a devotee means to be devoted without any other motivation other than serving the supreme object of devotion – Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, to be a disciple means that one must be disciplined by the spiritual master so that one is always acting in accordance with the order of the spiritual master. In other words, to be a disciple means to be perfectly obedient.

You have very nicely defined discipleship in a letter:

”If there is no obedience there cannot be any discipline. And if there is no discipline you cannot manage anything. That is not possible. Therefore this is very essential, that the students should be very disciplined. Disciple means one who follows discipline. This is a disciple, *śiṣya*. The Sanskrit word is also the same, *śiṣya*. I have several times explained. *Śiṣya,* it comes from the verb *śas śāsana,* ruling. So *śiṣya* means one who voluntarily accepts the ruling of the spiritual master. He is called *śiṣya. Śiṣya, śāsana, śāstra, śāsana* – these things are the same, from the same root. So this is the instruction. *Chadāṁsy adhiyitā guror āhutaś cet suyantritaḥ, upakrame āvasane ca caraṇau śirasā namet.”* (letter from Śrīla Prabhupāda)

In many conversations you have made the same point:

”Disciple means discipline. The word discipline comes from disciple, or disciple comes from discipline. So unless there is discipline, there is no question of disciple. This discipline must... That should be uniform. Otherwise, *śiṣya... Śiṣya,* the word *śiṣya,* it comes from the root, verb, *śās-dhātu. Śās. Śās* means ruling. From this word, *śāsana. Śāsana* means government. *Śāstra. Śāstra* means weapon, and *śāstra,* scripture, and *śiṣya.*..“ (page 73)

”These things have come from the one root *śās-dhātu*. So *śās-dhātu* means ruling under discipline. There is another English word, that ‘Obedience is the first law of discipline,’ or something. They say, ‘Obedience is the first law of discipline?’ So I am right? ‘Obedience is...?’ That is the...

Tamāl Krishna: Yes, that’s more or less what it is.

Prabhupāda: No, what is the word, exact. There is an English word. ‘Obedience is the first law of discipline.’ Unless there is obedience, there cannot be any discipline. And unless there is discipline, there is no question of disciple. Disciple means one who follows the discipline.” (Conversations with Śrīla Prabhupāda)

In fact you have emphatically stated that one who is not controlled by the order of the spiritual master should never claim that he is a disciple:

”If still you are unable to fulfill that promise to your spiritual master, then what is the use of calling yourself devotee and disciple? That is simply pretending. So you should think like this, that I have promised my spiritual master this, now I must obey him without any exception, otherwise I have no business calling myself his disciple.” (letter from Śrīla Prabhupāda)

In several instances you have declared that one who does not follow your instructions is not a disciple because “he is against the principle.”

So, I should recognize that to have the right to call myself your disciple, I should be willing to follow all of your instructions, not only the major ones, but the minor ones as well. Being your actual disciple is not an ordinary thing, but the most extraordinary thing. I aspire to one day be your disciple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please grant me the strength to accomplish this.

*Your servant,*  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

In the *Nectar of Devotion* you state, “devotional service is a continual process from one’s previous life. No one can take to devotional service unless he has some previous connection with it.”

Although that is the basic eligibility for practicing *sādhana-bhakti,* you also explain, “but even if there is no continuity, if only by chance a person takes interest in a pure devotee’s instruction, he can be accepted and can advance in devotional service.”

My coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness must be in the second category. By your mercy only was I given shelter at the mansion of your lotus feet, and my first spiritual account was opened in my new name, Dhanurdhara dāsa.

By giving me the treasure of the holy name and the wealth of service to your mission, my spiritual assets began to grow. Gradually I was able to make a small but permanent deposit within my consciousness of *śraddhā,* or a taste for devotional service.

On my second birthday in Kṛṣṇa consciousness you even deposited a precious *cintāmaṇi* jewel, residence in Vṛndāvana.

In every way you have made my fortune, as whatever value I have is from you. Thus a great anxiety burns in my heart, how can I possibly repay you?

Like worshipping the Gaṅgā with its own water, I thus can only offer at your lotus feet what you have given me: faith in the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and your vision for distributing it. As you have come only to increase the service of Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, I hope this will be of some pleasure to you.

Faith means to trust in something sublime. Although, in the form of your instructions, you have given us a clear road to the most sublime goal, faith in these directions may not always be easy. Especially when there are difficulties in our service, our experience, logic, and so-called practicality may cause us to doubt your directions, or even question if they are yours. However, without faith in your order, we cannot move an inch closer to the fulfillment of our spiritual practices and our service.

Particularly, I would like to offer you my faith in the instructions you have given regarding my service – Vṛndāvana Gurukula. I have no doubt what you told us, “It will be difficult to establish, but you will be successful!”

Actually, this faith is not mine alone. If I tried myself to discern and adhere to your vision for Gurukula, certainly I would have failed. I would thus like to present the faith also of our teachers. By their strict dedication to your instructions, Vṛndāvana Gurukula has become successful.

Our students also offer you their full allegiance. They strongly believe in your educational program and prove it by following your program with full enthusiasm.

For your pleasure I would thus like to offer at your lotus feet our unflinching faith in your vision:

”Any five year old student can be trained and within a very short time his life will become successful by realization of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Unfortunately, this training is lacking all over the world. It is necessary for the leaders of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement to start educational institutions in different parts of the world to train children, starting at the age of five years. Thus such children will not become hippies of spoiled children of society, rather they can become devotees of the Lord. The face of the world will then change automatically.” (*SB* 4.12.23 purport)

*Your servant,*  
Danurdhara Swami

## Gaṇapati Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my insignificant obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. Please forgive my offenses and purify me.

While engaged in your service of book distribution, I have on occasion been blessed to meet those prospective devotees who have, by the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa, awakened to a noteworthy appreciation of your transcendental qualities.

A vivid example of this occurred at the University of New Mexico, when an older female was immediately attracted to the photo of Your Divine Grace on the cover of *The Science of Selfrealization.* “That’s him! That’s the man in my dream!” With avid anticipation, I was able to elicit her story: She and her friend, acting as some sort of emissaries for planet earth, were aboard a spacecraft speeding towards their destination – the supposed abode of the sovereign of this universe. They were confidently carrying a rather strange, but prized “exhibit” – the spiritualized body of Your Divine Grace as you had left it upon your *samādhi* pastime. It appears that their mission was to convince the cosmic superintendents of the Earth’s worth by presenting them a specimen of the superior beings that it had sustained. Thus, in their estimation, they carried the remains of the man who showed how to be “all you can be” even on this lowly planet handicapped by the vicious qualities of Kali.

Of course, the lady didn’t know the real story behind your personality of success, but I’m sure she will have a better idea after reading the Prabhupāda biography and the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* that she walked away with.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, researchers or sociologists may have many theories about the enormous success of your ISKCON movement. But to your disciples and admirers, it is obvious that what sets the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement apart from all other attempts of knowing the Absolute Truth is, precisely, Your Divine Grace.
