The science of Godhead is only as effective as the one who professes it – the living example of Bhāgavata.

Your Divine Grace showed us that self-realization is practical and attainable. You demonstrated that devotional service is *apratihatā* – irrespective of all *dharmas,* transcendental to all *kleśas,* and superior to all *siddhis*. You exhibited the supreme status of *ahaitukī-bhakti,* devoid of *lobha, pūjā* and *pratiṣṭhā.*

May I simply desire your association and service (and chastisement), because wherever you are, Lord Kṛṣṇa is situated within your heart, along with all His *tadīyas*. There is no meaning or accessibility to Vṛndāvana Dhāma without you. Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa has submitted Himself to the will of Your Divine Grace. If you desire, my attraction to Śyāmasundara and His holy *dhāma* may be awakened. Without your mercy, I am just wasting my time. You truly are Jagat-guru, for everyone is your śiṣya. Some perceive it and some don’t. I am realizing that the greatest pleasure is to somehow be the medium for the reciprocal flow of mercy and attraction between Your Divine Grace and the sincere conditioned souls (what we call preaching). Please forever keep me thus engaged.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Girdhārī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Gazing across the harbor toward Hong Kong Island, I see the Hilton Hotel, where you stayed twenty years ago. The transcendental seed you planted here, in this Gateway to China, has grown into the fruit-laden tree of a thriving ISKCON and even sent creepers beyond the Bamboo Curtain.

Hearing from a Chinese man who was with you then, Yeung Pak Hei, I see your preaching mood to be hard as a thunderbolt and soft as a rose: strong and uncompromising, yet soft and compassionate.

It was Bhakta Yeung’s first meeting with you, and his first question: “How could one with a cultural background centered around the family take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness?” he asked during a lecture.

”He is diseased!” you answered in an angry, chastising voice.

”Yes I am,” Bhakta Yeung admitted.

In an instant your sword-like words had cut away lifetimes of attachment from Bhakta Yeung’s heart. Your mercy enabled him to surrender to the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace and Kṛṣṇa. Bhakta Yeung later became your only Chinese disciple, Yaśomatīsūta dāsa.

But your words were not meant for Yaśomatīsūta Prabhu alone. Your words were a message to Chinese people everywhere: “Give up your disease of mundane family attachment and taste the bliss of spiritual family attachment by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious.”

Some years later, in Māyāpura, you listened to Yaśomatīsūta Prabhu read a paragraph from his Chinese translation of Bhagavad-gītā As It Is. “Isn’t that sweet!” you said. Your message, originally planted in the heart of Yaśomatīsūta Prabhu, was now ready to be broadcast to 25% of the world’s population through the Chinese translation of your books.

Two simple exchanges, one like a thunderbolt, one like a rose. But you showed that by planting a seed in the heart of one disciple, you could conquer the largest country in the world. It is only a matter of time.

I pray to remain your humble servant in the Far East,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

**Part I**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. I’m writing to you from the United States, your first preaching stop in the Western world. While you were on this planet, the devotees here were all cooperating nicely under your expert guidance. The temples and the preaching that you established were expanding without limit. And there was no doubt in our minds that by your mercy this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would take over this country in just a short time.

To our great dismay, however, in less than a decade after your departure, we found ourselves left here with only a skeleton of the spiritual empire that you had risked your life to build. And, thus, the optimism we were once filled with turned to discouragement.

Those of us who have remained in America over these years have been trying to contemplate very deeply how to rectify ourselves and establish your mission on firm ground. It isn’t so difficult to see where we failed you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, during the time after you left. When you were present You had repeatedly warned us, “Our society is like one big family, and our relationships should be based on love and trust.” (Let. 70-08-06) “Let us try to work cooperatively. Otherwise the fighting spirit will ruin our great movement.” (Let. 74-04-10) “Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself wanted our cooperation. He is God, Kṛṣṇa. And therefore cooperation is very important thing. Nobody should think that ‘I have got so great ability. I can do.’ No. It is simply by cooperation we can do very big thing. ‘United we stand; divided we fall...’ Saṅkīrtana means many men combined together chanting. That is saṅkīrtana.” (Rm. conv., May, 76/03/16)

As we push on with our efforts to repopulate your temples, build your congregations, and distribute more and more of your books, we fervently pray that your above words will remain heavily implanted in our hearts and spurn us to kick out our envy, our selfish schemes, and our competitive spirit.

Some of our God brothers overseas have been jokingly calling us “Waskcon America.” We take that as a challenge from these loving well wishers to inspire us to use our American know-how to make you prouder of our efforts here than you have ever been and to help you fulfill your dream for the American people. As you have said, “I want to begin one revolution... against Godless civilization. That is my ambition. The American will be the best person to be educated in this line and to lead, to become leaders. They are already leader, but they must be real leader now, so that the whole world may be happy. That I can give direction. If the topmost American gentlemen come to me, I can give them direction how they can become the world leader. Actual leader. Actual leader, not bogus leader... That is my ambition.” (Morn. Walk, 73/12/14, LA)

We know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we will not be able to fulfill your ambition for America within the next few years. We have great hope, however, that by your Centennial Celebration in 1996 the devotees in America will be able to offer you such a united and well-working team that you have no doubt that soon your “American dream” will come true. Falling at your feet and praying to meditate on your mission night and day, I remain...

**Part II**

**Nothing else will ever do**

Pristine morning hours  
My favorite time to be  
Absorbed in meditation  
On all you’ve let me see.

Early morning hours,  
Just you, the Lord and me.  
I wish that it could last  
For all eternity.

Reflections in my journal,  
On all you have to say.  
Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee  
Showing me the way.

Your words, your voice, your smile,  
Placed within my heart.  
I pray that they remain there,  
To help me stay apart

From the deadly world of *māyā*,  
That beckons as the sun  
Rises in the heavens,  
Calling everyone.

As Kali’s agents stalk,  
Mad after their pray,  
Early morning thoughts recalled  
Will get me through the day.

With transcendental knowledge,  
I’ll beat them at their game.  
Your words and books will tame them,  
And help them chant the name.

Transcendental sound,  
Your mystical infusion,  
Will shower Kali’s agents,  
Ending in intrusion.

This chanting and remembering,  
Inspiring others, too,  
Is our eternal pastime,  
Revealed to us by you.

*Saṅkīrtana* forewer.  
What can be as sweet?  
Communing with the Lord  
And everyone we meet.

And as day is brought to a close,  
And morning comes once more,  
Again I yearn for *darśana*,  
Your mercy to explore.

And when I cannot get it,  
When time does not allow,  
I’m pained in separation,  
And cannot figure how.

I’d go on fighting *māyā*,  
If I couldn’t hear from you,  
Who’s giving love of Kṛṣṇa.  
Nothing else will ever do.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī Swami

## Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I am so filled with ignorance that I don’t really know who I am nor anybody else. Maybe I have some vague impressions of what is actually happening around me after twenty years of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Personally I feel like many years of following the sincerest line I know of for advancing your service is coming to a crucial point. The question is a life and death matter of whether my own practical realization of your books and ISKCON actually works. But even if it doesn’t, even if we have exhausted every avenue of making progress in pushing on this *saṅkīrtana* movement in your absence, still there is no doubt of lack of direction or diminished enthusiasm! We can “give up our bodies and go to the place of the spiritual master (*gurukula*)” and stay with you personally for more training. You will never give up on us. We will all ultimately learn to pass the most difficult tests and become *mahātmās,* just like so many of your other disciples.

By your absurdly clear instructions you have made this Kṛṣṇa consciousness so simple that even a first class fool can understand it and be in ecstasy, regardless of the greatest calamities. We have nothing to ask of you on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day. We only pray to you that we want to be your servants life after life and engage in devotional service just as you have taught us. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is actually very exciting, ecstatic and extremely blissful. Than you very much Śrīla Prabhupāda for so kindly teaching us how to become devotees and be really happy almost all the time.

*Your humble servant,*  
Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This year I circled the world,  
In service unto you,  
Preaching your glories and those of the Lord  
To the fallen and destitute.

Armed with knowledge and staff in hand,  
I challenged both learned and fool  
With śāstric evidence, logic and reasoning  
And even *prasādam* too!

Of all that is good and all that is bad  
There is nothing I didn’t see,  
For Heaven and Hell both exist  
In this world of duality.

But of all that is glorious and all that is great,  
There was nothing that compared  
With the miracle of your kindness  
In freeing us from despair.

For your mercy is like a touchstone  
That melts the hearts of all  
With Kṛṣṇa-*bhakti,* Love of God,  
Unto your beloved Lord.

In London I saw lords and ladies  
Clothed in royal dress;  
They smiled and waved from their motorcade  
As our *kīrtana* passed in bliss.

And Oh! That day that we marched on Parliament,  
Thirty-five thousand strong!  
They wanted to close your Manor,  
But they saw that it was wrong!

And in Moscow all your dreams came true,  
When our *kīrtana* numbered a thousand;  
Remembering my visit in ‘86  
I thought it would never happen.

In Durban, I saw heads of state  
Offer you glorification;  
To Rātha-yātrā they send their praise –  
De Klerk, Mandela and Butalezi.

In the southern towns, black with soot,  
Deep in the heart of Poland,  
The miners joked and sneered at us,  
Full of hate and misconception.

But in all those towns, every time,  
It always came to be,  
They’d sing and dance along with us;  
Your mercy set them free!

In Christchurch, far from Purī,  
We held Rātha-yātrā in the streets;  
Your childhood pastime expanded  
To bring Jagannātha within their reach.

In all the cities and all the towns  
Throughout the entire world,  
Your poem upon the Jaladuta  
Continues to unfold...

*tāra icchā balavān – pāscātyete ṭhān ṭhān*  
*hoy jāte gaurāṅger nām*  
*pṛthivīte nagarādi - āsamudra nada nadī*  
*sakalei loy kṛṣṇa-nām*

”The Holy Name of the Lord Gaurāṅga  
Will spread throughout the world,  
By the strong desire of Śrī Siddhānta  
Who bestows devotion to the Lord.”

O Prabhupāda! How happy I am  
To always be engaged  
In preaching your sublime message  
In this dark and evil age.

May you grant me service just like this  
In each and every birth,  
Until you’re satisfied to see  
Your mercy flood the earth.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

By celebrating this Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony, we will directly worship the most powerful representative of Vyāsa in modern times. Śrīla Prabhupāda was unique in his devotion and achievements. Of course we cannot separate the sublime life of Śrīla Prabhupāda from the growth and success of his spiritual society, the ISKCON organization. Actually Śrīla Prabhupāda’s entire life was a garland of offerings given to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, culminating in his opening of this worldwide society.

Once, on the occasion of the Vyāsa-pūjā of his own Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, Śrīla Prabhupāda said he had nothing to offer him, except the results of his preaching. So, in his sublime humbleness, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that all his disciples, temples and books were his Vyāsa-pūjā offering. That was what he possessed and what he could offer to his Guru Mahārāja.

Some may criticize, saying that this is a material result. But we can see that everything Śrīla Prabhupāda did was offered in perfect devotion and dedication to Kṛṣṇa. Actually, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said that we can measure the success of a Vaiṣṇava by the results of his preaching: how many devotees he has made, how many temples he has opened and how many books he has distributed.

The history of ISKCON is Śrīla Prabhupāda himself. It was he who built this house that can give shelter to the whole world. It was due to his firm devotion that thousands and thousands of people, mostly young people, changed the fate of their own lives. It was due to his great enthusiasm that these young people went all over the world, opening up new frontiers, establishing temples and distributing millions of books. The ISKCON spirit is shown in this missionary and progressive attitude, in the attack against illusion, in the devotion to Kṛṣṇa that Śrīla Prabhupāda had. Still today, and hopefully in the thousands of years to come, the members of ISKCON dedicate themselves to this brave mood, having confidence in the protection of Kṛṣṇa and are full of strength due to the strong preaching of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We should know very clearly that this ISKCON society is nothing more than an expansion of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental body. As a duty, its members should protect it very carefully so that it does not become corrupted. The true ISKCON men should spread this society to all corners of the world and should maintain it with the devotion and carefulness of someone who cultivates a flower garden.

We should know, as well, that the best way those within ISKCON can preach is through book distribution. We should prepare a big and wonderful birthday cake to be offered to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda today, and the millions of books distributed should be part of the ingredients of this cake.

As a faithful devotee worships the Ganges by offering back her own water, we should celebrate Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā by offering him the gifts obtained solely by the devotion and mercy that comes from him. All that we have was received by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Therefore, we should recognize who is the real enjoyer and proprietor and prepare the best offering possible. Giving back to Śrīla Prabhupāda this great result, we are assured that he himself will take this offering to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

This birthday cake is becoming bigger and more beautiful because it is being prepared by the best cooks and devotees. Each cook comes and adds an ingredient, increasing its sweetness and strengthening its devotion. Then this cake is offered with devotion to Śrīla Prabhupāda and we can all partake of the enjoyment of tasting it and distributing it to the whole world.

I humbly beg the mercy of all Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers so that I can also become a good cook and avoid spoiling the preparation of this cake. I wish to actually help as far as I can to make this a wonderful offering for the pleasure of Śrīla Prabhupāda and the benefit of the whole world.

*Your humble servant,*  
īśvara Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Although Śrīla Prabhupāda is always with us, I’d like to take some moments to consider what it would be like were he still with us physically.

We could envision that he’d stick to his daily routine – his translating, morning walks, giving classes, answering mail, taking massage and lunch, meeting visitors, and going out for programs. He would encourage us by sometimes smiling or joking with us or complimenting us for our efforts.

But most enlivening of all, I think, is this: He would upbraid us, rebuke us, put us down, shake us up, and pull us into line. He would smash us, crush us, pulverize our false egos. Excuses would be ridiculed and scoffed at. “Brilliant innovations” would be cut to shreds. Laziness, complacence, and foolishness would be exposed and put to rout.

His Divine Grace would cut through the tangled networks of our māyā neatly and smoothly. “Rascal,” “fool,” “useless” – these words would fly. Śrīla Prabhupāda would verbally decorate our heads with dunce caps.

The biggest leaders would get the heaviest broadsides (a sign of special mercy).

These would not be idle exercises or mere displays of ill temper. On the contrary, they would be an essential part of our training – “Disciple means discipline!” They’d be signs of affection. And they’d be expressions of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s urgent will to “get the show on the road.”

And condemnation, of course, would not be reserved for disciples. Nondevotees would come in for it roundly. Atheists, hypocrites, bluffers, cheats – nonsense exposed, boots in the face.

Lord Caitanya, it is said, was hard like a thunderbolt and soft like a rose. In the person of the Lord or His pure devotee, both sides are relishable, both sides absolutely for our good.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

My Dear Eternal Father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *ananta-koṭi daṇḍavats* at your divine feet on this glorious day.

Before beginning to answer Mahārāja Parikṣit’s questions, Śukadeva Gosvāmī first spoke the following verse (*Bhāg*. 1.2.4):

*nārāyaṇaṁ namaskṛtya*  
*naraṁ caiva narottamam*  
*devīṁ sarasvatīṁ vyāsaṁ*  
*tato jayam udīrayet*

In this verse, Śukadeva Gosvāmī first paid his obeisances to Lord Nara-Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi, then to Mother Sarasvati, and finally to his *guru*, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, who was also his father. Through this we learn the importance of offering our obeisances to our guru before beginning to speak on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Only by first receiving the blessings of the guru is one qualified to speak on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

Five thousand years ago when Śukadeva Gosvāmī spoke this verse, it was complete, as the last person praised was Vyāsadeva, the author. In the five thousand years that have elapsed since then, in this same disciplic succession, in this same *guru-paramparā*, so many other great and wonderful personalities have appeared, such as Madhvācārya, īśvara Purī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu – the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself – and recently the savior of the whole world and our eternal well-wisher, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. In this most degraded age of Kali, Guru Mahārāja, you descended from the spiritual world just to save us. Therefore, whenever I recite this verse before starting the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, I cannot help adding your name at the end of the translation to get your blessings, because of your unique achievements in bringing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to the whole world. Without paying our obeisances to you, and without becoming Prabhupāda conscious, we cannot become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

You are honored by many titles: *Jagat-guru*, the spiritual master of the whole universe; *nitya-siddha*, an eternally liberated soul, descending from the spiritual world; *patita-pāvana,* the savior of the most fallen, etc. To this list one could easily add another name, that of *mahājāna*. *Mahā* means great and *jana* means devotee of the Supreme Lord. Twelve such *mahājanas* are currently glorified throughout the universe, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, deserve to be glorified as much as they.

What is my reasoning for this understanding? Among the mahājanas Śrīla Vyāsadeva is glorified for compiling the Vedic literature. Śrīla Vyāsadeva did this our of compassion for the people of Kali-yuga because he saw that they would have very short lives and very short memories. He saw that they would have no inclination to take up spiritual practices, what to speak of taking initiation from a bonafide spiritual master – the preliminary requirement for one’s spiritual life. The people of the Kali-yuga will not be able to hear from the spiritual master just once and remember forever. For such unfortunate people, Śrīla Vyāsadeva arranged and compiled the Vedic literatures.

However, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to us you appear to have done more than that. Not only have you translated the Vedic literature into English and subsequently other languages, but you have also added most beautiful and wonderful purports. Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s disciples and grand-disciples did distribute the Vedic literatures widely, but you have distributed your translated *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and many other literary works all over the world. Not just in India and Asia, not just in Europe or America, not just in Africa or Australia, but everywhere, even the small islands in the oceans. Your books are not in Sanskrit only, but in every language in the world so that everyone can benefit. By Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s excellent effort the *Bhāgavatam* was made accessible, but only to a few. However, by your super-excellent effort the *Bhāgavatam* is now accessible to everyone. This is why we should glorify you as much as the other great *mahājanas*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have stated that this planet of Bhārata-varṣa in Kali-yuga is not a fit place for a gentleman to live in. However, it is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desire and prediction that one day His holy names will be chanted in every corner of every town and village in the world. You made it your mission in life to bring this about by building a house in which the whole world could live. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I long for that day when your dreams are realized. I long for that day when this whole planet will once again be that one house. I long for that day when all living entities of the planet will once again be one family. And I long for that day when everyone will once again be practicing their true *dharma-sanātana-dharma.*

I am old and weak, and my days in this body are running out, but there is so much work to be done, and so many souls to save. So I pray for every drop of your mercy to allow me to spend the rest of my life completely engaged in your service, not sparing even a single moment for anything else.

To my ever well-wisher,  
*From your ever-dependent son,*  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances again and again on the occasion of your 98th divine appearance. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace.

Usually, birthdays mean surprise gifts; presents and plans for celebrations are kept secret from the person whose anniversary is being celebrated. But here we are, so eager to share with you our strategies and plans, which we can hardly wait to tell you. If we were just preparing cakes and kacorīs you wouldn’t be interested either, but this is not the case. For your 100thbirth anniversary we want to offer what you value most. Devotees have started to get together in their respective countries and temples, forming committees and holding *iṣṭaoṣṭhīs*. We had fifty devotees in Māyāpura for this year’s GBC meetings to help finalize the master plan for your Centennial. Here is their conclusion, which I already hinted at in my previous offering; it is now fully focused: “We want to offer you a strong, dynamic, and united movement, with improved functioning and pleasing relations among the devotees.”

This reporting is significant, because it does not just directly deal with the festivities associated with the one-day celebration of your 1996 *mahā* Vyāsa-pūjā, but describes the beginning of a sincere endeavor by your followers to take your centennial as a challenge to achieve excellence in different fields of devotional service. I am certain that this spirit meets your expectations.

The lotus of Centennial activities is cultivated and is continuing to bloom year after year. In Māyāpura this March we reorganized it and added more petals – it now has a total of twelve. The lotus includes all three major aspects of your program for giving Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world:

1\. Spreading the holy name  
2\. Producing and distributing books  
3\. Expanding *prasādam* distribution

It also has a petal dealing with raising spiritual standards, as well as another concerned with building congregations. We also have more devotees assigned to take care of executing the strategy in fulfillment of each petal’s goal.

The “Uniting Prabhupāda’s (your) Family” spirit is growing all over the world, spreading to all the cross sections of your worldwide society. Devotees are feeling drawn back to your lotus feet and the shelter of your instructions. Here are the words of one of your disciples: “Prabhupāda is calling me and I think he is calling many of his disciples too.” The highlight of the Centennial discussions in Māyāpura were the deliberations on uniting your family by our GBC men. They expressed a strong willingness to do whatever is needed to bring as many devotees as possible back to the practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to ensure that all the members of your family feel comfortable and welcome in your house, which you built for the whole world to live in.

We are expecting several more reunions this year. We have realized that unfortunately all devotees may not be dear to us, but they are very dear to you, and your heart is broken that they are away. It is our duty to bring them back.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, 1896-1996 is the Bhaktivedanta Swami Era. While now preparing for the celebration of the century – your Centennial Celebration – we are getting ready to launch the second phase of this transcendental epoch.

During this critical period at the turn of the century, may you shower us abundantly with your causeless mercy.

I beg to remain always,  
*Your insignificant servant,*  
Lokanātha Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you.

**THE GOAL:**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, *maṅgala-ārotika* in your Māyāpura Candrodaya Mandira reminds me of our eternal relationship. Standing before Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and the Aṣṭa-Sakhīs, we remember the goal, the aim or our lives, which you have graciously given. We aspire for the pure unalloyed devotional service of Rādhā-Mādhava in the mood of the gopīs of Vṛndāvana – *gopī-bhāva*. The ideal life is in the spiritual world where Rādhā-Mādhava perform Their eternal blissful pastimes.

We see Them standing before us in a frozen moment of Their endless movement. In reality, Rādhā-Mādhava are playing sweet music and dancing ecstatically in the middle of the rāsa circle of gopīs – Tuṅgāvidyā, Citradevī, Campakalatā, Lalitā-sakhī, Viśākhā-sakhī, Indulekhā, Raṅgadevī and Sudevī – whirling and spinning around with the most astonishing and attractive steps of divine dancing. They are moving faster and faster as the tempo of the *kīrtana* increases more and more. They are surrounded by thousands of *gopīs* and *mañjarīs*, who at every moment are offering varieties of services for Rādhā-Mādhava’s pleasure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you live and serve Rādhā-Mādhava in the transcendental realm – the ideal land of pure love and absolute dedication. You have shown this as the goal of our heart and actions. Here in your presence we served you very briefly. But there in Goloka Vṛndāvana we’ll stay beside you forever serving you in your eternal service to Nayanamaṇī Mañjarī, and others in the line of Śrī Rupa Mañjarī.

We can only imagine and dream about the wonderful services you do for the pleasure of Rādhā-Mādhava. Śrīla Prabhupāda, could you be singing splendid poetic songs with sweet melodies? Could you be carrying a confidential not to Śyāma on Rādhā’s request? Could you be fanning Rādhā-Mādhava’s three-fold bending forms with a *cāmara* whisk? Could you be bringing cool, clean Yamunā water in a bejeweled pot for Rādhikā’s morning bath?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said that Māyāpura is your place of worship. But whom are you worshiping here? We begin every day by glorifying Rādhā-Mādhava and meditating on the goal of our lives: to worship Rādhā-Mādhava in Vṛndāvana, following the mood and manner of the Vraja *gopīs*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, how do you worship Rādhā-Mādhava in Māyāpura?

The way you worship Rādhā-Mādhava in Māyāpura is most significant and instructive. You serve Them here by worshiping Their combined form as Śrī Māyāpuracandra, the most merciful moon shining over Māyāpura and the world. You don’t worship Lord Gaurāṅga by serving Him betel, *guñjā-mālā* or a *cāmara*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you worship Śrī Māyāpuracandra by offering Him plates full of books praising Rādhā-Mādhava and the pure devotion of the Vraja *gopīs*. You offer Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu plates full of conditioned souls turned devotees, surrendered to Kṛṣṇa in *bhakti*. For Mahāprabhu’s *rāja-bhoga*, you make your ultimate offering – the whole world on the golden plate of devotional surrender. With arms raised high everyone everywhere is chanting, “Nitāi-Gaurāṅga! Jay Śacinandana! Hari bol! Hare Kṛṣṇa! Hare Rāma!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so kindly brought us to your place of worship. You gave us the service of Rādhā-Mādhava and the Aṣṭa-sakhīs. Now we beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us assist you in your worship of Lord Caitanya in Māyāpura. Please engage us in Mahāprabhu’s *mādhurya-līlā* of offering all the conditioned souls into the *rāsa* dance of pure devotional service to Rādhā-Mādhava.

**OUR REALITY:**

In a flash the huge curtain shuts, concealing the gorgeous forms of Rādhā-Mādhava and the *gopīs*. Now They will enjoy intimate pastimes with Their beloved servants. Turning to our left we meet reality: the ghastly ferocious form of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva. It’s time for Nṛsiṁha’s *maṅgala-ārotika*. He’s not dancing. There are flames burning everywhere, and it’s black all around. There are no colorful turbans, *sārīs*, and peacocks. He’s not playing a sweet, gentle flute. He’s holding a club, sword, *cakra* and razor-sharp nails. Lord Nṛsiṁha stands firm, immovable, ready for war. He’s poised to kill the hedonist Hiraṇyakaśipu.

Every day Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva confronts Hiraṇyakaśipu in the form of our demoniac material desires. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us Nṛsiṁha-deva to help us fight our war. Let Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva rip out the terrible *anarthas* and *aparādhas* from our hearts.

With folded hands, the peaceful Prahlāda stands fearlessly on Nṛsiṁha-deva’s right side offering prayers. In our day-to-day fight against *māyā* we take strength from Prahlāda Mahārāja. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you embodied Prahlāda’s fearlessness and showed it by your preaching. Prahlāda was your favorite devotee. You gave many classes on the teachings of Prahlāda Mahārāja.

All along you have told us to pray to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva for two things: to protect ISKCON, and to protect us individually from the demons within and without. By your mercy we see that our reality leads to the ideal. The sober, practical, down-to-earth struggle to root out material desires and in the desirable goal of Rādhā-Mādhava’s eternal service in Vṛndāvana.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura explains this nicely in his Navadvīpa Bhāva-taraṅga:

“In my heart reside fear, anger, envy, illusion, intoxication, duplicity, insincerity and the desire for fame. I will pray at the feet of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva to purify my heart, and give me the desire to serve Kṛṣṇa... When will Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva become pleased to mercifully speak to me, a worthless fool, and remove my fears?

”Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva will say, ‘Dear child, stay here happily in Gaura-dhama. Worship Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and develop attraction for the holy name. By the mercy of My devotees, all obstacles are transcended. With a purified heart just worship Rādhā-Mādhava, the abodes of sweet nectar.’

”After saying this, when will Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva joyfully place His feet upon my head? Instantly, by His mercy, I will show symptoms of ecstatic love for Rādhā-Mādhava, and I will roll on the ground by the door of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva’s temple.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is our wish and hope against hope, that by your mercy we will someday serve you and serve Rādhā-Mādhava in the blissful land of Vraja. Of course, it’s a fact that our reality keeps us millennia away from such a goal of pure devotional service.

From the beginning, you gave us both the gorgeous form of Rādhā-Mādhava and the ghastly form of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva. However, we finally see how They are compatible and intimately connected. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we continually pray to you for the fearlessness of Prahlāda Mahārāja in approaching Lord Nṛsiṁha for his blessings and divine protection.

The taste of bitter medicine brings the sweet taste of good health. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us the tonic of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva’s mercy to vanquish the disease of material desires. Soon we will taste the sweet nectar of perfect health: eternally serving you in your eternal service to the lotus feet of Rādhā-Mādhava in Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Nava-yogendra Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus-like feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who is very kind upon the fallen souls like myself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your divine appearance day, and yesterday was the appearance day of your Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Without your appearance how could it have been possible to understand the cause of all causes Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa?

You did not believe in welfare work. You used to say that this welfare work is just like saving the coat of a drowning man. You did the real welfare work by saving our souls. Śrīla Prabhupāda, without you and your great movement, ISKCON, we would have certainly gone to hell. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so great that we cannot imagine your greatness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me *śakti* so that I can be a little instrumental in your great ISKCON. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only your ISKCON can save the whole world. There is no doubt of this. Kindly give me the intelligence and strength so that I can become a good devotee.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can I glorify you? To glorify you is just like showing a lamp to the sun. Kindly forgive my offenses and allow me to serve you eternally. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda: Simply by loving Kṛṣṇa. It is very easy. You love here, there, so many. You just try to love Kṛṣṇa and stay in transcendental state. Love is there, but we are misplacing our love. That is our folly. (Śrīla Prabhupāda Lecture, Montreal July 6, 1968)

In this way our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is very simple, focusing our attention on Śrī Kṛṣṇa and giving our love to Him. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the *ācārya* teaching how to engage everything in the loving service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and not getting entangled unnecessarily in material affairs. Therefore as it states in the *Mahābhārata: dharmasya tattvaṁ nihitaṁ guhāyāṁ mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.* Let us follow in your lotus footprints, develop our Kṛṣṇa consciousness and preach your message purely and effectively to others.

Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are the life and soul of all the Vaiṣṇava devotees. Let me perform Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* under the shelter of your lotus feet. Let me participate in your plan of flooding the whole universe with massive book distribution. Otherwise, what is the meaning of my *sannyāsa* order? *Sannyāsī* means to preach, and the best way to preach is not by simple talks, but by your book distribution.

You already have a plan to flood the entire universe with your books. In many parts of the world, the devotees are selling huge quantities of books, however, I am not participating in this book distribution, so I am frustrated. What can I do? I have no qualification. All that there is left for me to do is to cry and to try my best to become a recipient of your mercy. Everything is being done by you and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. If it is your desire, certainly it will happen.

There are some parts of the world that book distribution is dormant. I am sure you are not satisfied with it, but, by your desire, at any time the fire will be lit, and you will give opportunity to many, many devotees to participate in the book distribution. Please, put me on your list.

I am begging for your mercy. I need to perform devotional service for the welfare of all. Please, let me be an instrument in your hands. Let me fulfill your desire to inundate this universe with intensive book distribution, otherwise my life will be a great waste of time.

*I hope you are well. Your servant.*  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacinandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Glorification**

You always live in Kṛṣṇa,  
but selflessly you came to us  
as a kind friend to all living entities – to save even the most fallen.  
No one can understand your deep feelings for your Lord.

You are always engaged in devotional service  
as the most capable and enthusiastic preacher in this world,  
and in the “other world” in ways totally incomprehensible  
for a lost soul like me.

You are totally above *māyā’s* ways,  
pure, expert, without cares and detached.  
But like the most merciful father,  
you lovingly care for all your disciples  
and pray for all conditioned souls.

O my master,  
in this life I have taken the most fortunate birth  
because by the inconceivable arrangement of the Lord  
I could meet you.  
By your causeless mercy,  
please be so kind to give me the necessary strength  
to make full use of being accepted by you as your humble servant.

**A Lamentation**

How I wish  
to race back in time  
to be with you and assist you  
during your early preaching in Jhansi,  
your silent life in Vṛndāvana,  
your sea voyage (I have grown up by the sea  
and am accustomed to high waves),  
your early preaching in New York,  
the program in the Avalon Ballroom.

I would chant with you  
both *japa* and *kīrtana*.  
I would sit and receive *prasādam* from you.  
I would sneak into your room,  
just to sit in a corner and hear and hear and hear.  
I would unnoticed by others get into the line  
to be embraced by you  
when you said goodbye to the devotees  
at the airport in New York,  
and feel very blissful.

You will say,  
”but what would you have done for me?”  
knowing me to be clumsy and simple.  
But I hope I can assure you  
I would have learned so many things to do,  
just by desiring to serve you.  
And seeing me so enthusiastic,  
you would have smiled  
to see my sincere desire to please you.

But here I am  
not even a shadow of your bona fide disciple  
separated by the corridors of time  
sadly counting the jewels of my brief personal encounters  
with the Lord’s dearmost servant  
over and over.

”Don’t lament,”  
I hear you say.  
”You can surely have my guidance and association even now.  
Simply be a genuine disciple.  
Do something substantial for this movement.  
Work together in peace with your God brothers.  
Travel and preach.  
Become a servant of the holy name.  
Be compassionate to the fallen souls.  
And if you want to be with me,  
chant offenselessly.  
Read my books and follow the instructions.”

I have faith in what you say,  
because I have seen what is for me your greatest magic:  
your ongoing accessibility.

**My Humble Request**

You know it best,  
I desperately need your mercy.

Please never hesitate to shake me  
from the bonds of complacency,  
independent thought and action,  
thinking me too strong-headed and proud.

Please make me more humble than the straw in the street  
and give me the strength to bear tests and troubles.  
Please give me the intelligence and sincerity  
to properly honor all living entities.  
At the same time please uproot from the inner chambers of my heart  
my deadly addiction to receive praise and honor.  
Then only I can chant offenselessly.

And please teach me,  
*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto*  
*mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*

I have the uneasy feeling  
that I’m asking for too much  
because I have not done anything  
to deserve these gifts.  
But I have heard and watched  
how you are causelessly merciful to your disciples.  
Therefore, I think it will be sufficient  
if you forever give a place  
in the cooling shade of your lotus feet,  
where I can stay unnoticed,  
and take whatever mercy comes my way  
from you.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Śacinandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Gosvāmī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I want to be enthusiastic and patient in my relationship with you. I know you are still guiding my life. This is a transcendental fact as important as any other spiritual facts. You are my authority, and our relationship is eternal. I am left in this world, and you have gone back to your eternal form in the spiritual world. Now I have to pass the test of time. If I don’t have faith in your presence in my life, then where is my faith in Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can’t understand how we will meet again at the time of death. How will I cross from this life to the next? I want the body of a devotee so I can serve you again. I want to *feel* that desire in my heart. I want it to be my main objective.

You sometimes say in your books that we should prepare ourselves for the time of death by practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. You also said, “I don’t know Kṛṣṇa, but I know my Guru Mahārāja.” Therefore, the more I can clear away impediments, the more I will want to be with you. Then I will remember you at the end.

At death I want to be clear. I don’t want to gamble. I know that my relationship with you is deep and it goes back to my youth. It was you who reached out to me and saved me from material life. It’s you who continues to save me. I live in your movement, sustained by you. That is my identity and I want it to be strong.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I took a walk this morning just before dawn. The road was dark and became like a symbol for me – I could barely see. My walking stick clicked against the stones and I thought, “this is the way a blind man walks, guiding himself with his stick. But I’m not totally blind. Śrīla Prabhupāda has shown me the goal and given me the tools to attain it.” You have also encouraged me to be patient. I don’t have to stimulate my Kṛṣṇa consciousness artificially or try to prematurely reach a stage for which I am not ready. I want to attain the goal of life in the way you want me to have it. I want to learn of that goal solely from your books. You say at the end of the *Madhya-līlā* that just as a person must eat grains to live, so we must read all your books – or we will fall down.

Is my desire for exclusive devotion to you unrealistic? Well, what can I say? I desire it. Therefore my spiritual life should be focused on serving you and praying to you. Kṛṣṇa awards desires. If my real desire is to be with you in my next life, then Kṛṣṇa will grant that. The dark road is symbolic. As dawn rises I am better able to see the road, better able to see my way through life to the next, with you.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Krishna Swami

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are the savior of the most fallen, the most merciful. Without you my destiny would have had been completely obscure, unfortunate and misdirected. I would have ended up in some most unfortunate situation. By your grace my future is bright.

In this endeavor there is no loss or diminution, and a little advancement on this path can protect one from the most dangerous type of fear. This is the statement from *Bhagavad-gītā* as we learn it from you. Mercifully, you have given so many conditioned souls a chance to make some advancement, thus saving them from the most dangerous type of fear. Everyone might not become pure devotees immediately and thus go back home to Godhead. But their and our path back home has started – we are on the way. Your desire was that we do not waste our time but make it back in this lifetime. Yet if we do not fully succeed in this lifetime, we will be able to continue our spiritual progress in future lifetimes.

In Bangalore, India last year, there was a dog on the temple grounds. It was one of those fortunate dogs who got the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas. I was told that this dog joined in with the Ratha-yātrā and followed the procession back to our temple. Once there, it refused to leave. In spite of all attempts to chase it away it remained by the temple, and the devotees concluded that that dog was meant to be there. So this dog guards the temple and occasionally drinks the water which washes the feet of the Vaiṣṇavas before they enter the temple. Sometimes he offers obeisances to the devotees. The first time I noticed the dog was when I was giving *Bhāgavatam* lecture. I could see that the dog was lying just in front of the entrance of the temple. We could speculate that it was listening to what was said. Who knows? Nevertheless, it si certainly a very fortunate soul in a dog’s body. That dog is becoming fortunate by Your Divine Grace.

Your grace is that you created so many nice Vaiṣṇavas who in their turn show such compassion even on the most fallen, even on a dog. You showed your compassion upon us, but were we much better than that dog? That dog has found the shelter of the temple and of the devotees, and similarly we have found the shelter of your lotus feet in the bigger temple of ISKCON in the association of your followers.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in his humility prayed to the Lord that if he had to take another birth in this world, that it should be in the house of a devotee of the Lord, even if it was in the form of an insect. Better as an insect in the house of a devotee than as a nondevotee Lord Brahmā.

You have made such a nice arrangement that we first get the opportunity to become devotees in this life. If we should fail and have to take another birth, there are many merciful devotees in whose homes we can take birth in one capacity or another. My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most merciful. I beg to remain your servant forever.

*Your servant,*  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is always difficult to find the words to describe your transcendental qualities. You are so great that you did the greatest miracle: walking through the world, you have given life to thousands of living entities. To revive a person from death is a great miracle, but you have saved thousands of living entities from death. You gave new life to thousands, and even after you interrupted your manifest pastimes, in your non-manifest pastimes, you are performing the same miracle everyday.

This material world is for ignorant people. Everybody is running around, working hard as an ass to try to enjoy this phantasmagoria of different forms. These forms are the illusory part of the creation. They are not real, they are temporary. Ignorant people cannot see anything beyond material form. Actually the real world is far beyond the material forms. The materialist can see only matter and material forms. Actually, reality is consciousness. The living entities are consciousness, whereas inanimate things like trees, rivers, mountains, and air are permeated by consciousness. The materialist is blind to this world of consciousness. For this reason, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* says that while these people are talking, working and moving around, they are actually dead. Only when the living entities awaken to the world of consciousness are they actually living. This is your great miracle. Ignorant people consider miracles to be curing the material body of giving new life to the material body, but actually those are minor miracles. To give life to dead consciousness, that is the major miracle.

Through your transcendental words, thousands of persons with dead consciousness have been revived. Through your books, you are continuing to perform thousands and thousands of miracles. The first miracle is followed by other miracles because there are higher states of consciousness. You continue to guide us to higher states of consciousness and deeper realizations. When we read your books or just look at your picture, you are there, pushing us to go deeper, to go higher. Pure love of Godhead is the goal. You are pushing us toward this goal. You desire that we become pure devotees of the Lord, and you have given us everything for this. But to achieve this requires profound realization and purification, greater humility, mercy and compassion. We should become so compassionate as to feel the pains of others as our own, so sensitive that we care for everybody, and we do not want to hurt anybody. We should become so deep in our consciousness as to bring the chanting out from the depth of our hearts. You are always there pushing and expecting us to become more and more Kṛṣṇa conscious. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to push, and on this auspicious day, I pray, “Please, my lord and master, do not let me down. I have no other shelter than your lotus feet. Without the shelter of your lotus feet, my life has no meaning.”

All glories to you. All glories to you. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
*Your unworthy servant,*  
Somaka Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most prostrated humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet.

Thank you thousands of times for coming to America to start your ISKCON society. By this act of pure mercy I have been given a chance to find relief from the bondage of my fruitive work. Without your mercy I was certainly doomed to suffer in the quagmire of material life due to my innumerable desires. These desires, like unrelenting, merciless enemies, always haunted me, even in my dreams. But without your kind appearance, this drab life would have been meaningless. Although I was hardhearted, by your merciful instructions my heart began to soften. Who would have thought it was possible? I was so cynical, so proud, but you were expert in affairs of the heart, and, in time, made mine ready to receive the *bhakti-latā-bīja*.

”Miracle done by Your Divine Grace.” This is truly the “priceless gift” that makes our lives more and more full as we try in our feeble way to repay it. So my one request then, is to be allowed to continue in my small, insignificant way in this attempt to repay you for all that I am indebted to you for.

*Your humble servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Varsānā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

Of all the wonderful and holy occasions  
which have occurred in the past,  
or await us in the future,  
the day you descended to this fortunate Earth  
is the most respected and blessed of all.  
On this day I so clearly remember how...  
From the mist on the shoreline of *māña’s* kingdom,  
where devotees had never ventured,  
you arose like the sun on the Eastern horizon  
to lead us across a most dangerous ocean.

After plying through seas of adversity  
and overcoming every obstacle,  
defying even the notice of death,  
you so kindly came to our rescue.

Though the sky was dark and foreboding  
and the atmosphere thick with doubt,  
you came to deliver the message of Godhead  
with perfect faith in your master’s words.

You told us about our homeland  
and the treasure awaiting our claim;  
you pointed the way to our highest attainment,  
where our dreams would be fulfilled.

You allowed us to be trusting;  
you taught us to be true,  
to be steady, though the path is long,  
to dissipate grief with happy song.

You gave us life, and in so doing,  
fulfilled our Lord’s command,  
that the holy name would spread  
to every village in the land.

You enchanted our minds and awakened our devotion  
by your pure presentation, full of sweetness and charm,  
softening our hearts with the warmth of your love,  
you planted seeds of *bhakti* which sprouted in time.

You are the embodiment of mercy,  
fully empowered by the Lord  
to expand the mission which He began,  
and in this way, you are immortal.

We never believed you might someday leave us,  
but then it appeared as if you did,  
the whole world again seemed empty  
and time itself was standing still.

On that terrible night, I remember  
how the Earth appeared to tremble,  
the clouds grew dark with mystery  
and the moon could not shine through.

And for those who had no purpose  
besides the service of our *guru,*  
our fate again seemed hopeless  
and there was no one we could turn to.

But then, like the wonder of wonders,  
as if you spoke to us again,  
the truth to be learned form your final pastime  
was beautifully revealed.

”He reasons ill, who thinks that Vaiṣṇava dies  
when thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living, they try  
to spread the holy name around.”

Yes, the mercy of a pure devotee  
can never be confined  
by time or place or death itself,  
but will surely ever shine,  
growing brighter and brighter as years go by,  
ever increasing in strength and clarity,  
living and breathing eternal life  
into every sincere devotee.

Thus your presence remains before us,  
and your glory is still to be seen  
as the blossoming lotus of fortune  
in the hearts of your devotees.

And so we continue very faithfully  
with the mission you began,  
waiting anxiously for that happy day  
when the sun will shine brighter than ever before  
and illuminate our hearts  
to the innermost chamber.

Then, from the portals of the Blessed Realm  
from where you have come and again returned,  
we will see you beckoning, drawing us onward  
to come and join you in Kṛṣṇa-*kīrtana*.

As we turn and look behind us now  
on the joys and sorrows past,  
it is clear that all is mercy  
meant to teach us proper faith.

All the trials, ordeals and victories  
through which you boldly lead us,  
portray the compassion of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees  
and dispel our mortal fears.

And so, like budding flowers  
we are cherishing that remembrance  
of a very special morning  
and the most glorious exalted day,  
when you rose into our vision,  
like the golden morning sun,  
hailing form the land of Gauḍa  
to brighten our horizon.

And now, whether in darkness  
or in sunlight, bright and clear,  
your words are ever with us  
and your presence is so near.

May our prayer on this special day  
when you appeared within our world  
be that we live in your instructions  
and that you will be there with us.

*From the servant of your servants,*  
Varsānā Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**Seeking Service is the Answer**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Do you have any service for me? Outside the temple room windows from where I sit is the strange, dark world of Kali-yuga. People out there are ignorant of Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. If you go to the streets of downtown Vancouver here in Canada and loudly shout “Gaurāṅga!” as they do in Māyāpura, people would think you were crazy. But in reality these fallen souls are the real crazy fellows of this world. They are destroying the air, the water and the animals. They have very few moral values, and they think themselves very advanced in civilization, although they do not know that the goal of life is to worship Śrī Gaurahari.

These people do not know that the *saṅkīrtana* movement is growing stronger and stronger around the world. Some of them think that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is waning here in North America. But they do not realize that Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement is coming to get them. I have seen it. It’s growing. It’s getting stronger. It’s getting bigger. You started a wildfire, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and they cannot stomp it out. It’s out of control.

But when will it hit here in a big way? Is there some way that I can help? Is there something I can do to assist? Is there some engagement for this insignificant soul in this process of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement? Am I to be left here twiddling my fingers, initiating a disciple here and there, going on *hari-nāma*, giving a few classes and looking out the temple room windows?

More than half of my life I have been chanting sixteen rounds a day. I joined your movement when I was twenty years old. Now I’m well in my forties. I’ve been a *sannyāsī* for more than half of the years that I’ve been a devotee. Everything is more than half. My hairs are getting gray. I see wrinkles on my hands. But even as I type these words, the holy names of the *mahāmantra* resound in the temple room as the devotees chant their *japa* and I rush this off to make the 1944 Vyāsa-pūjā book in time.

So, I am sheltered. I am protected by the spiritual environment of the temple and the devotees. I came back from Māyāpura only a few weeks ago, yet I am still in Māyāpura. I never left there, although I took an airplane and flew over here. How fortunate! Now, perhaps I need to go down the streets of Vancouver and shout “Gaurāṅga!” and see what happens. But surely I could get a bigger chance to do something significant for your movement. Isn’t their anything I can do for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Or is that a pretty big service in itself?

I just initiated a disciple and sent him immediately on book distribution. I told him: “Śrīla Prabhupāda is very pleased when he sees the devotees distributing books.” So off he went traveling with other *brahmacārīs* in the *saṅkīrtana* army. Maybe you can bless those great book distributors. They are your real devotees, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I am their servant. Maybe creating book distributors is the best way to please you. Can you please speak to me through your servants and tell me what you want me to do?

Once you wrote to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your spiritual master, and said you were a householder and not fortunate to live so close to the guru as some of your other *sannyāsī* Godbrothers. Therefore you asked for some service. Your Guru Mahārāja replied that you should preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English language. It would be good for you and for those whom you preach to. I’m one of those who were affected by your following that instruction. But now I’d like to ask you the same question. Is there any service for Vipramukhya Swami? You know me better than anyone else. Do you have any engagement for me? Or maybe I should just try to do better what I’m already supposed do be doing.

*śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam (Bg. 2.7)*

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Agartala

Most Worshipable, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept our very humble yet most unqualified obeisances at the dust particles of your two divine lotus feet. All glories to your Divine Grace!

Our limited senses make us feel very crippled and unqualified to fathom the mystery of your unlimited glories, especially the way Your Divine Grace inspired the most fallen and the most undeserving to aspire to achieve the supreme destination.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness (ISKCON) to inspire us to attain the purest consciousness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. At first you showed us the connection to Śrī Kṛṣṇa through *paramparā,* in other words, the *sambandha-jñāna*. Then you described how attractive it is to act upon that sublime eternal relation by quoting Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī:

*yuvatīnāṁ yathā yūni yūnām ca yuvatau yathā*  
*mano me ‘bhiramate tadvat mano me ramatāṁ tvayi*

**”As the minds of young girls take pleasure in thinking of a young boy, and those of young boys take pleasure in thinking of a young girl, may my mind take pleasure in You, O my Lord.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you emphasized this eagerness, being in relation with Kṛṣṇa, as the highest state of consciousness to be attained. And you also taught us how to develop that eagerness, or greed (*laulyam*), to attain full, unalloyed Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Only from your teachings have we learned that our attachment will develop where our investment is, and that the nature of our attachment determines our destination.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you established ISKCON to encourage us to engage (invest) our whole life in the unalloyed service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and to realize our supreme destination. And why not, as Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself declares in the *Bhagavad-gītā:*

*gatir bharitā prabhuḥ sākṣī*  
*nivāsaḥ śaraṇaṁ suhṛt*  
*prabhavaḥ pralayaḥ sthānaṁ*  
*nidhānaṁ bījam avyayam*

**”I am the goal, the sustainer, the master, the witness, the abode, the refuge and the most dear friend. I am the creation and the annihilation, the basis of everything, the resting place and the eternal seed.” (*****Bg.*** **9.18)**

**”When one becomes fortunate to evolve to this realization, his attachment to be always in love with Śrī Kṛṣṇa becomes unflinching, and he looks for nothing more under any circumstances.” (*****Bg.*** **6.22)**

But how is this great fortune possible for the downtrodden conditioned souls? Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have revealed the secret of success for us. The only way to become completely free from material contamination is to take full, unmotivated shelter of the dust of your lotus feet. As confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*rahūgaṇaitat tapasā na yati*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na chandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda rajo ‘bhiṣekam*

**”Mahārāja Jaḍa Bharata says, ‘My dear King Rahūgana, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot attain the Absolute Truth... There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee.” (*****Bhāg*****. 5.12.12)**

The fervor all over the world to glorify your Divine Grace properly during the upcoming Centennial Celebration is very exciting in many ways. Fortunately, we are guided by your most intimate and pure associates and servants. Therefore we have the firm faith that by remaining loyal to their instructions, we will have the fortune to be an instrument in your glorification for your pleasure.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, to express our mood of prayer and gratitude to your Divine Grace, the abode of unlimited compassion, we are reminded of this intimation of Vṛtrāsura to the Supreme Lord:

*ahaṁ hare tave pādaika-mūla*  
*dāsānudāso bhavitāsmi bhūyaḥ*  
*manaḥ smaretāsu-pater guṇāṁs te*  
*gṛṇīta vāk karma karotu kāyaḥ*

**”O my Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, will I again be able to be a servant of Your eternal servants who find shelter only at Your lotus feet? O Lord of my life, may I again become their servant so that my mind may always think of Your transcendental attributes, my words always glorify those attributes, and my body always engage in the loving service of Your Lordship?” (*****Bhāg*****. 6.11.24)**

*With deep love for you, aspiring to remain loyal to your dear servant,*  
ISKCON Agartala, Tripura, India.

## Almviks Gard

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We would like to build a house for you to live in and we with you, here at this God-forgotten place. To build a house of bricks, stone and wood is quickly done.

To build a house for everyone to live in seems difficult. But let us at least build a house for you to live in and for us to worship you. We contemplate a house where the cornerstones will be the four regulative principles: no meat eating, illicit sex, intoxication or gambling. The walls could be seen as mercy, austerity, truthfulness and cleanliness; the entrance steps - *śraddhā,* the entrance door – *sādhu-saṅga,* the activities inside – *bhajana-kriyā,* the shower room *– anartha-nivṛtti*, the kitchen – *niṣṭhā,* the *prasādam* room – *ruci,* the temple room and altar – *premā*

A house like this that shines from within with a golden hue of spiritual effulgence will attract the attention of all the outsiders passing by the house. They will be attracted like flies to fire in the night. In the spiritual darkness of Kali-yuga, the conditioned souls will be attracted to the effulgence emanating from the interior of that house.

As people enter this house to worship you and participate in the Hare Kṛṣṇa kīrtana there, the house will unlimitedly expand to fit all those who enter through the proper entrance.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is just a contemplated house, but we hope to be able to successfully build something like this in our rural farm community for your pleasure. Once it is built, we hope to stay with you in it forever. Actually you are the builder, you are the director, you have already built it, we are just trying to implement your instructions.

Thank you for saving us from the ocean of faults – the age of Kali. We beg to remain your humble servants.

*Your servants, disciples, granddisciples and others at New Caitanya Candrodaya Mandir/ Almviks Gard, Sweden.*

## Amsterdam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your transcendental ISKCON movement.

Since the devotees came to Amsterdam to open the first temple back in 1970, our *yātrā* has gone through a lot of changes. We have moved from Frissenstein in the Beilmer to Betanienstraat, then to Herengracht, where you came personally to install Lord Jagannātha, the Lord of the universe. After some time, we went to Keizersgracht, followed by Ruysdaelkade, and at present we are situated in the Van Hilligaertstraat. All these temples are in the memory of many devotees who have joined in Amsterdam and now serve in ISKCON temples all over the world.

Later, we had to leave the big building on Ruysdaelkade three years ago, The Amsterdam temple suffered some serious setbacks, and for a while the future of ISKCON, as you desired to be, became obscured. Unfortunately, despite everyone’s best intentions, over the past decade the Benelux *yātrā* has also been affected by several deviant philosophies which passed on as Kṛṣṇa consciousness, at least for some time. As a tail-end of the *ṛtvik*-affair, yet another innovation entered the minds of some: “Preaching with shaved heads and *dhotīs* was all right for the hippie era of the Sixties and Seventies, but we are now living in the Nineties. If we want to appeal to the more sophisticated class of society, we have to employ different ways to attract and impress people. We have to become more practical. The present ISKCON leaders do not understand what this means. But where others have failed, we, the Hare Kṛṣṇa Center in Amsterdam, will give it a try.”

While systematically undermining faith in the ISKCON authorities, they caused the temple room to look more and more like an office, the altar appeared so small that it was almost invisible, *maṅgalāratika* became a rare occurrence, and for coming to the Sunday Feast an invitation was required. What was the result, one may ask, of those far-reaching, mature management strategies, surpassing the vision of the pure devotee? Practically speaking, there was a decrease in the distribution of mercy, followed by death and fall down. Who needs stronger evidence of what dangers can occur when we start to act independently from the instructions of our founder*-ācārya*? It was as if they thought that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a *tri-kāla-jña* pure devotee of the Supreme Lord, who can know past, present and future by the grace of Kṛṣṇa, and who can enter into the hearts of all, were somehow not able to give us the right formula for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Why else would you, who are humility personified, have stated that your books would become “the law books for the next ten thousand years” (instead of ten)? Every page of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* glorifies the chanting of the holy name as the only means of deliverance in the age of Kali. You more than anyone else were thinking of novel ways of how to inject Kṛṣṇa consciousness into society at large. Being firmly fixed in this conclusion of the *śāstra*, you never stopped emphasizing the importance of chanting sixteen rounds, following the rules and regulations – especially attending the morning program – if we wanted to have any strength for preaching and influencing others.

But enough of this dark page, memories of which only cause you pain, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let us bring you some good tidings of this auspicious day of your descent into this material world. Fortunately, thanks to the prayers and vigilance of your faithful sons and followers, and to your protection, that difficult time has passed. Under the inspiring guidance of your GBC men, His Holiness Suhotra Mahārāja and His Grace Madhusevita Prabhu, as well as our respective spiritual masters, we are going forward, trying to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you showed us, through *hari-nāma*, Sunday Love Feasts, book distribution and *nāma-haṭṭa* programs. Shaved heads, *dhotīs*, incense – everything is there again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We even have a storefront temple! Let others call us fanatics or fundamentalists, we do not mind. Although it may seem that we are only a small group of individuals, many of whom come and go, by the grace of our authorities we have the faith that by simply following in your footsteps, we will spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the most effective way possible. If others have a different idea of how to do it, let them try it the way they think best, and we will, as you said, “judge the plan by its result.” But we are confident that in the meantime, your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will imperceptibly spread rays of mercy over the liven of many fallen souls in Amsterdam.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, many nice things have happened since last year. *Saṅkīrtana* has increased from 600 points to over 3000 points. Also about twenty *bhaktas* have joined here, and some of them have gone to Rāḍhadeśa to join the *bhakta* program. The amount of guests visiting your Sunday Love Feasts has increased from twenty to between sixty and eighty guests. And they love it! Everyone takes turns chanting in the microphone during *kīrtana*, even the children. Indeed, the temple room is becoming too small to fit everyone in. With the help of our congregation we have renovated the temple room very nicely, so it looks like a real temple, which attracts and inspires both the devotees and the guests. For many, the temple has become an oasis in a crazy city. And they really feel themselves at home.

But it is you who built the house in which the whole world can live. Without your help, how would it be possible for us to preach here? Remembering all the austerities you underwent helps us endure difficult times. You came all alone to the West in your old age. You suffered two heart attacks during the journey, and almost left your body in the middle of the ocean. Your *Bhagavad-gītā* manuscript was stolen. Finding a proper place to open a temple was a great struggle for you. In comparison to your sacrifices, our problems are very tiny. You as our grandfather are very, very merciful to us because we are your grandchildren, who are trying just a little bit to preach under adverse circumstances. That which is ordinarily very difficult becomes easy when we simply remember you.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious day we all bow down before you and we earnestly pray to you to give us all the strength and conviction required to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Dutch speaking part of the world. We need your mercy every step of the way. All glories to Your Divine, All-compassionate Grace!

*Your humble servants in ISKCON Amsterdam*.

## Atlanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. With all humility I fall at your lotus feet and beg you to please be merciful to your poor disciple. My only hope is to somehow become serious about carrying on the work of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you have mapped it out for your followers. You have given us all the necessary tools and weapons in the form of your books and instructions. You have displayed before us the perfect example of a pure Vaiṣṇava, in this way your example will always serve as a reference point for any attempt to establish what it means to be a devotee of Kṛṣṇa. What you have done in order to establish this movement and mission is unparalleled in the history of all Vaiṣṇava followers of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. I find it completely amazing that we, who are simply products of Kali-yuga societies, were allowed to glimpse your form and to subsequently attempt to take up your orders as if we know what it means to be a disciple and devotee of Kṛṣṇa. I think it must be your mercy upon us that we have the inspiration to make the effort to surrender even though our personal desire is to continue in this vicious cycle of illusion.

You were able to penetrate our dense ignorance with your sweet vibration and touch the very soul of our existence. The light you brought into our lives still shines bright even though we continue to struggle with material distractions practically on a daily basis. Somehow we feel that if we can simply stay on the track of strictly following your instructions, then everything will work out. It must be that this positive thought is one of the special gifts that you left for us, because it seems that many of us are realizing this more and more each day.

With each passing day it becomes clear that this body is fading away, just as you explained in your purports. Therefore the realization that there is no shelter in this world has become very solid in my mind. With your mercy I think that I can make the full transition from this state of neophyte consciousness to a more secure level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Again it must be your special gift that makes me feel this way even though I know how strong my attachments are to this material world.

So I simply pray to you on this auspicious day of your appearance that you continue to shine your mercy down upon me and all of your disciples and followers, so that we might be successful in this effort to get back to the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your humble servants at New Pāṇihāṭi Dhāma, Atlanta ISKCON.*

## Austria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Lord Caitanya’s saṅkīrtana mission.

It is a Wednesday evening in Vienna. People are rushing to catch a train, bus or taxi to their home. It seems an ordinary evening, yet in one quiet side street a rather unusual conversation between a pedestrian and a young man, holding some books, is taking place.

Pedestrian: “But I don’t need any books. I’ve got so many at home. I have no time and I never read!”

Young man: “Yes, I know but these are special books. I’ll give you a small one. It is very interesting. You will see. Just give a donation for the printing and the paper.”

Kṛpā is:

...why Lord Caitanya appeared,  
...the principle by which ISKCON spreads,  
...what holds us in spiritual life,  
what pushes us forwards,  
...the way to attain perfection,  
...what we are made of and of which Your Divine Grace is the personification.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg You, please give us a drop of Your mercy and also the ability to accept it.

*Your young and foolish grandchildren of the Austrian Yatra*.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

You propelled your ISKCON movement into the depths of Kali’s domain and with such velocity that during those early precious few years ISKCON gained a foothold in practically all the countries of the world. At last Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s storehouse of love of God would be distributed in mass, thus fulfilling the desire of the Lord and all the previous *ācāryas*.

You properly understood and served the instructions of His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, that most merciful personality, who, along with Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, had once again give credibility to Vaiṣṇavism and the teachings of the six Gosvāmīs, thus defeating and pushing aside all deviations.

Carefully, with great love and compassion, you nurtured your young Western disciples, taking them step-by-step into their devotional lives. Only when they had properly understood the previous lesson were you prepared to take them further. The lessons to learn were profound, testing the depths of their sincerity, breaking through the thin veneer of superficiality and entering into the unfathomed regions of their hearts.
