You made this very clear right from the early days of ISKCON when you would often spend hours in the kitchen demonstrating the culinary skills you picked up from your mother, Śrīmatī Rājānī’s – Spinach and Pea Samosas, spicy Kachori, Gulabjamuns, Sandesh, Chapatis (made without a rolling pin), Laglus and we can’t forget your “Simply Wonderfuls.” Everything you cooked was as if Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī Herself had cooked – nectar! Oh! If only we could have had the privilege of tasting the delicious *prasādam* you cooked. Like a true devotee of Rādhārāṇī, you loved cooking for Kṛṣṇa and this love was shown in the exquisitely succulent taste of your *prasādam*. However, not only were you inclined to cook for the Lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but you were also just as enthusiastic to distribute Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*.

In a lecture at a Christian Monastery on April 16, 1972 you explain: “So that is our program, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and distributing *prasādam*. We are distributing... When I started this movement, I was cooking myself and distributing *prasādam*. But we do not discriminate that ‘He is needy.. ‘Everyone is needy, So actually, everyone is in need of spiritual understanding. So, by distributing food, the spiritual food, simply by eating, he will be in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even if he does not do anything. But actually, we are inviting persons to come, sit down, chant with us the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and take *prasādam* and go home. That’s all. This is our program.”

I remember the story told in the *Līlāmṛta* of how the first devotees were fascinated to see your two pink lotus feet passing in front of them as they sat in lines at the storefront of 26 2ndavenue. They were all utterly delighted to see you encouraging them so much to take more and more delicious Kṛṣṇa *prasāda*. One boy, Stṛyadhīśa, would amaze you and everyone else by consuming a staggering 22 chapattis at one sitting! You would always laugh and encourage him to try to eat more the next time. You were the perfect host, Śrīla Prabhupāda. There was no false ego on your part; thinking yourself to be a superior person. No, you were happy to serve and train all those young boys and girls who drifted into your storefront “temple,” in the sacred tradition of honoring Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. Not only did you cook and feed your young followers, you also washed their dirty jeans, cleaned up after them, tolerated their many neophyte mistakes and joked with them like an equal. What a softhearted, kind, considerate and candid person you are.

”...Actually, these boys and girls, they were not advised in the beginning to become my initiated disciples. I simply invited, “You please come.” I was chanting in the Tompkins Park in New York, and many of them were coming. So I invited them, “Please come with me. Take some *prasādam*.” So they used to take that. In this way, gradually, they developed Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and after some time they proposed, “Swamiji, make me your disciple,” (Initiation Lecture at Christian Monastery, April 16, 1972)

Indeed you were perfect in every way, Śrīla Prabhupāda – “crest jewel” among the *paramahaṁsas*. However, paramount among all your Vaiṣṇava ornaments was your overwhelming “Śrī Prahlād-like” compassion for the fallen conditioned souls.

There seemed to be no limit to your love for others, nor could this love be fully fathomed. On many occasions you would shed a tear or look very sorrowful at the suffering and distress of others. On one such occasion (during the 1974 Māyāpur festival), you were so moved by feelings of compassion that you asked your senior devotees to instigate a new program – mass *prasādam* distribution. This new program would mark the beginning of a new phase in ISKCON’s social reform plan and the end of the most sinful crime of the millennium – cow killing.

”The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement vigorously approves this practice of preparing food, offering it to the Deity, and distributing it to the general population. This activity should be extended universally to stop sinful eating habits as well as other behavior befitting only demons. A demoniac civilization will never bring piece within the world. Since eating is the first necessity in human society, those engaged in solving the problems of preparing and distributing food should take lessons from Mādhavendra Purī and execute the Annakuṭa ceremony. When the people take to eating only *prasāda* offered to the Deity, all the demons will be turned into Vaiṣṇavas. When the people are Kṛṣṇa conscious, naturally the government will be also. A Kṛṣṇa conscious man is always a very liberal well wisher of everyone. When such men head the government, the people will certainly be sinless. They will no longer be disturbing demons. It is then and then only that a peaceful condition can prevail in society. (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya-līlā* 4.93)

How ISKCON’s world wide prasādam distribution began  
Excerpt from a lecture by H.H. Jayapatāka Swami (place and date unknown)

”It was the second or third International Māyāpur Festival. We had just finished the four story building that is today known as the Lotus Building. Śrīla Prabhupāda had invited several devotees from all over the world for the inauguration of this mandir. On this occasion we had a big feast. After the feast Prabhupāda took prasādam and then a little rest. But his rest was disturbed by the howling of dogs, and he got up. Prabhupāda said, ‘Bring all the sannyāsīs, bring the GBC immediately! Bring them!’

”Usually if Prabhupāda wanted to say something he would say, ‘Bring my secretary.’ But in Māyāpur he would call everyone. He would want everyone to hear what he was saying. At that time the devotees were just ‘recovering’ from a big feast. When Prabhupāda called, all the devotees came down rushing. ‘What is Prabhupāda calling for? What’s happening?’ They saw Prabhupāda intently looking out of the windows with tears in his eyes – he was crying! They looked out and saw a big pile of leaf plates thrown at the back of the temple after the feast. There were about fifteen dogs, and about fifteen to twenty boys and girls, little kids between eight and fourteen years, picking through the leaves and fighting of the dogs. They were eating the leftovers and licking the leaves. They were ordinary village children who were basically clean but poor.

”Prabhupāda was trying to communicate. Then Prabhupāda revealed his mind, ‘How hungry they must be! They are picking the *prasādam* from the pile – they must be so hungry! We must arrange for their *prasādam!* If you want to make this a *mandir*, a house of Kṛṣṇa, then you must see that within a 10 mile radius, no one goes hungry. That will be your success. Kṛṣṇa is everyone’s father. How can the son go hungry in the presence of the father? Immediately arrange to distribute *prasādam*.’

”When Prabhupāda wanted something done immediately, it meant the next day was too late. But anyway, we could not get it together until the next day. We just had a big tent in those days, and since Prabhupāda wanted *prasādam* distribution, we cooked some *kichari,* and in this way we started to distribute *prasādam*.

”Prabhupāda said that if we could have a *prasādam* distribution center at every 10 square miles throughout India, then we could transform the whole India. People will become so grateful to ISKCON that they would all definitely accept Lord Caitanya’s movement – it will be the most important movement, and they would follow very strictly. Simply by *prasādam* distribution, all the effects of Kali-yuga could be stopped.”

In a *Bhagavad-gītā* lecture on 7 January 1967, you explained to your fledgling disciples your “easy process” of self-realization: “So what is the difficulty there? And you come here, chant and dance. We don’t say that you make such exercise or press your nose or this or that. We simply say that ‘Come here, dance with us, chant with us and take *prasādam*.’ Is it very difficult? (laughter) It is not difficult. The easiest process of transcendental realization.”

Praying to assist you in Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s kitchen and push on your Food for Life mission to deliver all the sinful people of Kali, your *prasādam bhaktas* and potato peelers from all over the world humbly take shelter at your glowing white kitchen boots.

*Bṛhat* pots *kī jaya!*  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*

*Global Food for Life Program*  
(Written by Prīyavrata dāsa)

## Gurukula Alumni

Dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. Eighteen years ago, shortly before you departed from this mortal world, you wrote the following in one of your letters:

”Please also take care of the children. They are our future hopes, and the adolescent age is the most dangerous age. It is the turning point of one’s life. In this age, if you take care of the children, surely they will come out first class Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees.”

You always showed the greatest concern for our welfare. Many times you repeated the proverb, “child is the father of man” and emphasized that the leaders of this ISKCON should give all assistance to this “most important” project, so that we may be taken care of nicely. When one mother approached you on a morning walk and told you that they had been beating her son at Dallas Gurukula, you said the teachers who were beating the children should be hanged. You repeated this three times: “They should be hanged. They should be hanged. They should be hanged.”

No doubt each of us in the pioneer *gurukulas* had some *karma* to work through. We are glad that you weren’t informed of all that went on behind your back (had you been, we don’t know if you would have been able to translate as many books and continue preaching so forcefully in the little time you had left to set the groundwork for a Kṛṣṇa conscious world revolution.) The books, letters, tapes and videotapes you left behind will help us determine today, and for all times to come, gold from pyrite, the sincere from the insincere and truth from untruth.

Looking back, we are amazed at the power of your message that ingrained itself firmly in our hearts despite the poor delivery. Looking back, we know most of us can get over having been the guinea-pigs of an inexperienced and neophyte society. Today, please give us the strength so that we *can,* this time for real, make *gurukula* our most important project and give our own children a Kṛṣṇa conscious education from early childhood (and provide for their futures *within* ISKCON, through devotee job-placement programs and *varnāśrama* communities.) Then there is great hope for the future of the world.

*Your grandchildren at the Gurukula Alumni Association*.

## IFAST

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who appeared on this planet just to extend your love and mercy unconditionally for the benefit of the most fallen souls.

He reasons ill who says that Vaiṣṇavas die when  
Thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living,  
Try to spread the holy name around.

On this holy day of your appearance, you disciples, grandsons and granddaughters from the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology humbly beg your kindness to submit a simple report of our activities over the last four years.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you know, Washington, D.C., the capital of the United States, is perhaps the most demoniac city; but with your love and causeless mercy, the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology was founded by your son, Bhakti Tīrtha Swami, to provide a shelter for those personalities that are stagnated in the mode of goodness and entrapped in secular professions.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your love and causeless mercy, the Institute programs include daily *maṅgala-ārati, bhakta* and *bhaktin* training classes, Sunday Love Feast, and a range of ongoing stimulating classes simply to attract and demonstrate how the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness can be applied in all areas of life.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your love and causeless mercy, we have been given an opportunity to distribute your books. The transcendental message has pierced the hearts of politicians, lawyers, doctors, psychologists, college professors, Christian ministers, and many other materially qualified personalities and caused them to surrender their lives to the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your love and causeless mercy, we are distributing *prasādam* daily at “Govinda’s,” our new restaurant, in the most cosmopolitan area in the City of Washington, D.C. Each week the restaurant hosts a preaching program, and we have been most honored to have many of your dedicated sons and daughters come and speak the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to variegated audiences.

*The Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology – Washington, D.C.*

## ISKCON Foundation

**Part 1. Reflection of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial**

As 1996 draws nearer,  
100 autumns since your birth,  
a tiny mind, all in a fervor,  
prays your name will flood the earth.

As 1996 draws closer,  
one grows anxious, day by day,  
must he endure the soul’s foreclosure,  
eternal debt to you unpaid?

As 1996 draws closer,  
the kirtan reaches greater heights,  
your glories and the Name’s ambrosia,  
a heartening call in dead of night!

As 1996 draws nearer,  
good fortune spreads, for all to see,  
Prabhupāda, our ever well wisher,  
wrote the books that set us free.

As 1996 draws nearer,  
one thought appears above this test,  
when the celebration’s over,  
can we believe it’s time to rest?

For ’96 will come and go,  
as planets spin across the heaven,  
Prabhupāda-bhakti one can show,  
by doing more in ’97.

by Ambarīṣa dāsa  
Chairman, ISKCON Foundation

**Part 2**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Thank you for blessing us with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This year in Māyāpura more than sixty of your senior disciples and grand disciples met with members of GBC to chalk out plans for celebrating your centennial in 1996. The devotees divided themselves into groups to consider how to fulfill each of the 12 petals of the lotus of your centennial celebration. Some of their visions are summarized in the following fictitious news and literary clippings. It is our solemn prayer that you bless us with all it will take to make these visions become realities.

Centennial Blitz Puts Hare Kṛṣṇa Founder On World Stage (*Time Magazine*, August 15, 1996)

Most of us recall the “God men” of the 70’s and 80’s – Rajnesh, Rev. Moon, Mahārshi, et al. – and most of us know the Hare Kṛṣṇas. Now, for five beads and a *Bhagavad-gītā*, who can name the founder of the Hare Kṛṣṇas? Give up? It’s His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, known affectionately by his followers as Śrīla Prabhupāda. Although he passed away nearly two decades back, hi teachings seem more popular than ever. This year over 25 million of his books in 50 languages will be distributed from some 500 Kṛṣṇa temples worldwide. Now it’s the 100thanniversary of his birth, and the Hare Kṛṣṇas want to make sure you know it...

What’s Prasādam? Karma-free Food – Pure Vegetarian, Of Course! (*Vegetarian Times,* Sept., 1996)

Chemical-free, additive-free. OK, I know about this stuff. What kind of food is “Karma-free”? It’s *prasādam* – pure vegetarian fare made from veggies, fruits, milk and grains, and offered with love to Lord Kṛṣṇa. It’s that cosmic food passed out by the Hare Kṛṣṇas ever since the Woodstock days. Only now it’s gone global! This November 1, in honor of Śrīla Prabhupāda (their founder) and his centennial, Kṛṣṇas are mobilizing in thousands of cities the world over, determined to replace dead flesh with breaded cauliflower chunks on tens of millions of plates! At least on “World Feast Day,” a worldwide Kṛṣṇa food distribution frenzy slated for this fall, millions will enjoy some good old-fashioned *karma*-free *prasādam*.

ISKCON Shifts Major Responsibilities from Clergy to Congregation (*The Non-Profit Times,* Dec. 1996)

Over the past six years, ISKCON (the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness) has transformed itself from a cloistered, youth-based “alternative religious movement” (read “cult”) to a broad-based, congregation-driven, increasingly mainstream religion. They’ve gone from depending on street collections to the collection box, from “finger-in-the-dike” clergy-based operations to professional and effective congregational-based non-profit organizations. Now the clergy preach freely, and congregational members feel happier and more responsible. “It’s a textbook case,” comments non-profit development expert U.N. Owen, “because they’ve done it by studying and applying our own mainstream methods.”

ISKCON Hails First Class of Leadership Graduates (*ISKCON Foundation Bulletin*, Fall 1996)

Earlier this month sixteen new ISKCON Temple Presidents graduated from ISKCON’s Varṇāśrama College and left for their respective new posts in ISKCON temples throughout the world. In addition to exhaustive philosophical training and basics in Accounting, Media Relations, Corporate Law and Administration and Planning, the graduates completed numerous special courses, including Public Speaking, Human Relations, Time Management and Volunteer Leadership.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Disciples All Accounted For! (*From Back to Godhead*, September 1996)

Extensive promotion of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial has aided researches in tracking down over 90% of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s 4,000-plus initiated disciples. “The response has been overwhelmingly positive,” said one of the researchers. “Many hadn’t heard from ISKCON in years and had no idea about ISKCON’s many new congregational programs for them and their children.” In addition, over 10,000 of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s grand disciples have been contacted. All the devotees’ names are entered in an ISKCON Vaiṣṇava data bank created to help them keep Śrīla Prabhupāda’s family united and in touch.

River of Śrīla Prabhupāda Nectar Flows From Vaiṣṇava Hearts (From *Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā,* 1996)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the occasion of your centennial has rekindled your heartfelt presence in people throughout the world. From the lips of your disciples, grand disciples, friends, followers, and well-wishers everywhere flows a fresh stream of appreciation for your profound teachings and glorious life. This extensive flood of Śrīla Prabhupāda consciousness has overcome the banks of the river of ISKCON, promising to submerge the entire world.

Sādhana Standards Increasing (From *ISKCON World Review*)

Increased remembrance of Śrīla Prabhupāda and a sense of his presence have inspired staff and congregational members of ISKCON worldwide to renew their commitments to *sādhana-bhakti*. Attendance at morning programs has grown throughout ISKCON. Devotees suffering from revived *anarthas* are now shedding them, recommitting themselves to their vows. So a sense of renewed hope and direction pervades ISKCON, as devotees firmly fix their sights on the spiritual world.

1996 Centennial Awards Ceremony (*Transcript from Māyāpura Festival*, main stage, March 23, 1997)

...Largest new temples completed: Joint award to New Delhi and Bangalore! The 100thPadayātrā Country award goes to: Cuba Yātrā! And now for individual awards – and I’m sorry, there are too many names to read individually. For those completing all of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books we have 4898 awards. For those learning 100 we have 1594 awards. And finally, last year, 746 devotees found and collected names of 100 new people who are interested in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have awards for them, too.

1996 Literature Distribution More Than Doubles Any Previous Year! (*Saṅkīrtana Newsletter*)

...The huge across-the-board increase has been attributed to a vastly increased number of distributors. Unlike in previous years, most of these distributors live at home! These congregational members have dived deep into the ocean of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* nectar. Many are making up for lost time by competing, book for book, with those internationally renowned temple-based distributors who have been relishing this intoxicating nectar for years!

Prabhupāda Memorials Now Grace 108 ISKCON Centers (*Prabhupāda Toṣaṇī*)

Borrowing from the traditional Vaiṣṇava practice of enshrining sacred footprints at pilgrimage places, devotees have now installed Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footprints, often with photo displays and memorabilia, at every ISKCON center opened as part of His Divine Grace’s personal pastimes (1966-77). For venturesome travelers, a worldwide Śrīla Prabhupāda pilgrimage package tour is now available. In addition, ISKCON has now established Śrīla Prabhupāda Memorials at the following sites associated with his life: His birth site in Calcutta; Rādhā-Damodar Mandira in Vṛndāvana; Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Temple, Chippiwada, Delhi, where he published *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*; Keshavaji Gaudiya Math, Mathura, where he took sannyāsa; the Boston pier where he first arrived in the United States; the Butler, Pennsylvania, YMCA; and 26 Second Avenue, New York, ISKCON’s birthplace.

Local Religious Communities Unite for “World Holy Name Day” (*Los Angeles Times,* Monday, October 17, 1996)

Representatives of sixty-five Metro area churches joined with 25,000 parishioners yesterday to celebrate “World Holy Name Day” at the Coliseum. Sponsored by ISKCON, the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness (the Hare Kṛṣṇa), the celebration featured continuous repetition of God’s names as taught in each of the six religious traditions represented. Thousands of similar celebrations took place throughout the world today in honor of the 100thanniversary of ISKCON’s founder, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Calcutta Buzzing For Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda Centennial Festivities (*Times of India*, August 1, 1996)

This week Calcutta residents are being treated to a 100thBirthday party of their favorite sons – His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami (Śrīla) Prabhupādajī, founder*-ācārya* of the worldwide Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Netaji Stadium has been sold out for months in anticipation of the August fete. On that day, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *mūrtī* will be bathed with water taken from 100 holy places throughout India. After that, in keeping with the time-honored Vaiṣṇava tradition, his followers will feed 10,000,000 of Calcutta residents with Śrī Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. Later this fall, representative disciples from 100 countries will join the Prime Minister of India at the opening ceremonies for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s new birth site memorial. They will then march together in a spectacular parade reminiscent in scope and size to the famous ISKCON Calcutta Rathayātrā, the city’s largest religious festival of the year.

(by Kālakaṇṭha dāsa)

## ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance we thank you profusely for what you have given us. You are a representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself who has come to save all of us. You are extremely compassionate, or else how could you come on a ship across the sea at such an advanced age to the degraded Western world?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this day and age the training that children throughout the world are receiving is training befitting animals. The children are sent off to schools where nonsense is constantly stuck in their minds and where teachers are preaching about the nonexistence of God. Because children are taught by their parents and teachers that the goal of life is to enjoy, they become wild trying to satisfy their senses. When frustration occurs, they resort to bizarre activities – even suicide. In this way the future of the world is leaning toward a godless civilization.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can’t even imagine where we would be if you hadn’t established the gurukula system. By your mercy only have we been able to be trained up in Kṛṣṇa’s service. You have given us a chance to relish Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So please help us grab this opportunity and act fully according to your wishes.

Although we are extremely fallen we wish to please you in many ways. We want to show the modernized world that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a superior way of life. We want to establish a self-sufficient community in which devotion to Kṛṣṇa is the solution to all problems. We want to develop a group of artists who can sing, play different instruments, and depict and enact the Lord’s pastimes. We wish to become expert in distributing books and preaching to the many conditioned souls who are seeking ultimate happiness. All of this can be done by your mercy only. We too must become fixed in our own *sādhana* so that we can continue your movement. You have said, “You are very fortunate to have the opportunity of attending Gurukula, so don’t waste this opportunity. Take full advantage of it by always raising early, attending arati, classes, and chanting at least sixteen rounds daily without fail. These things will make you very strong in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (Śrīla Prabhupāda to Candramukhī devī dāsī, 2ndFebruary, 1975) This instruction you have given is meant for all of us children. Please give us the strength to follow it now and in our future life please allow us to fulfill your desires. Again and again Śrīla Prabhupāda we thank you for saving us from māyā’s clutches. We pray to remain...

*Your eternal servants,*  
(written by Gopī devī dāsī, a 15-year old Gurukula student)

## ISKCON World Review

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances. All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

This service you have kindly give us at ISKCON World Review allows us to see how devotees all around the world are joining with each other to serve you and the society you founded.

Each time we publish ISKCON World Review, we witness the far reaching influence of ISCKON. More and more heads of state receive your books. More people attend Ratha-yātrā festivals, learn about cow protection, and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. We can see how the devotees are turning to you more and more for guidance and inspiration. Our sincere prayer is that you will please allow us to assist you in uniting your followers and to help them share devotional service in loving cooperation. That is the purpose of ISKCON World Review.

That is also the purpose of the Hare Kṛṣṇa Directory and Resource Guide. One of the very first realizations you gave us was that the devotees need some means of locating and staying in contact with each other. Each year that went by, the need became clearer. Then three years ago, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you inspired Dhaneśvara Prabhu to strengthen his own connection to your society by helping others be more connected. He took this Directory project to his heart and the results are being appreciated by devotees all over the world today. What took shape was a display of the amazing variety of devotees that your society encompasses – *gurus, sannyāsīs*, householders, *brahmacaris, brahmacarinis*, congregational members, entrepreneurs, professionals, artists, teachers, accountants, farmers, and friends. This resource book of Kṛṣṇa conscious society is helping devotees come together and nurtures spiritual community development.

For the second time we are compiling a Directory of your spiritual society. This 1994 Hare Kṛṣṇa Directory and Resource Guide is a further testament to your divine power. There are now more Kṛṣṇa conscious centers, projects, and people to connect for your pleasure.

Please direct us all to understand how to further all of your plans for establishing Varṇāśrama society. This social basis for teaching and entrenching Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere is to your glory. Please help us understand the urgency of this mission. Your faithful preachers work so hard to distribute your books and make devotees. Now we must also work as hard to make a society in which devotees will remain and their children will carry ISKCON further through the next century.

Please help us, Guru Mahārāja. Without your mercy, we can do nothing at all. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Serving your society is giving us the greatest happiness.

*Sarva-satya dāsa, ISKCON World Review.*

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**From Seed To Sustenance**

The oxen pull the disk to cultivate the soil.  
The oxen pull the 3 row grain drill to plant the seeds.  
The oxen turn the gears that draw the water from the river to irrigate the fields.

The oxen pull the sickle bar mower to harvest the golden grain.  
The oxen turn the mill stones that grind the grain into flour.  
The baker’s ovens, fueled by methane gas from fermenting cow manure, produce breads, cookies and cakes.

The devotees harvest the cotton planted by the oxen.  
The devotees weave the cotton into cloth.  
The devotees sew the cloth into dhotīs and sārīs.

The oxen harvest the castor beans.  
The oxen turn the mill stones that grind the castor beans into oil.  
The devotees use the castor oil to light their lamps.

The cows chew the grass planted by the oxen.  
The cows turn the green grass into creamy white milk.  
The milker receives the precious gift.  
The cook turns the milk into ghee, yogurt and milk sweets.

The vegetables, castor beans, cotton and grains are gifts from Mother Earth and Father Bull.  
The milk is a gift from Mother Earth and Mother Cow.  
The gifts are offered to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your dearmost friend, Lord Kṛṣṇa, with all the love and devotion we possess.

”Our farm projects are an extremely important part of our movement. We must become self-sufficient by growing our own grains and producing our own milk, then there will be no question of poverty.” (SPL to Rūpanūga, December, 1974)

”Now our next program will be to organize farming land to set an example to the whole world how people can be peaceful, happy and free from all anxieties simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra and living an honorable life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (SPL to Karttikeya, October 19, 1975)

*Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection*.

## KrishnaFest

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

We cannot say that we are capable preachers of that we even know how to preach, but we do know that bringing conditioned souls to the shelter of your movement and engaging them in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness and distributing your books is, according to you, “real preaching.” We are attempting to do this and are having some success by your grace. We realize that you have given us all the formulas for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and if we employ those methods our success is guaranteed.

In the past year we have been more conscientious about cultivating relationships with people we meet and providing better facilities for them to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We started a newsletter for the youth, set up a correspondence office and personally visited the more interested people, began regular *nāma-haṭṭa* programs and established new *bhakta* and *bhaktin* training programs. In addition, we’ve continued to maintain our regular college preaching and festival program. The results have been encouraging. Many young people are seriously practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness at home, while others are regularly joining our *bhakta/bhaktin* program.

Whenever we get a little success, *māyā* immediately tries to interfere with our progress and create problems for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. *Māyā* also tried to stop your forceful preaching work, but was unable to succeed. We, on the other hand, are easy prey for her attacks. We need your blessings so that we never deviate from your instructions and never give up the fight to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, no matter how difficult, trying or disturbing it may be.

Two years ago in Vṛndāvana, KrishnaFest established its organizational policies and preaching strategies. Due to our lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness we have not been able to properly follow all of them. We offer this “Mission Statement” at your lotus feet and beg that you empower us do adhere to it.

**Krishnafest Mission Statement**

1\. To develop and maintain relationships with all interested people we contact and to guide them in their progressive surrender to Kṛṣṇa.  
2\. To always deal with people in ways that will be best for their spiritual advancement.  
3\. To focus on book distribution in all of our preaching.  
4\. To create an atmosphere that will attract new devotees to join, especially those who are intelligent and responsible.  
5\. To maintain professionalism in all fields of endeavor.  
6\. To train devotees to behave properly, to effectively communicate, and to act with integrity before permitting them to represent Śrīla Prabhupāda’s movement in public.  
7\. To make our presentations to the public increasingly attractive and tasteful.  
8\. To strive for exemplary cleanliness and orderliness in our living and working environments.  
9\. To have devotees’ spiritual well-being come before any other considerations.  
10\. To provide a conductive atmosphere for cultivating the practices and principles of the *brahmacari, vanaprastha*, and *sannyasa* ashrams.  
11\. To train all of our men in the highest standards of *brahmacārī* life and Vaiṣṇava etiquette.  
12\. To relate to one another on the highest platform of love and trust.  
13\. To instill within our members the importance of teamwork and respect for authority and seniority.  
14\. To train our men to become well-rounded and to be expert in as many ways as possible.  
15\. To make certain that the devotees will always receive sufficient counseling.  
16\. To always fill the ether with transcendental sound, doing away with prajalpa and frivolity.  
17\. To continuously monitor all our activities in light of the principles and guidelines for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness established by Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
18\. To treat others as we would ourselves.  
19\. To offer exemplary Vaiṣṇava hospitality to all devotees or guests who come into our company.  
20\. To make ourselves available for teaching what we have learned to preachers and managers within our movement.  
21\. To demonstrate that preaching in America can be as successful now as it was in the past.  
22\. To inspire other devotees with a workable example of what we are teaching in our preaching course.

Your fallen servants at KrishnaFest

## Padayātrā America

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Our small *padayātrā* up until now has only nine countries to offer you and all of South America waiting for our arrival. Even with all of this great work ahead, I have a great yearning to ask your blessing in a special project.

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu said that in every town and village this Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* would be heard. This we have full faith in. With your unflinching faith in the words of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Swami Prabhupāda, you came to the West to help us. Maybe it is completely impossible for us to go to other planets at this moment, but there must exist a way to go to other *varṣas* of this planet. In a recent trip to South India, Śrīman Sadāputa Prabhu found an old map drawn with the descriptions of the Fifth Canto, and it appeared just as he thought. I understand that to this date we have just scraped the surface of this planet as we know it. Still, other parts are awaiting the arrival of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Please help us spread this movement to all parts of this planet.

*Your servants in Padayātrā America*.

## Padayātrā Europe

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We would like to offer our most respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. You have come to this mortal world to save so many conditioned souls, trapped in three modes of some goodness, a lot of passion and a tremendous amount of ignorance. You showed us how to chant, dance and be happy, how to eat only *prasādam* and get purified, how to hear and read the transcendental glories and pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa and His associates and how to work hard day and night for Kṛṣṇa.

We have read about your glories and your teachings in your books, and we have been inspired by your disciples to join your ISKCON movement.

We are now transcendentally engaged in walking from village to village, bringing to reality the desires of Lord Caitanya. We are going on *hari-nāma* every day, chanting the holy names, even when there is nobody on the road, as we understand that even the animals and the trees will benefit. We are trying to distribute as many books and as much *prasādam* as possible all along the way, to give a chance to these souls to get your causeless mercy. We are preaching cow protection, and our best preachers are the two beautiful oxen that pull the chariot of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasūndara. Everyone says that these Deities are the most beautiful and effulgent. They are our inspiration and guidance.

Going from country to country, we have seen how everywhere, be it in Ireland, England, Holland, Belgium, France, Spain, Italy, Greece or Macedonia, people are just drawn to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, sitting on our cart, so real and pure, so attractive. They are intrigued by you, and they want to know who you are, and they want to read your literature.

We are trying to be your instruments in propagating this auspicious movement, and it’s all so much fun and so ecstatic, we don’t want to stop.

*Your grand disciples on Padayātrā Europe from Belfast to Moscow (1990-1996).*  
(written by Reṇukā devī dāsī)

## Padayātrā India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. Although the lotus feet of many great sages are folded in an *āsana* while they meditate with devotion on Paramātmā in the heart, this can never be said for you. Your lotus feet are always quickly moving, barely touching the ground. They carry you to all corners of the globe, inspiring and guiding the lost souls of Kali-yuga, especially through your books, *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* etc., as distributed by your dedicated followers.

To connect with ISKCON, one must connect with you, and to stay connected, one must continue that association on a daily basis. “I’ll live forever in my books, and my followers will live with me.” ...”chant without fail sixteen rounds of Hare Kṛṣṇa daily, read *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* at least one hour daily, and in this way, without any doubt, you will very soon become very happy, and your life will be sublime!”

In reply to a letter from a Bombay student suggesting that you go on *padayātrā* throughout India, you encouraged him: “If you are very eager, please get hold of at least a half dozen men like you, then with another half dozen foreign disciples, I can immediately take up this program and tour village to village and town to town. It will be very, very effective, I know that.” (letter to Punjabi Premananda, 13 April 1976) That student didn’t take it up, but your devoted disciple, Lokanātha Swami did, not only here, but also throughout the world.

Those of us on Padayātrā India want to thank you for giving real meaning and happiness to our lives, reconnecting us to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You have given us such a wonderful opportunity to serve you in a loving exchange, by bringing both you and Nitai-Gaurasundara in your Deity forms to every town and village, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare; Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare and distributing your books and *prasādam*. In this way, these misguided people who are often very pious can be blessed as we are by taking shelter of your lotus feet. Actually it is you who are guiding us on this transcendental pilgrimage, and we pray that you may continue to do so, step by step, eternally.

*Your eternal Padayātrīs, Padayātrā India.*  
(Akhilādhāra dāsa)

## Radio Krishna Central

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, again and again.

Your lotus feet are always dancing on the transcendental land of Vṛndāvana Dhāma, and their glories are spread all over the three worlds. They are perfectly capable, and in themselves sufficient, to give complete shelter to all spirit souls. Such is their power of purification that, after having been in contact with them, willingly, unwillingly or unknowingly, even for a fraction of a moment, one is forcefully dragged to reach perfection. No matter how deeply one is sunk into the material atmosphere, by remembering your wonderful personality, your teachings, your instructions and acting accordingly, a living entity can resume its topmost fortunate position as a loving servant of the Supreme Lord and His pure devotees.

You have been teaching us that, inconceivably deciding not to follow our desire to submit ourselves to the Supreme Personality of Godhead and thus misusing our capacity of exercise an infinitesimal particle of independence, we have been given into the care of Durgā devī. By putting us under the spell of her deluding power, she shows us what happens if we go against our nature as energy (servants) and behave as if we were the energetic (master) by trying to enjoy this material manifestation throughout our senses. By submerging us under the cloud of ignorance, she makes us forget who the Supreme Lord really is, and by forgetting this we also fail to remember which particular, eternal, loving relationship ties us to the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His devotees. In this way, losing the ability to identify ourselves with our original eternal personality, we forget our particular identity. Just like the sunlight’s photons appear as if having a separated existence from the sun globe when filtered through a rain cloud, under the cloud of ignorance we imagine ourselves as having a separated existence from the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and we think of ourselves as self-existing and independent like Him. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, due to this forgetfulness, we have been loitering in this dark and dangerous material universe, completely lost in the search of a permanent, eternal platform from which act as an imitation of the supreme controller. As a result we have become very surrendered servants of the most obnoxious, perverted desires, worse than street dogs, experiencing a continuous never-ending, stream of suffering.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, putting us back into our original condition, you have been showering, over and over, your unlimited mercy on us, even if we are not at all deserving such a matchless gift. Please, by your divine association, give us a fraction of the pure love that you have for Śrī Kṛṣṇa so that the same love, dormant in our hearts, can be reawakened; as much as the fire, dormant inside wood, can be reawakened by the contact with a particle of fire. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, personally I’m only an imitation of a devotee, and not even a very good imitation, still you are the owner of my life and I belong to you. I am completely confident that if I keep trying again and again to follow your instructions, the moment will come when my heart will be cleansed from every dirty desire and will be a suitable place for Your Divine Grace to sit upon. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you keep allowing me to stay in your association, even if I am a fool and rascal, I’m sure that I’ll be able to reach the point where my only reason to act will be to please Your Divine Grace.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I take this opportunity to again renew my prayer: please never let me forget your wonderful personality, pastimes and instructions so that wherever I may be, I’ll always be with Your Divine Grace. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants at Radio Kṛṣṇa Central, Terne, Italy.*  
(written by Tridaṇḍī dāsa)

## Vaishnava Scholarly Press

Mostly I am reluctant to reveal dreams I have at night, especially those that are about Śrīla Prabhupāda. Recently, I had one that I feel compelled to share, because it was particularly explicit and instructive. I also sense that Śrīla Prabhupāda has selected me to convey its important message, as follows:

Śrīla Prabhupāda was seated majestically in a large, ornate room somewhere in India. Many dignitaries were coming to visit him, including the ambassadors from all the countries of the world. My service was receiving everyone in the outer room, bringing them into the inner room, and seating them before Śrīla Prabhupāda.

While I was going back and forth between the two rooms, I could hear Śrīla Prabhupāda speaking very clearly. What he was saying was like a proclamation, which he wanted all to hear and understand, but which was directed toward myself especially as follows:

”The mercy of the spiritual master is everything. One can chant Hare Kṛṣṇa only by the mercy of the spiritual master. What is the value of chanting if the mercy of the spiritual master is lacking? One cannot live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness without the mercy of the spiritual master.”

After these utterances, the dream abruptly ended. When I awoke, I was struck by the vividness of the dream and the immediacy of its message. A disciple should always be aware of what Śrīla Prabhupāda was saying in the dream, and yet because this message had to be conveyed it meant that I had not sufficiently understood this important teaching. It appeared that I was taking his fundamental instruction for granted and that I needed to ruminate upon it further.

At the beginning of every day we sing Viśvanātha Cakravarti Ṭhākura’s declaration that the mercy of the spiritual master is like a cloud of rain that showers upon all. But, the next question is how can one obtain the spiritual master’s mercy. Continuing the great *ācārya’s* simile, one only has to come outdoors to be able to bathe in the spiritual master’s rain mercy. If one stubbornly remains indoors, the rain of mercy will only be indirectly perceived but not truly experienced. In other words, one must only desire the mercy of the spiritual master to be able to receive it. If one does not crave it, he or she will not be able to obtain it.

Another great *ācārya* has written that the disciple is a creation of the spiritual master’s mercy; I am my spiritual master’s mercy; I do not exist separate from my spiritual master’s mercy; and if he were to withdraw his mercy, due to my careless offenses, I would cease to exist.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has written in *Gurudeva! Kṛpā-bindu-diyā* that the disciple begs to have even one drop of the rain of the spiritual master’s mercy, as follows: “Give me all help. Give me strength... When will such mercy fall to one who is as weak and devoid of intelligence as I? Allow me to be with you. If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful to me, I can only weep and will not be able to maintain my life.”

Thus, I can have access to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy in proportion to how much I love him. Reciprocation is the secret key that opens the door of intimacy between the guru and the disciple. In the opening of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* the sages express their eagerness to hear from Sūta Gosvāmī because he has been especially blessed by his spiritual masters, due to his obedience and submissiveness (SB 1.1.8). Śrīla Prabhupāda writes that this is the secret of success in spiritual life. Sūta had achieved the highest level of love of God: “Sūta was endowed with all favors by his learned and self-realized spiritual masters such as Śrīla Vyāsadeva and others.” However, in his purports to the Caitanya-caritāmṛta (*Madhya* 17.15) Śrīla Prabhupāda fine tunes this verse from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* by introducing the great Śrīdhara Svāmī’s comments that the Sanskrit word *snigdhasya* in the *Bhāgavatam* verse, which is usually translated as submissive, really means to love. “The disciple who has actual love for the spiritual master is endowed, by the blessings of the spiritual master, with all confidential knowledge.”

With this confidential knowledge, stressed also in the *Bhagavad-gītā*, we will know everything – what to do and not to do. We will be able to purely perpetuate this movement according to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire. Nothing more nor anybody else is required; we do not even have to endeavor to achieve the association of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, separately. “If a disciple is very serious to execute the orders of his spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* and *vapuḥ*. This is the secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush in Vṛndāvana, while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to this principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty.” (*Bhāg*. 4.28.51) This same point is stressed by Śrīla Prabhupāda in the following statement: “The blessings of the spiritual master are more powerful than one’s personal endeavor for such advancement.” (*SB* 8.15.28)

When I first met Śrīla Prabhupāda and surrendered to him, the first thing I did was to give him all the money I had. I emptied out my bank account and cashed in my stocks and bonds. It was quite a sum in those days; and years later in reminiscing about those days, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that when I joined him, all his financial problems were solved.

However, when handing over the money to Śrīla Prabhupāda, I felt personal pride that it was I who was helping Śrīla Prabhupāda in his insolvent state. Śrīla Prabhupāda must have sensed this. When I asked Śrīla Prabhupāda for a set of the three *Bhāgavatam* volumes he had brought with him from India, presuming that he should give them to me as a reward for my generous donation, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that I could have the books but I would have to pay for them. “These are extra,” he said smiling, as he handed them to me. Even though I had just given him thousands of dollars, by having to go and arrange for an additional sixteen dollars and eighty cents, my arrogance was cut to pieces. Later I would read in one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports that whatever the disciple has – his life, his wealth, his intelligence, or his words – should be offered to the spiritual master. “Everything should be offered to the spiritual master as a matter of duty, but the offering should be made to the spiritual master with heart and soul, not artificially to gain material prestige... Whatever is offered to the spiritual master should be offered with love and devotion, not for material adoration.” (*Bhāg*. 7.7.30)

Śrīla Prabhupāda became like a father to me and my brother Gargamuni, replacing our material father who had utterly rejected us. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave me food which was wonderful, shelter without having to worry about paying for the rent, and something to do: a job as the first temple president so that I would not be bored. He also gave me a mission and goal in life, thus saving me from aimlessness and hopelessness. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda was more than a father; he was also my *guru*. A father can sometimes be disobeyed, and yet forgive his son; but a *guru* has to be obeyed and expects obedience and discipline from his disciple.

Later Śrīla Prabhupāda kept me by his side as his secretary, allowing me to serve him intimately, thus satisfying me completely. But, I was attached to the prestige of the nearness to this greatly worshipable saint. Śrīla Prabhupāda again must have sensed it, because he would regularly send me back to my zone, far away from all association and even communication, in Africa. After a while Śrīla Prabhupāda would call me back to serve him as his secretary, but soon he would find another reason to send me away again. This angered and disappointed my false ego, and I committed offenses and fell down. Śrīla Prabhupāda has written about such disciples, “Also there are so-called disciples who become submissive to a spiritual master most artificially, with an ulterior motive. They also cannot understand what Kṛṣṇa consciousness or devotional service is.” (*Bhāg*. 3.32.40) The spiritual master tests the disciple, constantly, just as in school the teacher gives a daily quiz to see how the students are learning their lessons. The more difficult the test by the spiritual master, the more opportunity the disciple has to serve and please the spiritual master, and thus advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In many places in his books, Śrīla Prabhupāda has written of the importance of the disciple’s following the orders of the spiritual master.

”This is the secret of success. After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravarti Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana, Bg*. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life and substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master. Thus a disciple should always meditate on the order of the spiritual master, and that is perfectional meditation. Not only should he meditate upon that order, but he should find out the means by which he can perfectly worship and execute it.” (*SB* 4.24.15)

”This combination, the instruction of the spiritual master and the faithful execution of the instruction by the disciple, makes the entire process perfect. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura describes in his explanation of the verse in *Bhagavad-gītā, vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ*, that one who wants to be certain to achieve spiritual success must take the instruction from the spiritual master as to what his particular function is. He should faithfully try to execute that particular instruction and should consider that his life and soul. The faithful execution of the instruction which he receives from the spiritual master is the only duty of a disciple, and that will bring him perfection. One should be very careful to receive the message from the spiritual master through the ears and execute it faithfully. That will make one’s life successful.” (*Bhāg.* 3.22.7)

On this wonderful day in celebration of the appearance of Śrīla Prabhupāda, his disciples and followers are faced with a perpetually perplexing problem: how to try to repay or compensate for what Śrīla Prabhupāda has given to each of us. But, even this frustration for each disciple has been articulated by Śrīla Prabhupāda, thus relieving us on some way of this futility but also increasing it by once again demonstrating that he has given us everything.

”Knowledge of Kṛṣṇa is such a great gift that it is impossible to repay the benefactor... The word *vinoda-pātram* can be divided into two words, *vinā* and *uda-pātram*, or can be understood as one word, *vinoda-pātram*, which means ‘joker.’ A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt.” (*Bhāg*. 4.22.47)

Our predicament is that we are the servants of one who is perfect in every way. How do we follow such a personality and serve his instructions? In a sense, we are doomed to failure, because his perfection makes our inadequacies so apparent. How do we surrender to a personality who himself is a totally surrendered soul? Only Śrīla Prabhupāda could write the following passage in his books, which upon consideration becomes appalling:

”The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading now all over the world, and sometimes I think that even though I am crippled in many ways, if one of my disciples becomes as strong as Dhrūva Mahārāja, then he will be able to carry me with him to Vaikuṇṭhaloka.” (*Bhāg*. 4.12.32)

I am unfortunate due to many faults, but I am convinced of one realization: only Śrīla Prabhupāda can save me from *māyā*; nothing or nobody else can. I, who am the creature of *māyā*, am assured that only Śrīla Prabhupāda can save me from the great calamity from which no one escapes, of being reborn in a lower species. I therefore submit myself at his lotus feet.

Vaiṣṇava Scholarly Press, Brahmānanda dāsa

## Vedic Student Association

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. These are the same feet that performed the immense austerity of traveling worldwide to broadcast the glories of the Supreme Absolute Truth, Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

In the course of this transcendental pastime of spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement, you extended your sublime mercy to me. I was very miserably wasting my life in pursuit of polished animal life. The *śāstras* describe the soul who gets the mercy of a pure devotee as *bhāgyavān,* fortunate.

In repayment for this great act of mercy, you simply asked that I work cooperatively with the other members of ISKCON to extend the same good fortune to others.

This request sounds simple, and in one sense it is, because I have your example and instructions. But there is one difficulty. I must be willing to act as your instrument. Your instrument must work as you desire and not otherwise. That requires that I give up all the various dreams of sense gratification that presently occupy my consciousness. These dreams are just like blood-sucking leeches; they leave me in a spiritually anemic condition, to weak to be of any help to your mission.

On this most auspicious occasion of the anniversary of your divine appearance, I pray that you purify me and make me a fit servant in your society. That will be the perfection of my meager existence.

Hoping in the future to properly serve you,  
*Vedic Student Association – Champaign-Urbana, Illinois*.  
(by Vāṇīnātha Vāsu dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. I’m sitting in Suburbia, USA at a relative’s house, having just left Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma. My health is weak, and I’m still in a state of shock: one day in the dhāma, the next in mleccha-deśa. I’m feeling great shelter in remembering you, and the basic siddhānta you have taught us, which over the years has become the basis of our existence.

This material world is simply a horrible place of suffering. I have no permanent connection with this body, but rather I’m a soul, Kṛṣṇa’s eternal servant. If I chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and serve you, your mission and your devotees, I can attain the goal of life – love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Vṛndāvana.

If I hadn’t come in touch with you, I’d probably be sitting right now in a similar house somewhere in America, thinking this material world a nice place, thinking this weak body to be myself and having no idea what Hare Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara and Vṛndāvana Dhāma were all about.

Thank you for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thank you from saving me from the hellish suburbs. Thank you for the opportunity to serve your devotees. Thank you for the opportunity to serve in Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma.

*Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education*.  
(by Vraja-vihārī dāsa)
